<meta>This page contains the legal "Privilege du Roy," the royal authorization required to publish books in 17th-century France. It establishes the copyright for Pierre Arnauld for three foundational alchemical texts.</meta>

### *Extract from the King’s Privilege.*

BY Letters Patent of the King given at Paris the 12th day of March, one thousand six hundred and twelve, signed by the King in his Council **Ceberet**, and sealed with the Great Seal in yellow wax: It is permitted and granted by <term>special privilege</term> <note>original: "privilege special." This was an early form of copyright that gave a publisher the exclusive right to print a specific work for a set number of years.</note> to **PIERRE ARNAULD**, Lord of La Chevallerie in Poitou, to have printed by whomever he sees fit, *three treatises not yet printed*, namely: *The Secret Book of the very ancient Philosopher <note>Artephius was a legendary figure in alchemy, often claimed to be over a thousand years old through the use of an "elixir of life."</note> Artephius, dealing with the occult art and metallic transmutation, in Latin and French: plus the Hieroglyphic Figures of Nicolas* **FLAMEL**, <note>Nicolas Flamel (c. 1330–1418) was a Parisian scrivener who, long after his death, became legendary as an alchemist who supposedly discovered the Philosopher's Stone.</note> *with the explanation of them by the said* **FLAMEL**: *Together with the true Book of the learned Synesius, a Greek Abbot*: <note>Synesius of Cyrene was a 4th-century philosopher-bishop; in the alchemical tradition, many texts were pseudonymously attributed to him to lend them ancient authority.</note> And to sell, distribute, and offer them in all places and locations of this Kingdom during the term of ten years, beginning from the day of these presents, with prohibitions and bans to all booksellers, printers, and others involved in printing in this Kingdom, against printing them in the Latin, French, or any other language, nor exposing them for sale directly or indirectly during the said time, without the permission or consent of the said Arnauld, upon pain of confiscation of the said books, a fine of two thousand <term>livres</term> <note>The "livre" was the currency of France until 1795.</note>, and all costs, damages, and interests: His Majesty further wills that by placing a summary of the contents of the said privilege at the beginning or at the end of the said treatises, it shall be held as duly notified and come to the knowledge of all.

->By the King in his Council.<-

->Signed, **CEBERET**.<-

->And sealed with the Great Seal in yellow wax.<-

<summary>This page records the royal publishing license granted to Pierre Arnauld in 1612, giving him a ten-year monopoly on the publication of major alchemical works by Artephius, Nicolas Flamel, and Synesius.</summary>
<keywords>Royal Privilege, copyright, Pierre Arnauld, Artephius, Nicolas Flamel, Synesius, alchemy, book trade, 17th-century law</keywords>

### Extract of the Royal Privilege.

<image-desc>A decorative square woodcut initial letter 'P' featuring floral and foliate motifs.</image-desc>

BY Letters Patent <term>Letters Patent: An open legal document issued by a monarch to grant a right, monopoly, or title.</term> of the King given at Paris on the 12th day of March, one thousand six hundred and twelve, signed by the King in his Council, Ceberet, and sealed with the Great Seal in yellow wax: It is permitted and granted by special privilege to PIERRE ARNAULD, Lord of the Chevallerie in Poitou, to have printed by whomever he sees fit, *Three Treatises not yet printed*, namely: *The Secret Book of the very ancient Philosopher Artephius <note>Artephius was a legendary figure in alchemical lore, rumored to have lived for a thousand years through his mastery of the art.</note> treating the occult art and metallic transmutation, in Latin and French; furthermore, the Hieroglyphic Figures of Nicolas FLAMEL <note>Nicolas Flamel (c. 1330–1418) was a Parisian scribe who, long after his death, became famous as an alchemist who supposedly discovered the secret of the Philosopher’s Stone.</note>, with the explanation of the same by the said FLAMEL; Together with the true Book of the learned Synesius <note>Synesius of Cyrene was a 4th-century philosopher-bishop, though the alchemical works attributed to him are likely of later origin.</note>, a Greek Abbot:* And to sell, distribute, and trade them in all places and areas of this Kingdom during a period of ten years, beginning from the day of these presents. Prohibitions and warnings are issued to all Booksellers, Printers, and others involved in the printing business in this Kingdom, forbidding them from printing these in the Latin, French, or any other language, or from exposing them for sale directly or indirectly during the said time, without the permission or consent of the said Arnauld, under penalty of confiscation of the said books, a fine of two thousand livres <term>livre: The primary currency of France during this period.</term>, and all costs, damages, and interests. Furthermore, His Majesty wills that by placing a summary of the contents of the said privilege at the beginning or at the end of the said Treatises, it shall be held as duly notified and come to the knowledge of all.

->By the King in his Council.<-

->Signed, CEBERET.<-

->And sealed with the Great Seal in yellow wax.<-

<summary>This page presents the official royal copyright or "privilege" granted to Pierre Arnauld in 1612, authorizing him to publish three significant alchemical texts by Artephius, Nicolas Flamel, and Synesius. It outlines the legal protections against unauthorized printing and the hefty fines for those who violate this exclusivity.</summary>
<keywords>Royal Privilege, Pierre Arnauld, Artephius, Nicolas Flamel, Synesius, alchemy, metallic transmutation, letters patent, 1612, book history, copyright law</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page follows the Extract of the Royal Privilege (page 3), providing an introductory preface to the reader regarding the author Artephius and the nature of the work.</meta>

### ->PREFACE TO THE READER.<-

Our Artephius [benevolent Reader] alone among all other Philosophers is not at all envious <note>In alchemical literature, "envy" refers to the common practice of masters hiding secrets from those they deemed unworthy; to be "not envious" meant being generous with one's knowledge.</note>, just as he himself says hereafter in several places; this is the reason why he explains the whole art in very clear words in this treatise, interpreting as much as he can the windings and sophistries <note>original: "ambages & sophismes"</note> of others. However, so that the impious, the ignorant, and the wicked might not easily find the means to harm the good by learning this science, he has veiled the core of the art slightly by a clever method—acting as if he were repeating the same thing several times. For in these repetitions, he always changes a few words, often seeming to say the opposite of what he said before, wishing to leave to the reader's judgment the good path as well as the bad, so that if someone finds what he desires, he may give thanks to God; if, on the contrary, he finds he is not making progress, he should duly reread these writings.

Thus does the learned Johannes Pontanus <note>A 15th-century author often cited for his letter describing the "Secret Fire" of alchemy.</note> [who says in his Epistle printed in the *Chemical Theatre* <note>original: "Theatre Chimique," referring to the *Theatrum Chemicum*, a massive multi-volume collection of alchemical writings first published in 1602.</note>]: "They err [he says, speaking of all those who labor in this work], they have erred, and will always err, because the Philosophers never put in their books the proper agent, except for a single one called Artephius; but he speaks for himself, and if I had not read Artephius and known what he was talking about, I never would have perfected the work." Therefore, read this book—indeed, reread it—until you have recognized him speaking, and you can obtain the desired end. 

It would be superfluous to say more about our author; it is enough that he lived for the span of a thousand years by the grace of God and the use [as he says] of this <term>quintessence</term> <term>Quintessence: In alchemy, the fifth and highest element, or the purest essence of a substance, believed to possess life-prolonging or curative powers.</term>. This same fact is witnessed by Roger Bacon <note>Roger Bacon (c. 1214–1292) was a medieval English philosopher and friar who emphasized the study of nature through experience.</note> in his book *On the Admirable Works of Nature*; and again by the very learned Theophrastus Paracelsus <note>Paracelsus (1493–1541) was a Swiss physician and alchemist who is considered the father of toxicology and modern chemical medicine.</note> in his book *On Long Life*. Which span of a thousand years no other Philosopher, not even Father Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the origins of alchemy and "Hermetic" philosophy are attributed.</note>, was ever able to reach. Consider, then, if [perhaps] this man did not understand the manner of using this <term>stone</term> <term>Philosopher's Stone: A legendary alchemical substance said to be capable of turning base metals into gold and providing the Elixir of Life.</term> better than the others. However, just as he is, use him and our labors for the glory of God and the benefit of the Kingdom of France. 

->To God.<-

<summary>This preface introduces Artephius as a uniquely transparent alchemist who purposely used repetitive methods to hide his secrets from the wicked while guiding the worthy. It emphasizes his legendary longevity—one thousand years—as proof of his mastery over the alchemical quintessence.</summary>

<keywords>Artephius, Johannes Pontanus, Roger Bacon, Paracelsus, Hermes Trismegistus, quintessence, Philosopher's Stone, alchemical secrecy, longevity</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page continues the preface introduced on the previous page, further establishing the unique transparency of Artephius compared to other alchemical authors and citing his legendary longevity.</meta>

<page-num>3</page-num>

### ->PREFACE TO THE READER.<-

<image-desc>Decorative ornamental initial 'A' with floral and vine-like motifs.</image-desc>

**O**ur Artephius [benevolent Reader] alone among the Philosophers is without envy <note>As noted previously, "envy" in alchemy refers to the practice of intentionally hiding secrets to prevent the "unworthy" from succeeding.</note>, as he himself asserts in several places hereafter. For this reason, he explains the entire art in the clearest of words, and as much as he is able, he resolves and clears away the ambiguities and sophistries <note>original: "ambages & sophismata"; these were intentionally confusing linguistic traps used by other alchemical writers.</note> of the wise. However, so that he might not provide the means of doing harm to the impious, the ignorant, and the wicked, he has veiled the truth beneath a clever method: at times affirming a point, at others denying it, and using repetitions. In doing so, he leaves to the reader's judgment the path of virtue, truth, and genuine labor. If anyone is able to grasp this path, let him return immortal thanks to God; but if he sees that he does not walk upon the true track, let him reread the author until he is able to reach deep into his mind. Thus did the most learned *Johannes Pontanus* <note>A 15th-century scholar whose "Epistle" is a foundational text in the alchemical canon.</note> do, who says in his *Epistle* printed in the *Chemical Theatre* <note>original: "Theatro Chimico"; a vast collection of alchemical texts published in the 17th century.</note>:

> They err [he speaks of those laboring in the art], they have erred, and they will continue to err, because the Philosophers have not set down the proper agent <note>original: "proprium agens"; the specific substance or "secret fire" that triggers the alchemical transformation.</note> in their books, except for one who is named **ARTEPHIUS**. But he speaks for himself, and unless I had read **ARTEPHIUS** and felt him speak to me, I would never have reached the completion of the work.

Therefore, read him, and reread him, until you feel him speak, and you are able to obtain your desired end. There is no reason for me to say much more regarding our author; let it suffice that he lived for a thousand years by the grace of God (as he says) and through the use of this marvelous quintessence <note>term: "quintessence" (the "fifth essence"); a highly purified substance believed to be the life-force of matter, capable of healing and extending life.</note>. This is also witnessed by *Roger Bacon* <note>A 13th-century English philosopher and friar often associated with early science and alchemy.</note> in his book on the marvelous works of nature, and also by the most learned *Theophrastus Paracelsus* in his book on long life. They note that other Philosophers—not even the father *Hermes* <note>Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of alchemy.</note> himself—were able to reach such a span of a thousand years. See to it, then, that you do not overlook whether this author perhaps <unclear>knows</unclear> the virtues of our Stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone.</note> better than others. Regardless of how it may be, enjoy his work, and our own labors, for the glory of God and the benefit of the *Kingdom of France*. Farewell.

<sig>A iij</sig>
<warning>Ink stains at the bottom of the page obscuring the area around the signature and catchword.</warning>

<summary>In this preface, the editor introduces Artephius as a uniquely generous alchemist who lived for 1,000 years thanks to his discoveries. The text emphasizes that while Artephius is clearer than other masters, his work still requires careful, repetitive reading to grasp the "proper agent" of the alchemical process.</summary>

### ## ->PREFACE TO THE READER.<-

Our ARTEPHIVS <gloss>[ benevolent Reader ]</gloss> alone among all the other Philosophers is not envious <note>In alchemical literature, "envy" refers to the common practice of authors intentionally obscuring their recipes to prevent the "unworthy" from succeeding.</note>, just as he himself says hereafter in several places; this is the reason why he explains the whole art in this treatise in very clear words, interpreting as much as he can the ambiguities and sophistries <note>original: "ambages & sophismes"; these are the linguistic traps and riddles used by other alchemists.</note> of the others. However, so that the impious, ignorant, and wicked might not easily find the means to harm the good by learning this science, he has slightly veiled the core of the art through a clever method, acting as if he repeats a thing many times; for in these repetitions he always changes some words, often appearing to say the opposite of what he said before. He wishes to leave to the reader's judgment the right path as well as the wrong, so that if someone finds what they desire, they give thanks to God; if, on the contrary, they find they are not working correctly, they should reread these writings.

So did the learned Johannes Pontanus <gloss>[ who says in his Epistle printed in the Chemical Theatre ]</gloss> <note>The "Theatrum Chemicum" was a massive and influential compendium of alchemical texts published in the 17th century.</note>. "They err," <gloss>[ he says, speaking of all those who labor in this work ]</gloss> "they have erred, and they will always err, because the Philosophers have never put the proper agent <term>agent</term> <note>The "agent" is the specific catalyst or "secret fire" required to start the alchemical transformation, which most authors kept hidden.</note> in their books, except for one alone who is called Artephius; but he speaks for himself, and if I had not read Artephius and known what he was talking about, I never would have perfected the work." Therefore, read this book—indeed, reread it—until you have understood him speaking, and you are able to obtain the desired end.

It would be superfluous to say more of our author; it is enough that he lived for the space of a thousand years, by the grace of God and the use <gloss>[ as he says ]</gloss> of this quintessence <term>quintessence</term> <note>Literally the "fifth element," the most refined and pure substance, often associated with the Elixir of Life.</note>. This same fact is testified by Roger Bacon in his book *On the Admirable Works of Nature*; and again by the very learned Theophrastus Paracelsus in his book *On Long Life*. Which span of a thousand years no other Philosopher, not even Father Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Egyptian founder of the Hermetic and alchemical tradition.</note>, was ever able to reach. See then if <gloss>[ perhaps ]</gloss> this man did not understand the method of using this stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the alchemical quest.</note> better than the others. Nevertheless, such as it is, use it and our labors for the glory of God and the utility of the Kingdom of France. To God.

# ## ->PREFACE TO THE READER.<-

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial A featuring floral and vine motifs.</image-desc>

Our Artephius <gloss>[kind Reader]</gloss> alone among the Philosophers is free from envy <note>In alchemical literature, "envy" (invidia) refers to the common practice of authors intentionally obscuring their recipes to prevent the "unworthy" from succeeding.</note>, as he asserts of himself in several places hereafter; and for this reason, he explains the whole art in very clear words, and resolves and clears up the ambiguities and sophistries <note>original: "ambages & sophismata"; these are the linguistic traps and riddles used by other alchemists to hide their secrets.</note> of the wise as much as he is able. Yet, lest he provide the means of doing harm to the wicked, the ignorant, and the evil, he has veiled the truth within an artful method, sometimes asserting and sometimes denying, throughout his repetitions. He leaves to the reader's judgment the way of virtue, of truth, and of true labor; if anyone is able to grasp it, let him give immortal thanks to God alone. If, however, he sees that he does not walk in the true path, let him reread the author until he can reach the depth of his mind. So did the most learned **Johannes Pontanus**, who says in his Epistle printed in the *Chemical Theatre* <note>original: "Theatro Chimico"; a massive and influential compendium of alchemical texts published in the 17th century.</note>:

> They err (he speaks of those laboring in the art), they have erred, and they will err, because the Philosophers did not set down the proper agent <term>agent: the active principle or catalyst in alchemical transformation</term>, except for one who is named **ARTEPHIUS**; but he speaks for himself, and unless I had read **ARTEPHIUS** and sensed him speaking, I would never have reached the completion of the work.

Therefore, read him, and reread him, until you sense him speaking, and you are able to obtain the desired end. There is no need for me to say much about our author; let it suffice that he lived for a thousand years, by the grace (he says) of God and the use of this wonderful quintessence <term>quintessence: the "fifth element" or the purified essence of a substance, believed to grant health and long life</term>. This is also attested to by **Roger Bacon** in his book *On the Marvellous Works of Nature*; and also by the most learned **Theophrastus Paracelsus** in his book *On Long Life*, noting that other Philosophers—not even father **Hermes** <note>Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of the hermetic and alchemical tradition.</note> himself—could reach that span of a thousand years. See to it then, that perhaps this author knows the virtues of our stone <term>stone: the Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the "Great Work"</term> better than <unclear>others</unclear>. Use him, however it may be, and our labors, for the glory of God and the benefit of the Kingdom of France. Farewell.

# THE SECRET BOOK OF THE VERY-
## ancient Philosopher ARTEPHIVS treating of the occult Art and the Philosopher’s Stone.

---

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial letter 'L' featuring a landscape with foliage and a small building.</image-desc>

Antimony is of the parts of Saturn <note>In alchemy, Saturn is the planetary name for the metal lead. Antimony was often considered a "leaden" mineral because of its weight and appearance.</note>, having in all ways its nature; likewise, this Saturnine Antimony is suited to the Sun <note>The Sun is the alchemical symbol for Gold.</note>, having within itself the quicksilver <note>original: "argent vif"; mercury.</note> in which no metal sinks except gold: that is to say, truly only the Sun sinks into the Antimonial Saturnine quicksilver, without which quicksilver no metal can be whitened. It whitens, therefore, the latten <term>latten: traditionally a copper alloy similar to brass, but here used to refer to the "body" of the gold that must be purified</term>, that is to say, the gold, and reduces the perfect body into its first matter, that is to say, into sulfur and quicksilver of a white color, more shining than a mirror. It dissolves (I say) the perfect body which is of its nature: For this water is friendly and pleasing to the metals, whitening the Sun, because it contains a white quicksilver. And from this you must draw a very great secret, namely that the Saturnine Antimonial water must be Mercurial and white, so that it may whiten the gold, not burning it, but only dissolving it, and afterwards congealing in the form of a white cream. This is why the Philosopher says that this water makes the body [the metal] become volatile, because after it has been dissolved and cooled, it rises up to the surface of the water. 

Take (he says) raw gold in leaves, or in plates, or calcined <term>calcined: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or change its chemical state</term> by Mercury; place it in our Saturnine Antimonial vinegar, Mercurial, and drawn from sal ammoniac <term>sal ammoniac: ammonium chloride, a salt used in alchemy to help dissolve metals</term> (as it is called); place it in a glass vessel, wide and four fingers high or more, and leave it there in a temperate heat, and you will see in a short time rise up something like an oily liquor floating on top in the form of a film. Collect it with a spoon, or by dipping a feather, and thus several times a day gather it, until nothing more rises; then evaporate the water by fire—that is, the superfluous moisture of the vinegar—and there will remain for you a quintessence of gold in the form of a white oil, incombustible. Within this oil the Philosophers have placed their greatest secrets, and this oil is of an exceeding sweetness, having great virtues to soothe the pain of wounds.

The whole secret, therefore, of this Antimonial secret, is that by the above mentioned

# THE SECRET BOOK
## of the most ancient philosopher ARTEPHIUS
### concerning the occult art and the philosopher’s stone.

<margin>1</margin>
Antimony is part of the nature of Saturn <note>In alchemy, Saturn is the planetary name for the metal lead. Antimony was often considered a "leaden" mineral because of its weight and appearance.</note>, and in all ways it possesses its nature; this Saturnine Antimony is suited to the Sun <note>The Sun is the alchemical symbol for Gold.</note>, and within it is a quicksilver <note>original: "argentum vivum"; mercury.</note> in which no metal sinks except gold—that is to say, the Sun truly sinks only into the Antimonial Saturnine quicksilver. Without that quicksilver, no metal can be whitened. It whitens, therefore, the latten <term>latten: original "latonem"; here referring to the base or "impure" body of the gold that must be purified</term>, which is to say the gold, and reduces the perfect body into its first matter, namely into sulfur and quicksilver of a white color, more shining than a mirror. It dissolves (I say) the perfect body which is of its own nature. For that water is friendly and pleasing to the metals, whitening the Sun because it contains a white quicksilver. And from this you may draw a most great secret: namely, that the water of Saturnine Antimony must be Mercurial and white so that it may whiten gold; it does not burn it, but rather dissolves it, afterwards congealing itself into the form of a white cream. Therefore the Philosopher says that this water makes the body volatile, because after it has been dissolved in this water and cooled, it rises to the top, floating on the surface of the water. Take (he says) crude gold in leaf, or in thin plates, or calcined <note>reduced to a powder through heat</note> by Mercury, and place it in 
<margin>vinegar</margin>
our Saturnine, Mercurial, Antimonial vinegar <note>original: "aceto"; alchemists used the word "vinegar" to describe powerful acid solvents</note> and sal ammoniac <note>original: "salis armoniaci"; ammonium chloride, used to help dissolve metals</note> in a glass vessel four fingers wide and high, or more. Leave it there in a moderate heat, and in a short time you will see a liquor like oil rise and float on the top like a thin film. Collect this with a spoon or a feather by dipping it in; collect it thus several times a day until nothing more rises. Then, by fire, you shall evaporate the water—that is, the superfluous moisture of the vinegar—and there will remain for you the fifth essence <note>original: "quinta essentia"; the quintessence or purest medicinal core of a substance</note> of gold in the form of a white, incombustible oil. Within this oil, the Philosophers have placed their greatest secrets; this oil possesses a most profound sweetness <note>original: "dulcedinem"; likely referring to a chemical property or a metaphorical spiritual "sweetness"</note> and is of great value in soothing the pains of wounds.
<margin>2</margin>

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the "Antimonial water" or "philosophical vinegar," explaining its role as the primary solvent that breaks down metals into their foundational elements to eventually form the Philosopher’s Stone.</meta>

...the heavy, viscous, and precious water, resolving all crude bodies into their first matter—that is, into earth and viscous powder, which means into sulfur and quicksilver <note>original: "souphre & argent vif"; in alchemy, these are the two fundamental principles of all metals</note>. If, therefore, you put any metal whatsoever into this water, whether filed or thinned, and leave it for a certain time in a gentle and slow heat, it will be entirely dissolved and changed into viscous water and white oil, as has already been said. In this way, it softens the body and prepares it for fusion and liquefaction; indeed, it makes all things fusible, such as stones and metals, and then gives them spirit and life. Therefore, it dissolves all things by an admirable solution, converting the perfect body into a medicine that is fusible, flowing, penetrating, and more fixed, increasing both its weight and color.

<margin>5</margin>
Work, then, with this water, and you shall have what you desire. For it is the spirit and the soul of the Sun <note>Gold</note> and the Moon <note>Silver</note>, the oil, the dissolving water, the fountain, the Bain Marie <term>Bain Marie: a water bath used to provide gentle, even heat, named after Mary the Jewess, a legendary alchemist</term>, the fire against nature, the moist fire—secret, occult, and invisible—and the strongest vinegar, of which an ancient Philosopher said: 
> I prayed to the Lord, and he showed me a clear water, which I knew to be a pure altering vinegar, penetrating and digesting: a vinegar, I say, penetrative and an instrument moving gold or silver to putrefaction, resolution, and reduction into their first matter. 

This is the unique agent in this world for this Art, which alone can resolve and reincrudate <term>reincrudate: to return a processed metal to its "crude" or raw natural state</term> metallic bodies while preserving their species. It is therefore the only fit and natural means by which we must resolve the bodies of the Sun and the Moon by an admirable and solemn dissolution, preserving the species without any destruction, but only changing it into a new, nobler, and better form and generation—that is to say, into the perfect stone of the Philosophers, which is their admirable secret.

Now, this water is a certain intermediate substance, clear as pure silver, which must receive the tinctures of the Sun and the Moon so that it may congeal and be converted with them into white and living earth. For this water has need of the perfect bodies so that, along with them after dissolution, it may congeal, fix, and coagulate into white earth; forasmuch as their dissolution is their coagulation, because they have one and the same operation, and the one cannot be dissolved unless the other congeals. And there is no other water that can dissolve the bodies except that one alone which remains permanent with them in matter and form. Indeed, the permanent cannot exist unless it be of the same nature as the other body, so that they may become one. When you see, therefore, your water coagulate itself with the bodies within it...

<margin>5</margin> Work, therefore, with this water, and you will achieve what you desire from it. For it is the spirit and the soul of the Sun <note>original: "Solis"; gold</note> and the Moon <note>original: "Lunae"; silver</note>, the oil and the dissolving water, the fountain, the Bath of Mary <term>Bain Marie: a gentle water bath used to provide even heat, traditionally named after the legendary alchemist Mary the Jewess</term>, the "fire against nature," the moist fire, the secret, hidden, and invisible fire, and the sharpest vinegar, about which a certain ancient philosopher says:

> I prayed to the Lord, and he showed me a clear water, which I knew to be a pure altering vinegar, penetrating and digesting.

A penetrative vinegar, I say, and an instrument moving toward putrefaction, resolution, and the reduction of gold or silver into their first matter; it is the unique agent in the whole world in this art that can clearly resolve and return metallic bodies to their raw state <note>original: "reincrudare"; to return a metal to its 'crude' or 'raw' foundational form</note> while preserving their essential nature. It is, therefore, the only suitable and natural medium through which we must resolve the perfect bodies of the Sun and Moon by a marvelous and formal solution, while preserving their nature and without any destruction—except toward a new, nobler, and better form or generation: namely, into the perfect Stone of the Philosophers, which is their marvelous secret and mystery.

<margin>6</margin> This water is a certain intermediate substance, as clear as pure silver, which must receive the tinctures <note>original: "tincturas"; the coloring or transformative essence</note> of the Sun and Moon so that it may be congealed and converted into a living white earth. For this water has need of the perfect bodies, so that after dissolution, it may be congealed, fixed, and coagulated with them into a white earth. Moreover, their solution is also their congelation; for they have one and the same operation, because one is not dissolved without the other being congealed. Nor is there any other water that can dissolve bodies except that which remains with them in matter and form; indeed, it cannot remain unless it is of the same nature as the other, so that they may together become one. When you see, therefore, the water coagulate itself with the bodies in it...

...dissolved, be assured that your knowledge, method, and operations are true and philosophical, and that you are proceeding well in the Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy, often called the "Great Art" or "Sacred Art."</note>.

<margin>4</margin> Thus nature is improved by its like nature; that is to say, gold and silver are bettered in our water, just as our water is by these bodies. This water is also called the medium and the middle of the soul, without which we cannot work in this Art. It is the vegetable, animal, and mineral fire, which preserves the fixed spirit of the Sun <note>original: "Soleil"; gold</note> and the Moon <note>original: "Lune"; silver</note>, the destroyer of bodies, and the victor: because it destroys and dissolves the body, and changes the metallic forms, ensuring that the bodies are no longer bodies, but only fixed spirits, converting those forms into a moist, soft, and fluid substance. This substance has the ability and power to enter into other imperfect bodies <note>Base metals like lead, tin, or copper</note>, and to mingle with them indivisibly, dyeing and perfecting them together—something these bodies could not do before, because they were dry and hard, and this hardness has no power for dyeing nor for perfection. 

Therefore, we appropriately convert these two bodies into a fluid substance, inasmuch as every dye stains a thousand times more effectively as a soft and liquid substance than as a dry one, as can be seen with saffron <note>Alchemists frequently used saffron as an analogy because a tiny amount can color a large volume of liquid, much like the "Tincture" or Philosopher's Stone was said to do with metals.</note>. Thus, the transmutation of imperfect metals is impossible through hard and dry bodies, but only through soft and liquid ones. From this, one must conclude that the moisture must be brought back, and the hidden revealed. This is called re-incrudating <term>reincrudation: the process of returning a processed or "mature" metal to its "crude" or raw, liquid-like state so it can be reworked.</term> the bodies—that is to say, cooking and softening them until they are deprived of their hard and dry physicality, because dry matter does not penetrate anything, nor does it dye anything but itself. Therefore, the dry and earthy body does not dye anything unless it is itself dyed: for (as it is said) the thick earthy substance does not enter nor dye; because it does not enter, it does not alter. Consequently, gold does not dye until its hidden spirit is pulled and extracted from its belly by our white water, and is made entirely spiritual—a white smoke, a white spirit, and an admirable soul.

<margin>8</margin> Therefore, we must use our water to thin the perfect bodies, to alter them, and to soften them, so that afterwards they can mingle with the other imperfect ones. This is why, if we had no other benefit or use for this Antimonial water of ours but this—that it makes the perfect bodies subtle, soft, and fluid according to its nature—it would be enough for us. For it reduces the bodies to the first origin of their sulfur and Mercury <note>Sulfur and Mercury were considered the two primary spiritual "seeds" or components from which all metals grew in the earth.</note>. Then, after a short time, in less than an hour of a day, we can do with them upon the earth what nature has done beneath it, in the mines of the earth, in a thousand years—which is almost miraculous. Our final secret is, therefore, by our water to make the bodies vo-

...[once they are] dissolved, be assured that your knowledge, method, and operations are true and philosophical, and that you are proceeding correctly in the Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy, the sacred science of transformation.</note>.

Therefore, nature is improved within its own like nature; that is, gold and silver are improved in our water, and the water is likewise improved by those bodies. This water is also called the "medium of the soul," without which we can do nothing in this Art. It is a vegetable, animal, and mineral fire that preserves the fixed spirit of the Sun <note>original: "Solis"; alchemical code for Gold</note> and the Moon <note>original: "Lunae"; alchemical code for Silver</note>. It is the destroyer of bodies and the victor: 
<margin>7</margin>
because it destroys, breaks down, and changes metallic forms. It ensures that they cease to be bodies and become fixed spirits instead, converting them into a moist, soft, and fluid substance. This substance has the power and virtue to enter into other imperfect bodies <note>Base metals like lead or tin</note>, mixing with them to the smallest degree, dyeing and perfecting them. They could not do this when they were dry and hard metallic bodies, which have no power of entry nor the virtue of dyeing and perfecting imperfect things. 
<margin>✓</margin>
We therefore rightly convert these bodies into a fluid substance, because every tincture <note>A "tincture" is the concentrated essence or "medicine" used to transform metals.</note> dyes a thousand times more effectively as a liquid and soft substance than as a dry one, as is evident with saffron <note>original: "croco"; alchemists often compared the "Stone" to saffron because a tiny amount of either can color a vast amount of material.</note>. Therefore, the transmutation of imperfect metals is impossible to achieve through perfect bodies that are dry, unless they are first reduced into a soft and fluid primary matter. From these things, it is necessary that the moisture be returned and that which is hidden be revealed. And this is to "re-incrudate" <term>Re-incrudate: To return a metal to its "crude" or raw, liquid state (its "first matter") so it can be fundamentally changed.</term> the bodies—that is, to boil and soften them until they are stripped of their hard and dry physicality. For a dry thing does not enter or dye anything except itself. Therefore, a dry, earthy body does not dye unless it is dyed itself; because (as has been said) an earthy thing does not enter or dye itself; and because it does not enter, it does not alter. For this reason, gold does not dye until its hidden spirit is extracted from its belly by our white water, and it becomes entirely spiritual: a white smoke, a white spirit, and a wondrous soul.

<margin>8</margin>
Wherefore we must, by means of our water, thin, alter, and soften the perfect bodies, so that they may then be mixed with the other imperfect bodies. Hence, if we had no other benefit from that Antimonial water except that it renders bodies subtle, soft, and fluid according to its nature, that would be enough for us. For it reduces bodies to their first origin of sulfur and Mercury <note>The "Mercury-Sulfur theory" was the prevailing belief that all metals were made of these two principles; balancing them perfectly created gold.</note>, so that from these, in a short time—less than an hour of a day—we may accomplish upon the earth what nature has worked beneath in the mines of the earth over thousands of years, which is almost miraculous. It is therefore our final secret: to make [spiritual] bodies through our water.

And it is the fountain in which the King <note>Gold</note> and Queen <note>Silver</note> wash themselves, and the mother who must be placed and sealed in the belly of her infant, who is the Sun who proceeded from her and whom she brought forth; therefore they mutually love and cherish each other like mother and son, and are joined together, since they came from one and the same root and are of the same substance and nature. And since this water is the water of vegetable life <note>original: "vitæ vegetabilis"; referring to the power of growth and multiplication.</note>, it therefore gives life, and causes that same dead body to vegetate, grow, and sprout, and to resuscitate it from death to life by solution and sublimation. In such an operation the body is turned into spirit, and the spirit into body, and then friendship, peace, concord, and the union of contraries is made—that is, of body and spirit—which mutually change their natures which they receive, and communicate with each other in the smallest parts, so that heat is mixed with cold, and dry with moist, and hard with soft. In this way, the mixture of contrary natures occurs—namely cold with heat, and moist with dry—and a wonderful connection is formed among enemies. Our dissolution of bodies then...
<margin>12</margin>

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the alchemical "water," focusing on the process of "mortification" (the symbolic death and breakdown of the material) and its subsequent elevation into a "white stone" or "permanent water" through the union of the volatile and the fixed.</meta>

<page-num>18</page-num>

which occurs in this first water, is nothing other than a **mortification** <note>In alchemical practice, "mortification" refers to the breakdown or "death" of a substance's initial form, allowing it to be transformed into a higher state.</note> of the moist by the dry, insofar as the moist is always coagulated by the dry; for moisture is contained and halted only by its own dryness, ending in a body or in earth. Our hard and dry bodies, therefore, put them into our first water, in a well-closed vessel, where they shall remain until they are dissolved and rise to the top; and then these bodies may be called a **new body**, the **white gold of Alchemy**, the **white stone**, the **non-burning white sulphur**, and the **stone of Paradise**—that is to say, the stone that converts imperfect metals into fine, white silver. Having this, we also have all together: the body, the soul, and the spirit. Of this spirit and soul, it is said that they cannot be extracted from perfect bodies <note>Gold and silver.</note> except by the conjunction of our dissolving water; for it is certain that the fixed thing cannot be raised upward except by the conjunction of the volatile thing. The spirit, therefore, by means of the water and the soul, will draw itself from the bodies, which body will become "non-body," because in that same instant the spirit with the soul of the bodies rises to the top, to the superior part, which is the perfection of the stone and is called **sublimation**.

This sublimation (says **Florentius the Catalan** <note>Florentius Cathalanus, an alchemical author often cited in 14th and 15th-century manuscripts.</note>) is performed by sour, spiritual, and volatile things, which are of a sulphurous and viscous nature, which dissolve and cause the bodies to rise into the air as spirit. And in this sublimation, a certain part and portion of our aforementioned first water rises upward with the bodies, joining together, ascending and sublimating into a "middle substance" that partakes of the nature of both—namely, of the two bodies and the water. Therefore, this middle substance is called the **corporeal and spiritual compound**, **Corsufle**, **Combar**, **Ethelie**, **Zandarith**, and **the good Duenech** <note>These are traditional "Decknamen" or secret names, many of Arabic origin, used to describe the intermediate state of the matter during the Great Work.</note>.

However, it is properly called **permanent water**, because it does not flee from the fire, remaining perpetually joined with the conjoined bodies—that is to say, with the **Sun** <note>Gold</note> and the **Moon** <note>Silver</note>—communicating to them a living, incombustible, and very firm **tincture** <note>The "tincture" is the transformative essence that can "dye" or transmute other metals.</note>, more noble and precious than the previous one those bodies possessed. Because afterward, this tincture can flow upon the bodies, just like oil, piercing and penetrating everything with an admirable fixation, because this tincture is the spirit, and the spirit is the soul, and the soul is the body: for in this operation the body is made spirit of a very subtle nature, and similarly the spirit incorporates itself and becomes of the nature of the bodies with the bodies; and thus our stone contains body, soul, and spirit.

O nature, how you change bodies into spirit! Which you could not do if the spirit did not incorporate with the bodies, and if the bodies with the spirit did not become volatile, and afterward permanent. They have therefore passed into one another, and have been converted together by **wisdom**. O wisdom <note>original: "sapience"</note>, how you make gold volatile and fugitive, even though naturally it is very fixed. It

--- Page 26 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the alchemical "first water," explaining the process of separating the pure, "subtle" essence of the material from its "gross" or worthless physical residue through a process of natural sublimation.</meta>

<page-num>20</page-num>

One must therefore dissolve and liquefy these bodies with our water, and make them into **permanent water**, **sublimated golden water**, leaving at the bottom the heavy, terrestrial, and superfluous dry matter. And in this <term>sublimation</term> <note>The process where a substance is heated until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used here to purify the material.</note> the fire must be gentle and slow: for if the bodies are not purified by this slow-fire sublimation, and if their grossest terrestrial parts (note this well) are not separated from the **filth of the dead**, you will not be able to complete the work. For you have need only of this subtle and light nature, which rises to the top of the dissolved bodies, which will be easily given to you by our water if you work gently, for it will separate the <term>heterogeneous</term> <note>different or incompatible substances</note> from the <term>homogeneous</term> <note>substances of the same kind or nature</note>.

Our compound therefore receives a cleaning and purification by our moist fire—that is to say, by dissolving and sublimating that which is pure and white, and setting aside the <term>feces</term> <note>The dregs or waste matter left over after a chemical reaction.</note> like a **voluntary vomiting**, as **Azinaban** <note>An authority often cited in medieval alchemical texts, possibly a corruption of an Arabic name.</note> says. For in such natural dissolution and sublimation, a choice of the elements is made, a purification and separation of the pure from the impure, so that the pure and the white rise to the top, and the impure and fixed terrestrial matter remains at the bottom of the water and the vessel. This must be thrown away and removed because it is of no value, taking only the middle substance—white, flowing, and melting—leaving the terrestrial dregs that remained at the bottom, which come mainly from the water. What remains in this bottom is nothing but mud and **damned or condemned earth** <note>original: "terre damnée"; the useless, exhausted residue remaining after distillation or sublimation.</note>, which is worth nothing, nor can it ever be worth anything, unlike this clear white matter, pure and clean, which alone we must take.

And upon this **Capharean rock** <note>original: "rocher Capharée"; a reference to the dangerous Cape Caphareus in Greece where many ships were wrecked. Here, it is a metaphor for a common mistake where alchemists fail in their work.</note>, the knowledge and the "ship" of disciples and students of Philosophy most often perish very imprudently (as has happened to me in the past), because Philosophers most often teach to do the opposite—that is to say, that one must only remove the moisture, which is the blackness. However, they only say and write this in order to deceive the coarse and ignorant who, on their own and without a master, tireless reading, or prayer to Almighty God, desire to carry away victoriously this **blessed Golden Fleece** <note>A mythological reference used by alchemists to represent the ultimate goal: the Philosopher's Stone.</note>.

Note, therefore, that this separation, division, and sublimation is undoubtedly the key to the entire work. Thus, after the <term>putrefaction</term> <note>The stage where the matter breaks down and turns black.</note> and dissolution of these bodies, our bodies rise to the top, even to the surface of the dissolving water, in a white color; and this whiteness is **life**. For into this whiteness, along with the spirits of the **Sun** <note>Alchemical symbol for Gold.</note> and the **Moon** <note>Alchemical symbol for Silver.</note>, is infused the **Antimonial and Mercurial soul**, which separates the subtle from the thick and the pure from the impure, raising little by little the subtle part of the body from its dregs, until all the pure is separated and raised. And in this is accomplished our philosophical and natural sublimation.

<summary>This page describes the essential process of purification through gentle heat, where the pure "white" essence rises and the worthless "damned earth" is discarded. The author warns that many seekers fail here by misinterpreting philosophical metaphors about removing blackness.</summary>

This residue is to be abandoned and removed since it is of no value, taking only the middle substance—white, flowing, and melting—and leaving the foul earth which remained below in the bottom, especially from the water's part, which is the dross and **condemned earth** <note>original: *terra damnata*; a technical term for the useless residue left after chemical extraction.</note>, which is worth nothing, nor can it ever provide anything good, unlike that clear white matter, pure and shining, which alone we must accept. However, they only say and write this so that they may deceive the unwary who, without a teacher or tireless reading and prayer to Almighty God, desire to pluck this **Golden Fleece** <note>The prize of the Argonauts, representing the ultimate goal of the alchemical Work.</note>.

Note therefore that this separation, division, and sublimation is without a doubt the key to the whole work. Therefore, after the <term>putrefaction</term> <note>The stage where matter breaks down and turns black.</note> and dissolution of these bodies, our bodies raise themselves on high as far as the surface of the dissolving water, into the color of whiteness. And in this is completed our philosophical and natural sublimation. And with this

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the purification process through sublimation, specifically focusing on the extraction of the "soul" or "mineral virtue" from the material bodies (the Sun and Moon, representing Gold and Silver) and its transformation into the "Philosophical Mercury."</meta>

<page-num>22</page-num>

And with this whiteness, the soul is infused into the body—that is to say, the **mineral virtue**, which is more subtle than fire, seeing as it is a true <term>quintessence</term> <note>The "fifth essence" or purest, most concentrated part of a substance.</note> and true life, which desires and longs to be born and to strip away the gross, terrestrial feces it has taken from the <term>menstrual</term> <note>In alchemy, a "menstruum" is a solvent used to dissolve solids, not a biological term.</note> and from the corruption of its place of origin. And in this consists our **philosophical sublimation**, not using common <gloss>vulgar</gloss> Mercury, which is wicked and possesses no qualities similar to those with which our Mercury is adorned, being extracted from its <term>Vitriolic</term> <note>Derived from vitriol (metallic sulfates), often associated with the "caverns" or depths of the earth.</note> caverns; but let us return to our sublimation. 

It <gloss>is</gloss> therefore certain in this art that this soul extracted from the bodies can only be raised by the addition of the volatile thing which is of its own kind, by which the bodies are rendered volatile and spiritual as they rise, becoming subtle and sublimated against their own corporeal nature—which is heavy and weighty. In this manner, they become non-corporeal, incorporeal, and a quintessence of the nature of spirits, which is called the **bird of Hermes**, and the **Mercury extracted from red iron**; and thus the terrestrial parts remain at the bottom, or rather the grosser parts of the bodies, which cannot be perfectly dissolved by any subtle means or artifice of the spirit. 

And this white smoke, this **white gold**—that is to say, this quintessence—is also called the **composed magnesia**, which contains (just like a human) or is composed (just like a human) of body, soul, and spirit: Its **body** is the fixed earth of the **Sun** <note>Gold</note>, which is more than highly subtle, and which rises upward heavily by the force of our **divine water**; Its **soul** is the tincture of the **Sun** and the **Moon** <note>Silver</note>, proceeding from the conjunction of these two; and the **spirit** is the mineral virtue of the two bodies and of the water, which carries the soul, or the white tincture, onto the bodies and from the bodies, just as the dye of all dyes is carried onto cloth by water. 

And this **Mercurial spirit** is the bond of the Solar soul, and the Solar body is the body of fixation, containing the spirit and the soul along with the Moon. The spirit therefore penetrates the fixed body, the soul joins, tints, and whitens; from these three united together is made our **Stone**—that is to say, from the Sun, the Moon, and Mercury. Therefore, with our **golden water**, nature is extracted, overcoming all nature; and for this reason, if the bodies are not dissolved by this water of ours, and by it imbued, softened, and gently and diligently ruled until they leave behind their thickness and density and change into a subtle and impalpable spirit, our labor will always be in vain. This is because if the bodies are not changed into "non-bodies"—that is to say, into the **Mercury of the Philosophers**—one has not yet found the rule of the Art; and this is because it is impossible to extract from the bodies that most subtle soul which contains within itself all tinctures, unless those bodies are first [dissolved].

...with whiteness, the soul is infused into the body—that is, the **mineral virtue**, which is subtler than fire, since it is a true <term>quintessence</term> <note>original: "quinta essentia"; the "fifth essence" or the purest, most concentrated part of a substance, beyond the four physical elements.</note> and life. It <margin>17</margin> is therefore most certain in this art that this soul, once extracted from the bodies, cannot be raised except by the addition of a volatile substance of its own kind. Through this, the bodies are rendered volatile and spiritual, lifting, refining, and sublimating themselves against their own heavy and weighty corporeal nature. In this way, they become "non-bodies" and a quintessence of a spiritual nature, which is called the **Bird of Hermes** <note>A famous alchemical symbol representing the process of sublimation and the volatility of the spirit.</note> and the **Mercury extracted from the red servant** <note>original: "seruo rubeo"; likely a reference to a specific mineral or ore like iron used in the process.</note>. Thus, the terrestrial parts remain at the bottom—or rather the grosser parts of the bodies—which cannot be perfectly dissolved by any ingenuity of the mind.

And that white smoke, that "white gold"—that is, this quintessence—is also called **composite magnesia**. It contains, or is composed like a man, of body, soul, and spirit. Its body is the fixed **solar earth**, which is more than subtle and is raised by the weight of our divine water. And that Mercurial spirit is the bond <note>original: "vinculum"</note> of the solar soul, and the solar body is the "body of fixation" containing the spirit and soul along with the Moon. Therefore, the spirit penetrates, <margin>18</margin> the body fixes, and the soul joins, tints, and whitens. --- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page appears to follow the previous discussion of sublimation and the "Bird of Hermes." Although the previous page was numbered 23 and this is numbered 22, the alchemical process remains consistent, moving from the extraction of the soul to the dissolution of bodies into a spiritual, "living" state. The catchword "Sufficit" (it is enough) suggests a conclusion to this specific instruction.</meta>

<page-num>22</page-num>

...re-dissolved in our water. Dissolve then the bodies in the golden water, and cook them until, by the force and virtue of the water, the whole tincture comes out in a white color, or as a white oil. And when you see this whiteness upon the water, know then that the bodies are liquefied; continue your <term>decoction</term> <note>The process of heating or boiling a substance in a liquid to extract its essence or medicinal properties.</note> still further until they give birth to the cloud, which they have already conceived—dark, black, and white. You shall therefore place the perfect bodies in our water, in a vessel <term>hermetically sealed</term> <note>original: "scellé Hermetiquement"; an airtight seal, named after Hermes Trismegistus, essential for maintaining the internal pressure and "spirit" of the work.</note> which you will keep over a gentle fire, until all is re-dissolved into a most precious oil.

Cook (says **Adfar**) <note>Adfar Alexandrinus, a legendary Arabian alchemist said to have been the teacher of Morienus.</note> with a gentle fire, as if for the nourishing and hatching of chicks from eggs, and until the bodies are dissolved, and until their tincture—note this well—which shall be most lovingly joined one to the other, comes out entirely. For it does not come out, nor is it extracted all at once, but only little by little, every day and every hour, until after a long time this dissolution is completely finished; and that which is dissolved immediately rises upon the water. 

In this solution, the fire must be slow, gentle, and continuous, until the bodies are made into a viscous, impalpable water, and until the whole tincture comes out at the beginning in a black color—which is the sign of true dissolution—and then afterwards, through long decoction, it becomes white and permanent water. For by governing it in its bath, it afterwards becomes clear, finally appearing like common <term>quicksilver</term> <note>original: "argent vif vulgaire"; mercury.</note>, rising into the air above the first water.

And therefore, when you see the bodies dissolved into viscous water, know then that they are converted into vapor, and that you have the souls separated from your dead bodies, and that they are placed by <term>sublimation</term> <note>In alchemy, sublimation is the process of purifying a substance by heating it into a vapor and then condensing it; it represents the spiritualization of matter.</note> into the order and state of spirits. Thereby both bodies, with a portion of our water, are made into flying spirits rising into the air; and the body composed of the male and the female—of the **Sun** and the **Moon** <note>Alchemical symbols for Gold (Sun/Male) and Silver (Moon/Female).</note>—and of this most subtle nature, cleansed by sublimation, takes life. It is inspired by its own moisture—that is to say, by its water—just as man is inspired by the air. This is why, from now on, it multiplies and grows in its kind, like all other things in the world. 

And in such philosophical elevation and sublimation, they all join together, and the new body, inspired by the air, lives vegetatively, which is miraculous. Therefore, if the bodies are not refined by water and fire to this point—so that they can rise like spirits, and until they are made like water, smoke, or **Mercury**—nothing is accomplished in the Art. However, as they rise like spirits, they are born in the air, and change into air, and become "life with life," so that they can never again be separated, just like water mixed with water. 

And thus it is said that the stone is wisely born in the air, because it is entirely spiritual. For this **Vulture** <note>A symbol for the volatile principle or the acidic solvent that "consumes" the base metals; flying without wings suggests it has been successfully spiritualized.</note> flying without wings cries out upon the mountain, saying: "I am the white from the black; and the red from the white; and the citron child of the red; I speak the truth, and I lie not."

<summary>This page describes the delicate process of dissolving metallic "bodies" into a spiritual "oil" or "vapor" using gentle heat, likened to hatching eggs. It explains the transition through the alchemical colors—black, white, and yellow—to reach a state where the matter becomes "living" and spiritualized.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the alchemical dissolution of "bodies" (metals). It expands on the instructions attributed to the sage Adfar mentioned on the previous page, detailing the transition from the black stage (nigredo) to the white stage (albedo) and the ultimate spiritualization of the matter.</meta>

<page-num>25</page-num>

...they may be resolved in our water. Therefore, dissolve the bodies in the golden water, and boil them until the whole tincture comes out through the water as a white color or into a white oil. When you see that whiteness upon the water, know then that the bodies are liquefied. Continue the <term>decoction</term> <note>The process of heating or boiling a substance in a liquid to extract its essence.</note> until they bring forth the cloud which they have conceived—dark, black, and white. Therefore, place the perfect bodies in our water, in a vessel <term>hermetically sealed</term> <note>original: "Hermetice sigillato"; an airtight seal essential for keeping the "spirits" of the matter from escaping during heating.</note>, over a gentle fire, and cook them continuously until they are perfectly <margin>19</margin> resolved into a most precious oil.

Cook [says **Adfar**] <note>Adfar Alexandrinus, a legendary teacher of alchemical secrets.</note> with a gentle fire, as if for the nourishing of eggs <note>A common alchemical metaphor for a slow, steady heat similar to an incubator.</note>, until the bodies are dissolved, and their most closely joined <gloss>[ note ]</gloss> tincture is extracted. However, it is not extracted all at once, but it comes out little by little, every day and every hour, until in the fullness of time a dissolution of this kind is completed; and that which is dissolved always seeks the higher part. In such a dissolution, let the fire be gentle and continuous, until they are dissolved into a viscous, impalpable water, and the whole tincture comes out in a black color first, which is the sign of a true solution <note>The "nigredo" or blackness, signifying the successful "death" and breakdown of the initial material.</note>. 

Then continue the decoction until it becomes a permanent white water, because ruling in its own bath, it will afterwards become clear and finally arrive as common quicksilver <note>original: "argentum viuum"; mercury.</note>, climbing through the air above the first water. And so, when you see the bodies dissolved into a viscous water, know then <margin>20</margin> that the bodies have been converted into vapor, and that you have the souls separated from the dead bodies, and brought into the order of spirits by <term>sublimation</term> <note>The process where a solid turns into a gas and then back to a solid, used here to describe the purification and "spiritualization" of the metal.</note>. 

Whereby both, along with a portion of our water, are made into spirits climbing into the air, and there the body—composed of male and female, of the Sun and Moon <note>original: "Sole & Luna"; symbolic terms for Gold and Silver, or the active and passive principles.</note>, and from that most subtle nature cleansed by sublimation—receives life. It is inspired by its own moisture—that is, by its own water—just as a human is by the air; wherefore it shall multiply thereafter and grow in its own kind, like all other things. 

In such an elevation and philosophical sublimation, therefore, all are joined to one another, and the new body, inspired by the air, lives vegetably <note>Meaning it now possesses the power of growth and reproduction, like a plant.</note>, which is miraculous. For unless the bodies are thinned by fire and water until they ascend as spirits, and until they become like water and smoke, or Mercury, nothing is achieved in the Art. Yet as they <margin><unclear>curru</unclear></margin> ascend, they are born in the air and are turned in the air, and they become life with life, so that they can never be separated, just like water mixed with water. And therefore it is wisely said to be "born in the air," since it is made entirely spiritual.

For that very **Wingless Vulture** <note>original: "Vultur sine alis"; a famous alchemical emblem representing the fixed part of the matter that has been made volatile or "spiritual."</note>, flying above the mountain, cries out saying: "I am the white of the black, and the red of the white, and the yellow son of the red; speaking the truth, I do not lie."

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the alchemical process. It is enough for you, therefore, to put the body into your water in the vessel once, and then to close it well, until the separation is made—which is called by the envious <note>The "envious" (French: *enuieux*) refers to previous alchemical authors who deliberately obscured their meaning to prevent the unworthy from succeeding.</note> conjunction, sublimation, extraction, putrefaction, ligation, marriage, subtilization, generation, etc.—and until the whole <term>magistery</term> <note>The "magistery" refers to the completed alchemical process or the Great Work.</note> is perfect. Act, then, just as in the generation of man and of all vegetables: place the seed only once into the matrix, and then close it well. 

You see by this means how we have no need for many things, and that our work requires no great expense, because there is only one stone, one medicine, one vessel, one regime, and one successive disposition, for both the white and the red. And although we say in several places, "take this, take that," nevertheless we do not mean that anything but one thing must be taken, which must be placed [in the vessel] only once, and then close the vessel until the work is finished. For the envious Philosophers suggest taking these various things in order to lead the ignorant and the unwise astray, as has already been said. 

Is this art not also Cabalistic <note>Referring to the Kabbalah, implying the art is encoded with hidden, mystical meanings.</note> and full of the greatest secrets? And you, fool, do you believe that we clearly teach the "secrets of secrets"? And do you take the words according to the sound of the speech? Know for certain (for I am not at all envious like the others): any person who takes the words of the Philosophers according to their vulgar meaning and ordinary definitions, that person having already lost the thread of Ariadne <note>In Greek mythology, Ariadne’s thread helped Theseus navigate the Labyrinth; here it means the logic or key to the alchemical puzzle.</note> amidst the turnings of the labyrinth, wanders most greatly and has destined his money to ruin. 

And I myself, **ARTEPHIUS**, after I had learned all the art in the books of the true **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy.</note>, I was also like the other envious ones. But since I have seen through the space of a thousand years, or nearly so (which thousand years have already passed over me since the time of my birth, by the grace of the only Almighty God and the use of this admirable <term>quintessence</term> <note>The "fifth essence," the purest and most concentrated form of matter sought by alchemists.</note>), and since I have seen in this long space of time that no other has perfected the <term>magistery</term> of Hermes because of the obscurity of the words of the Philosophers, I—moved by piety and the integrity of an honest man—have resolved in these final days of my life to write everything sincerely and truly. 

I do this so that nothing remains to be desired for performing the work, except for one certain thing, which it is not lawful for any person to say or write, because it is always revealed by God or by a master. Yet even this can be easily learned in this book, provided one is not too dull-witted and has a little experience. I have therefore written the truth nakedly in this book, though clothing it nonetheless in a few small rags, so that every good and wise man may happily gather from this <term>philosophical tree</term> the admirable apples of the Hesperides <note>In Greek myth, these were golden apples that granted immortality; here they represent the Alchemical Gold or the Elixir of Life.</note>. And therefore, praised be God most high, who has placed this kindness in our soul, and with a very long old age, has given us true love of heart, by which it seems to me that I embrace, cherish, and truly love all [those...]

men. But let us return to the art. Truly our work is accomplished quickly: For what the heat of the Sun does in a hundred years in the mines of the earth for the generation of a single metal (as I have often seen), our secret fire—that is to say, our fiery, sulfurous water, which is called the **Bain-Marie** <note>original: "Bain Marie"; literally "Mary’s Bath," a double-boiler used to provide gentle, even heat.</note>—does in a short time.

And this work is not a great labor for one who understands and knows it; indeed, its matter is not so expensive (seeing that a small quantity suffices) that it should cause anyone to withdraw their hand from it, because it is so brief and so easy that it is rightly called **women’s work and children’s play.** <note>A common alchemical phrase suggesting that once the initial secret is discovered, the remaining process requires little effort other than patience.</note> Work then courageously, my son, pray to God, read the books assiduously, for one book opens another; think upon it deeply, and flee from those things that follow and vanish in the fire. For your intention must not be in combustible and scorched things, but only in the **coction** <term>coction</term> <note>A process of continued heating or "cooking" to bring a substance to maturity.</note> of your water extracted from your **luminaries.** <note>The "luminaries" refer to the Sun (gold) and the Moon (silver).</note> For by this water, color and weight are given even unto infinity; it is a white smoke which descends into the perfect bodies like a soul, entirely removing their blackness and impurity, consolidating the two bodies into one, and multiplying their water. And there is nothing else that can take from the perfect bodies—that is to say, the **Sun and the Moon**—their true color except **Azoth,** <term>Azoth</term> <note>The "universal solvent" or essential mercurial spirit of the work.</note> that is to say, this water which colors and makes the red body white according to the regimes.

But let us treat of the fires. Our fire is mineral, equal, continual; it does not vaporize unless it is too greatly excited; it participates in sulfur, is taken from elsewhere than the matter; it breaks everything, dissolves, congeals, and calcines; it is artificial to find, and is an expense without cost (or at least not very great). It is also humid, vaporous, digesting, altering, penetrating, subtle, aerial, non-violent, without burning, surrounding and environing, containing, and unique. It is the **fountain of living water** which surrounds and contains the place where the **King and the Queen** <note>Alchemical symbols for the masculine (gold/sulfur) and feminine (silver/mercury) principles.</note> bathe. In all the work, this humid fire suffices you at the beginning, the middle, and the end. For in this consists the whole art: it is a fire natural, against nature, unnatural, and without burning; and finally, this fire is hot, dry, humid, and cold. Think upon this, and work rightly, taking no foreign natures. For if you do not understand these fires, listen well to this: I give you the most abstruse and occult <term>cavillation</term> <note>A sophisticated or deceptive argument; here, the author is explaining a riddle of the ancient philosophers.</note> of the ancient Philosophers, which has never yet been written in books until now.

We have, properly, three fires, without which the art cannot be perfected; and he who works without them takes much care in vain. The first is that of the lamp, which is continuous, humid, vaporous, aerial, and artificial to find: For the lamp...

men (as it seems to me) I embrace, cherish, and truly love. <margin>24</margin>
And this work is not of heavy labor to him who knows and understands, and its material is not so expensive (since a small quantity suffices) that anyone could be excused from holding back their hand from the work; for it is so brief and easy that it is rightly called **women's work and children's play**. <note>A common alchemical aphorism suggesting that while the theory is difficult, the physical process is simple once the secret is known.</note> Go on then diligently, my son; pray to God, read books assiduously—for one book opens another—think deeply, and flee from things that vanish in the fire, because your intention must not be in these combustible things, but only in the **coction** <term>coction</term> <note>From the Latin "coctio," meaning a process of ripening or "cooking" through heat to transform a substance.</note> of your water extracted from the **luminaries.** <note>The "luminaries" are the Sun (gold) and the Moon (silver).</note> For from this water, color and weight are brought forth even unto infinity; and this water is a white smoke, which flows into perfect bodies like a soul, and completely takes away their blackness and impurity, and consolidates the bodies into one, and multiplies their water. There is nothing which can take away color from perfect bodies—that is, from the Sun and Moon—except **Azoth** <note>A central term in alchemy representing the essential agent of transformation or the "Universal Solvent."</note>, which is our water that colors, and renders the red body white according to its regimens.

<margin>25</margin>
But let us speak of fires. Our fire is mineral, it is equal, it is continuous, and it does not vaporize unless it is too greatly excited. It participates in sulfur and is taken from elsewhere than from the matter; it destroys all things, dissolves, congeals, and calcines. It is artificial to find, and a shortcut without expense—or at least very little. It is also moist, vaporous, digesting, altering, penetrating, subtle, airy, not violent, non-burning, surrounding, containing, and unique. It is the fountain of living water which circles and contains the place of the washing of the **King and Queen** <note>Alchemical symbols for the male and female principles, usually Gold (Sulfur) and Silver (Mercury).</note>. In the whole work this moist fire suffices for you—in the beginning, middle, and end—because in it the whole art consists. It is a natural fire, yet against nature, unnatural, and without burning; and as a corollary, it is a fire that is hot, dry, moist, and cold. Think upon these things, and act rightly without any foreign nature. But if you do not understand these fires, hear these things from the more abstruse and hidden **riddles** <note>original: "cavillatione"; literally a sophistry or mockery, referring here to the intentionally confusing language used by ancient alchemists to hide their secrets.</note> of the ancients regarding fires, never written in books until now.

<margin>26</margin>
We have properly three fires, without which the art is not perfected, and he who labors without them takes up cares in vain. The first is that of the lamp, and it is continuous, moist, vaporous, airy, and artificial to find...
