<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the alchemical process, shifting from the general ease of the work to the precise management of the three types of "fire" and the sequence of colors that signal success.</meta>

<page-num>30</page-num>

The lamp must be proportioned to the enclosure, and in this lamp one must use great judgment—something that never reaches the understanding of the dull-witted—because if the fire of the lamp is not geometrically and congruently adapted to the furnace, then either through a lack of heat you will not see the signs expected in their time (and thus lose hope through too long a wait), or else, if it is too vehement, you will burn the flowers of gold and sadly lament your labors. 

The second fire is that of the ashes in which the hermetically sealed vessel remains seated; or rather, it is that very gentle heat which surrounds the vessel, arising from the temperate vapor of the lamp. This fire here is not violent unless it is over-excited; it is digestive and altering, it is taken from somewhere other than the matter itself, it is unique, it is also humid, and so forth. 

The third is the natural fire of our water, which for this reason is called **fire against nature** <note>original: "feu contre nature"; a translation of the Latin *ignis contra naturam*. It refers to a chemical solvent that acts like fire by dissolving and "calcining" substances, even though it is a liquid.</note> because it is water, and yet it causes gold to become a true spirit—something which common fire could not do. This one is mineral, equal, participates in sulfur, breaks, congeals, dissolves, and calcines everything; it is penetrating, subtle, and non-burning. It is the **fountain in which the King and the Queen wash themselves** <note>In alchemical symbolism, the King (Gold/Sulfur) and Queen (Silver/Mercury) must be purified and united; this "washing" represents the dissolution of the metals into a single substance.</note>, which we have need of always: at the beginning, the middle, and at the end. Of the other two aforementioned fires, we do not need them always, but only sometimes, etc. Therefore, when reading the books of the Philosophers, join these three kinds of fires together, and without a doubt, you will understand all the deceptions regarding their fires.

As for the colors: He who does not blacken cannot whiten, because blackness is the beginning of whiteness, the sign of **putrefaction** <term>putrefaction</term> <note>The "Nigredo" or black stage, where the matter is broken down and "dies" before being reborn.</note> and alteration, and a sign that the body is already penetrated and mortified. Therefore, during the putrefaction in this water, there will first appear to you a blackness similar to a **bloody, peppery broth**. <note>A traditional description of the dark, grainy, and bubbling appearance of the matter during the early stages of heating.</note> 

Then, afterwards, the black earth will whiten through continual **decoction** <term>decoction</term> <note>A process of heating or boiling a substance in a liquid to extract its essence or transform it.</note>, for the soul of the two bodies floats upon the water like white cream; and in this whiteness alone all the spirits unite, so that thereafter they cannot flee from one another. And therefore one must **whiten the latten** <term>latten</term> <note>original: "leton"; a brass-like alloy. In alchemy, "whitening the latten" refers to purifying the base, dark matter until it shines like silver.</note> and "break the books" <note>An idiom suggesting that one should move past literal reading to find the practical truth, or perhaps that the truth is so powerful it shatters previous scholarly assumptions.</note> so that our hearts do not break; because this entire whiteness is the true **White Stone**, the noble body necessitated by its end, and the tincture of whiteness of a most exuberant reflection, which does not flee when mixed with a body. 

Note here, then, that the spirits are not fixed except in the color of whiteness, which consequently is more noble than the other colors, and ought to be more desirably awaited, seeing as it is almost the entire accomplishment of the work. For our earth is first purified in blackness, then it cleanses itself in the elevation; afterwards it dries out and the blackness departs, and then it whitens, and the dark, humid empire of the woman perishes. Then also the white smoke penetrates into the new body, and the...

Regarding the colors: he who does not make things black cannot make them white, because blackness is the beginning of whiteness and the sign of **putrefaction** <term>Putrefaction: the chemical and symbolic "death" of the matter, where it decomposes into a dark mass to allow for rebirth.</term> and alteration; it shows that the body has now been penetrated and "mortified." 

Therefore, in this putrefaction within this water, blackness first appears like a thick, peppery broth; secondly, through continuous boiling, this black earth is made white, because the soul of these things floats on top like a white cream. In this whiteness, all the spirits are united so that they can no longer flee; for this reason, the **laton** <note>original: *laton*; refers to the base metallic matter or "unclean" gold that must be purified until it shines like silver.</note> must be made white. 

"The books should be torn up so that our hearts are not torn" <note>A famous alchemical aphorism suggesting that once the stage of Whiteness is reached, the truth is so clear that the student no longer needs to struggle with the confusing metaphors of old texts.</note>, because this whiteness is the perfect stone for the white work—a noble body by the necessity of its end, and a most exuberant **tincture** <term>Tincture: a concentrated medicinal or alchemical substance capable of "dyeing" or transforming other bodies.</term> of whiteness, of reflection, and of shining splendor, which does not depart from the mixed body.

Note here, then, that spirits are not fixed except in the white color, which is therefore more noble than the others and always to be sought with desire, since it is in a way the completion of the whole work. For our earth putrefies into black, then is cleansed in its "elevation" <note>likely referring to sublimation, where the matter is vaporized and condensed to purify it.</note>; afterward, once dried, the blackness recedes, and then it is whitened. At this point, the dark, humid dominion of the **woman** <note>In alchemical allegory, the "woman" often represents the volatile, moist principle (mercury), which must be "killed" or dried out by the "man" (sulfur) to achieve stability.</note> perishes; then also, a white smoke penetrates into the new body, and the spirit...

It emphasizes that the white stage is the first true completion of the stone, where the volatile spirits are finally "fixed" or stabilized.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the alchemical stages, moving from the state of blackness (Nigredo) described on the previous page to the subsequent colors of the Great Work: whiteness, yellowing, and redness. spirits are tightened in its dryness, and the corrupted, deformed, and black [matter] caused by humidity vanishes; then also the new body rises again, clear, white, and immortal, carrying the victory over all its enemies. And as heat acting upon the moist engenders blackness, which is the first color, likewise by continuous cooking, heat acting upon the dry engenders whiteness, which is the second color, and afterwards engenders **citrinity** <note>original: *citrinité*; a yellow or lemon-gold stage that follows the white stage</note> and redness acting upon the pure dry; so much for the colors.

We must therefore know that the thing which has a red and white head, white and then red feet, and previously black eyes—that this single thing is our **magistery** <term>Magistery: from the Latin *magisterium*, referring to the completed alchemical Work or the Philosopher's Stone itself.</term>. Let us speak then of the **Sun** <note>Gold or the masculine principle of Sulfur</note> and the **Moon** <note>Silver or the feminine principle of Mercury</note> in our dissolving water, which is familiar and friendly to them, and of their close nature; which is sweet to them, and like a womb, mother, origin, beginning, and end of life. This is the reason they find improvement in this water, because nature rejoices with nature, and nature contains nature and is joined with it in true marriage, and they become a single nature, a new body, resurrected and immortal. And thus one must join those of the same blood with those of the same blood; then these natures follow one another, putrefy, engender, and rejoice, because nature is governed by its close and friendly nature. 

Our water then (says <term>Danthin</term> <note>Likely a reference to an alchemical authority or a corruption of a name like "Dastin" or "Dantinus."</note>) is the beautiful, pleasant, and clear fountain, prepared only for the King and the Queen, whom it knows very well, and they know it; for it attracts them to itself, and they remain in it to wash for two or three days—that is to say, two or three months—and it makes them young again, and makes them beautiful. And because the Sun and the Moon have their origin from this water, their mother, they must therefore enter again into their mother's womb in order to be born anew, and so that they may become more robust, more noble, and stronger. And therefore, if these do not die and convert into water, they will remain all alone and without fruit; but if they die and resolve into our water, they will bring forth a hundredfold fruit, and from the place where it seemed they had lost what they were, from that same place they will appear as what they were not before. 

Therefore, with the Sun and the Moon, fix the spirit of our living water with very great subtlety. For these being converted into the nature of water, they die and become like the dead; however, from there, afterwards being inspired, they live, grow, and multiply like all other vegetable things. It is enough for you, then, to sufficiently arrange the matter externally, for it works sufficiently toward its perfection in its interior. For nature has within itself a certain inherent movement, and according to the true way, better than any order that could be imagined by man. Therefore, you only prepare, and nature will complete it. For if it is not hinder-

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the alchemical process, moving from the unification of opposites to the final stages of the Great Work. It explains how to achieve the final red color (Rubedo) and how to "multiply" the power of the resulting Stone through repeated cycles of dissolution and coagulation.</meta>

<page-num>34</page-num>

...checked by the opposite; it will not pass its movement until it is certain, both for conceiving and for giving birth. Therefore, take care only after the preparation of the matter—namely, that you do not overheat the bath. And lastly, do not let the spirits escape: for they would afflict the one who works—that is to say, the operation would be destroyed—and they would give the Philosopher many infirmities, meaning sadness and anger. From the above is drawn this axiom: namely, that by the course of nature, he is ignorant of the construction of metals who is ignorant of their destruction. Therefore, you must join the parents, for like natures find like natures, and in purifying themselves they mingle together, indeed they mortify and revive. It is therefore necessary to know this corruption and generation, and how the natures embrace and are pacified by a slow fire, how nature rejoices in nature, how nature retains nature, and converts it into a white nature. After this, if you wish to rubify <note>original: *rubifier*; to turn the matter red, the final stage of the work</note>, you must cook this white in a continual dry fire until it reddens like blood, which then will be nothing other than fire and true tincture. And thus by the continual dry fire, the whiteness is changed, corrected, and perfected; it turns yellow <note>original: *se citrinise*; refers to the yellowing stage called Citrinitas</note>, and acquires redness and a true fixed color. Therefore, the more the red is cooked, the more it is colored, and becomes a tincture of more perfect redness. Consequently, one must—by a dry fire and by a dry **calcination** <term>Calcination: heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off moisture or impurities, reducing it to a powder.</term> without moisture—cook the compound until it is clothed in a deep red color, and becomes the perfect **Elixir.**

If afterwards you wish to multiply it, you must once again resolve <note>original: *resoudre*; to dissolve or break down</note> this red into new dissolving water, and then once again by **decoction** <term>Decoction: the process of boiling or heating a substance in a liquid to extract its essence.</term> whiten it and rubify it through the degrees of fire, reiterating the first regimen. Dissolve, congeal, reiterate, closing the door, opening it, and multiplying in quantity and quality at your will. For by new corruption and generation, a new movement is introduced once more, and thus we could never find an end if we always wished to work by the reiteration of solution and coagulation, by means of our dissolving water—that is, dissolving and congealing as was said for the first regimen.

And thus its virtue increases and multiplies in quantity and quality, so that if in your first work one part of your stone tinged <note>original: *teignoit*; dyed or transmuted</note> a hundred, the second time it will tinge a thousand, the third ten thousand, and so if you continue, your **projection** <term>Projection: the act of casting the finished Philosopher's Stone upon base metals to transform them into silver or gold.</term> will reach toward infinity, truly, perfectly, and fixedly tinging whatever quantity it may be; and thus, by a thing of low price, color, virtue, and weight are added. Therefore our fire and **Azoth** <term>Azoth: the "universal medicine" or essential agent of transformation, often equated with the spirit of mercury.</term> are sufficient for you; cook, cook, reiterate, dissolve, congeal, continuing thus at your will, and multiplying

unless nature is hindered to the contrary, it will not pass its fixed movement, both for conceiving and for giving birth. Therefore, take care only <insert>after the preparation of the matter</insert> that the bath is not overheated by too much fire; secondly, that the spirit does not exhale <note>original: *exhalet*; meaning to evaporate or escape the vessel</note>, because it would harm the one laboring—that is, it would destroy the operation—and it would bring many infirmities, meaning sadness and anger. From what has been said, this axiom is clear: namely, that by the course of nature, he who is ignorant of destruction necessarily ignores the construction of metals. Therefore, it is necessary to join the kindred, because natures find their similar natures, and by putrefying <note>the process of breakdown or fermentation in the vessel</note> themselves they are mixed together, and they mortify <note>the symbolic "death" of the matter before its rebirth</note> themselves. It is therefore necessary to know this corruption and generation, and how the natures embrace each other and are pacified in a slow fire—how nature rejoices in nature, how nature retains nature, and converts it into a white nature. But if you wish to turn it red <note>original: *rubificare*; the final stage of the Great Work, creating the Red Stone</note>, you must cook this white substance in a continuous dry fire until it is turned red like blood, which then will be nothing other than fire and true tincture; and thus by continuous dry fire the whiteness is improved, turned yellow <note>original: *citrinatur*; the stage of Citrinitas</note>, and acquires redness and a true fixed color. Therefore, the more it is cooked, the more it is colored, and it becomes a tincture of a more intense redness. Wherefore it is necessary to cook the compound with dry fire and dry calcination <note>reducing a solid to powder through heat</note>, without moisture, until it is clothed in a most red color, and then it will be the perfect Elixir.

<margin>30</margin>
If afterwards you wish to multiply it, it is necessary to repeatedly resolve that red substance in new dissolving water, and to whiten it again by cooking, and to turn it red through the stages of fire, repeating the first regimen: Dissolve, congeal, repeat, closing, opening, and multiplying in quantity and quality at your pleasure. For through new corruption and generation, a new movement is introduced again; thus we could not reach the end if we always wanted to work through the repetition of dissolution and coagulation by means of our dissolving water—that is, by dissolving and congealing, as was said through the first regimen. And thus its power is augmented and multiplied in quantity and quality, so that if in the first work it received a hundred <note>referring to the ratio of the Stone's power to transmute base metal</note>, in the second you will have a thousand, in the third ten thousand, and so by proceeding, your projection <note>the act of casting the Stone onto base metal to transform it</note> will reach toward infinity, dyeing truly and perfectly, and fixedly, any quantity whatever; and thus through a thing of cheap price, color, power, and weight are added. Therefore our fire and <term>Azoth</term> <note>The essential transformative agent, often associated with Mercury</note> are sufficient for you; cook, cook, repeat, dissolve, congeal, and thus continue, multiplying at your pleasure...

as much as you wish, until your medicine is fusible like wax, and it has the quantity and the virtue that you desire. Therefore, the entire accomplishment of the work, or of our second stone <note>The "second stone" often refers to the Elixir after its first rotation or a specific stage of refinement in the process.</note> (note this well), consists in you taking the perfect body, which you will place in our water within a glass house <note>A "glass house" is a common alchemical term for the hermetic flask or cucurbit.</note> well closed and sealed with cement, so that the air does not enter, and so the enclosed moisture does not escape; which you will keep in the digestion of a gentle, slow, and very temperate heat, similar to that of a bath or manure <note>Alchemists often used "horse dung" (fimus equinus) or a "water bath" (balneum Mariae) to provide the steady, low heat required for the breakdown of matter.</note>, upon which, with the fire, you will continue the perfection of the decoction until it putrefies and is resolved into a black color, and then rises and sublimes through the water, so that by this it may be cleansed of all blackness and darkness, becoming white and subtle, until it reaches the ultimate purity of sublimation, and becomes volatile, and white inside and out. Then continue your suitable fire, and this spirit—that is to say, this subtle substance of the body and of the Mercury—will rise above the water; this quintessence <term>quintessence: the "fifth essence," the purest and most concentrated part of a substance</term> is whiter than snow; continue further, finally strengthening the fire until all that is spiritual rises to the top. It is necessary then (as the Sibyl <note>Referring to the Sibylline Oracles, which were often cited by alchemists to provide ancient, prophetic authority to their recipes.</note> said) that from its earth the son of the Virgin be exalted, and that the white quintessence after its resurrection rises toward the heavens, and that at the bottom of the vessel and the water remains the coarse and the thick; for afterward, once the vessel has cooled, you will find at the bottom the black, parched, and burnt feces, separated from the spirit and the white quintessence, which you must throw away. At this time, the quicksilver rains from our air onto our new earth, which is called quicksilver sublimed by the air, from which is made the viscous, clear, and white water, which is the true tincture separated from all black feces; and thus our latten <term>laton: a base metal alloy, here representing the impure matter that must be "whitened" into silver</term> is governed with our water, purifying itself and adorning itself with a white color, which color is only produced by the decoction and coagulation of the water. Cook then continually, remove the blackness of the latten—not with your hand, but with the stone, or the fire, or with our second Mercurial water which is a true tincture. For this separation of the pure from the impure is not done with hands, since it is nature alone that truly perfects it, working circularly toward perfection. Thus it appears that this composition is not manual labor, but only a change of nature. Because nature itself dissolves and joins, sublimes, rises, and whitens, having separated the feces. Finally, it becomes volatile and is rendered white both inside and out, because the Vulture flying in the air without wings <note>An image representing the solid matter (the fixed) becoming a vapor (the volatile) within the sealed flask.</note> cries out that it may go above the mountain—that is, over the water—upon which the white spirit is carried. Then continue with a suitable fire, and that spirit—that is to say, the subtle substance of the body and of the Mercury—will ascend above the water. This is the fifth essence <note>original: "quinta essentia"; the purest, most refined state of matter.</note>, which is whiter than snow. In the end, continue further and strengthen the fire so that the whole spiritual portion may ascend completely: for know that what is clear, pure, and spiritual ascends high into the air in the manner of white smoke, which is called the Virgin’s Milk <note>original: "lac Virginis"; a common name for the white, milky mercury produced during this stage.</note>.

<margin>
32
</margin>
Therefore it is necessary [as the Sibyl said <note>Alchemists often cited the Sibyls—ancient prophetesses—to lend divine or ancient authority to their chemical descriptions.</note>] that the son of the Virgin be exalted from the earth, and the white fifth substance, after the resurrection, be raised toward the heavens; and in the bottom of the vessel and the water, let the coarse and thick matter remain. Once the vessel is cooled, you will find at its bottom black, scorched, and burnt dregs <note>original: "faeces"</note>, separated from the spirit and the white fifth essence; these you should cast away. In these times, quicksilver <note>original: "argentum viuum"</note> rains from our air upon the new earth, which is called quicksilver sublimated from the air. From this is made a viscous, clean, and white water, which is the true tincture <note>The "tincture" is the concentrated essence that has the power to transform other substances.</note> separated from all black dross. Thus our brass <note>original: "aes"; here referring to the base matter being transformed.</note> is governed with our water, purified, and adorned with a white color. This whitening <note>original: "dealbatio"</note> does not happen except by cooking and the coagulation of the water. Therefore, cook continuously; wash the blackness from the latten <note>original: "latone"; a brass-like alloy, used here to represent the impure metal during the middle stage of the work.</note>—not with the hand, but with the stone, or with fire, or with our second Mercurial water, which is the true tincture. For this separation

<margin>
33
</margin>
of the pure from the impure is not done with hands, but by nature itself alone, working circularly toward perfection, it is truly perfected. Therefore it is clear that this composition is not a manual operation, but a transformation of natures, because nature dissolves and joins itself, sublimates and raises itself, and whitens itself, once the dregs are separated. And in such a sublimation, the more subtle parts are joined more...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the whitening stage (Albedo) of the alchemical work. It elaborates on the necessity of "sublimation"—the process of vaporizing and re-condensing the matter—to separate the pure spiritual essence from the coarse earthly dregs.</meta>

<page-num>38</page-num>

...pure and essential, especially since when the fiery nature raises the most subtle parts, it always raises the purest and, consequently, leaves behind the coarsest. Therefore, by a moderate, continual fire, one must sublimate into vapor so that the stone <note>The "Stone" here refers to the alchemical matter in the flask, not yet the final Philosophers' Stone.</note> inspires in the air <note>original: "s'inspire en l'air"; literally "breathes in the air."</note> and can live. For the nature of all things takes life from the inspiration of air, and thus all our <term>magistery|magistère; the master-work or central alchemical process</term> consists in the vapor and sublimation of water.

We must therefore raise our <term>leton|latten; an alchemical term for the base metal or "body" before it is purified and whitened</term> by degrees of fire, so that it rises upward freely of its own accord, without violence. Consequently, if the body is not thinned and refined by fire and water until it rises like a spirit, or like fleeing quicksilver <note>Mercury</note>, or like the white soul separated from the body and carried away in the sublimation of spirits, nothing is achieved in this art. However, as it rises thus on high, it is born in the air and changes into air, making life with life, being entirely spiritual and incorruptible. And thus by such a regimen, the body becomes a spirit of a subtle nature, and the spirit incorporates with the body and becomes one with it. And in this sublimation, conjunction, and elevation, all things become white.

Therefore, this philosophical and natural sublimation is necessary, which establishes peace between the body and the spirit; this cannot be done otherwise than by this separation of parts. That is why it is necessary to sublimate both, so that the pure may rise, and the impure and earthly may descend into the turmoil and tempest of the fluctuating sea <note>A metaphor for the chaotic, boiling state of the chemicals during the heating process.</note>. Therefore, one must cook continually so that the matter becomes subtle in nature, and that the body attracts to itself the white <term>mercurial|related to the "spirit" or volatile aspect of the matter</term> soul, which it naturally retains and does not allow to be separated from itself, because it is equal to it in proximity of its primary nature—pure and simple. 

It is clear from this that one must perform the separation by <term>decoction|long-term heating or boiling in a closed vessel</term> until nothing remains of the fatness of the soul <note>The "fatness" refers to the flammable or oily impurities</note> that has not been raised and exalted into the upper part; for thus the two shall be reduced to a simple equality and simple whiteness. Therefore, the Vulture flying through the air and the Toad walking on the earth is our magistery. <note>These two animals represent the two states of matter: the Vulture is the volatile (spirit/gas) and the Toad is the fixed (body/solid).</note>

Therefore, when you gently and with great spirit separate the earth from the water—that is, from the fire—and the subtle from the thick, that which is pure shall rise from the earth to Heaven, and that which is impure shall descend into the earth; the most subtle part shall take on the nature of the spirit above, and the nature of the earthly body below. And thus, by this operation, raise the white nature with the most subtle part of the body, leaving the feces <note>The solid waste or sediment remaining at the bottom</note> behind, which is done quickly; for the soul is aided by its associate and is perfected by it. 

"My mother," says the body, "has brought me forth, and through me she is brought forth herself." <note>A classic alchemical paradox describing how the matter and its spirit act upon and transform one another in a cycle.</note> However, after she has taken flight, she is as full of piety as one could desire, cherishing and nourishing her son whom she has brought forth until he has reached the perfect state. pure and essential; because the fiery nature, when it raises the subtler parts, always raises the purer ones, and therefore leaves the coarser ones behind. Therefore, it is necessary to sublimate <note>Sublimation in alchemy is the process of heating a substance until it vaporizes, then collecting the condensed "spirit" at the top of the vessel.</note> continually into vapor with a moderate fire, so that it may be inspired by the air and be able to live. For the nature of all things receives life from the inspiration of air; thus also our entire <term>magistery|magisterium; the mastery or the Great Work of alchemy</term> consists in vapor and the sublimation of water.

Therefore our copper <note>original: "æs"; likely referring to the "latten" or base material mentioned on the previous page</note> must be raised through degrees of fire, and let it ascend freely by itself without violence; and so, unless the body is broken down by fire and water, and thinned until it ascends like a spirit, or like climbing quicksilver <note>Mercury</note>, or even like the white soul separated from the body and carried away in the sublimation of spirits, nothing happens. Wherefore let the white nature be raised through such an operation with the subtler part of the body, leaving the dregs behind, which happens in a short time.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the whitening stage (Albedo), focusing on the internal mechanics of how the "Mercurial water" interacts with the solid body to achieve a permanent union. It bridges the concepts of sublimation and the onset of putrefaction.</meta>

our <term>Mercurial water|A philosophical solvent, not common mercury, used to dissolve and "wash" the metallic body</term>, until it rises on high with the **white soul**, and the terrestrial part descends to the bottom, which is called the **remaining earth**. Then you shall see the water coagulate with its body, and you will be assured that the science is true, because the body coagulates its moisture into dryness, just as the <note>original: "lait caillé de l’agneau"; referring to rennet used in cheesemaking</note> curd of a lamb coagulates milk into cheese. In this way, the spirit will penetrate the body, and the mixture will be perfectly made; the body will draw its moisture to itself—that is to say, its **white soul**—just as a magnet attracts iron because of the similarity and proximity of their natures and its own greed. Then one shall contain the other, and this is our **sublimation** and **coagulation**, which retains every volatile thing and ensures there is no more escape.

Therefore, this composition is not at all an operation of the hands, but (as I have said) it is a change of natures and an admirable connection and binding of the cold with the hot, and the moist with the dry. For the hot mixes with the cold, and the dry with the moist; thus, by this means, the mixture and conjunction of the body and the spirit is achieved, which is called the **conversion of contrary natures**. For in such solution and sublimation, the spirit is converted into body, and the body into spirit; thus, being mixed together and reduced into one, the natures change one another, because the body incorporates the spirit, and the spirit changes the body into a tinted and white spirit.

And therefore (and here is the last time I shall say it) decoct it in our **white water**—that is to say, in Mercury—until it is dissolved into blackness. Then afterward, by continual decoction, its blackness will be lost, and the body thus dissolved at the last shall rise with the **white soul**. Then the one will mix into the other, and they will embrace each other in such a way that they can never again be separated. Then, with a royal accord, the spirit unites with the body, and they become permanent; this is the **solution of the body** and **coagulation of the spirit**, which are one and the same operation. He who knows how to marry, to make pregnant, to mortify, to putrefy, to engender, and to vivify the species; to give the white light and to clean the <term>Vulture|An alchemical symbol for the substance in its initial, "devouring" or black state</term> of its blackness and darkness until it is purged by fire, colored, and purified of all stains, he shall be the possessor of so great a dignity that Kings will do him great honor.

And therefore, let our body remain in the water until it is dissolved into a new powder at the bottom of the vessel and the water, which is called **black ash**; and this is the **corruption of the body**, which by the Sages is called <term>Saturn|The planet and god associated with lead and the "blacker than black" stage of the work</term>, <term>latten|A base metal needing purification</term>, **Lead of the Philosophers**, and the **discontinued powder**. And in this putrefaction and resolution of the body, three signs will appear: namely, the black color, the discontinuity and separation of the parts, and the stinking odor, which is similar to that of graves. This ash, therefore, is that of the...

Then one will mix into the other, and they will embrace in such a way that they can never again be separated. Then, with a true harmony, the spirit unites with the body and they become permanent. This is the **dissolution of the body** and **coagulation of the spirit**, which are one and the same operation. He who knows how to marry, impregnate, mortify, putrefy, engender, and vivify the species—to give the **white light** and clean the <term>Vulture|An alchemical symbol for the volatile spirit or the corrosive solvent that "devours" the metal</term> of its blackness and darkness until it be purged by fire, colored, and purified of all stains—he will be the possessor of so great a dignity that Kings will do him great honor.

And therefore, let our body remain in the water until it be dissolved into a new powder at the bottom of the vessel and the water, which is called **black ash**. This is the **corruption of the body**, which by the Sages is called <term>Saturn|The planet and god associated with the densest metal, lead, and the stage of blackness</term>, <term>Leton|Also called latten; a term for the base metallic matter before it has been purified and whitened</term>, **Philosophers' Lead**, and the **discontinuous powder**. And in this <term>putrefaction|The stage of "rot" or breakdown where the matter turns black, a necessary step before purification</term> and resolution of the body, three signs will appear, namely: the black color, the discontinuity and separation of the parts, and the stinking odor, which is similar to that of graves. This ash, therefore, is that of...

<meta>catchword: neris [likely "veneris", referring to Venus or copper]</meta>

<summary>The author describes the "conversion of natures" where the spirit and body become permanently unified, followed by the transition into the Nigredo or "putrefaction" stage, characterized by blackness and a foul odor.</summary>
<keywords>Mercurial water, sublimation, coagulation, white soul, conversion of natures, blackness, Saturn, Leton, Philosophers' Lead, putrefaction</keywords>

--- Page 53 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the whitening stage (Albedo), picking up from the description of how the "Mercurial water" interacts with the solid body. It explains the "conversion of natures" where the spirit and body become inseparable, and then transitions into the stage of "putrefaction," where the matter turns black and is referred to by names such as "Saturn" or "Philosophers' Lead."</meta>

...will have been perfected. Listen to this secret: Keep the body in our **Mercurial water** <note>original: "aqua nostra Mercuriali"; a specialized solvent in alchemy, not literal mercury</note>, until it rises with the **white soul**, and the earthy part descends to the bottom, which is called the **remaining earth**. Then you will see the water coagulate itself with its body, and you will be certain that the science is true, because its body coagulates the moisture into a dry state, just as the **lamb's curd** <note>original: "coagulum agni"; referring to rennet used in cheesemaking</note> coagulates milk into cheese. In this way, the spirit will penetrate the body, and the mixture will be made at the most minute level. The body attracts its own moisture to itself—that is, the **white soul**—just as a **magnet** attracts iron because of the kinship of their natures and its greedy essence. Then one contains the other, and this is our **sublimation** and **coagulation**, which retains every volatile thing and ensures there is no more escape.

Therefore, this composition is not a manual operation <note>meaning it happens through natural chemical processes rather than physical stirring or grinding</note>, but 
<margin>36</margin>
(as I have said) it is a **mutation of natures**, and an admirable connection of their cold with the hot, and the moist with the dry. For the hot is mixed with the cold, and the dry with the moist; in this way also occurs the mixing and conjunction of the body and the spirit, which is called the **conversion of contrary natures**. For in such dissolution and sublimation, the spirit is converted into the body, and the body into the spirit; thus once mixed and reduced into one, they turn into each other. For the body incorporates the spirit, while the spirit turns the body into a **tinged and white spirit**.

Therefore, for the final time (I say), cook it in our **white water**, that is, in **Mercury** <term>Mercury: Here refers to the "Philosophical Mercury," the essential liquid principle of the Work</term>, until it is dissolved into blackness. Then, by continuous cooking, it will be deprived of its blackness, and the body thus dissolved will finally ascend with the **white soul**. Then one is mixed with the other, and they embrace each other so that they can no longer be separated from each other. Then, with real harmony, the spirit is united with the body, and they become one permanent thing. This is the **dissolution of the body** and the **coagulation of the spirit**, which are one and the same operation.

He, therefore, who knows how to lead, to make pregnant, to kill, to rot, to generate, to bring species to life, to induce the **white light**, and to cleanse the **Vulture** <note>original: "Vulturem"; an alchemical symbol for the volatile spirit being "fixed" or purified</note> from blackness and darkness until it is purged and colored by fire, and purified from the last stains, will be a possessor of such great dignity that Kings will honor him.

<margin>37</margin>
Therefore, let the body remain in the water until it is dissolved into a new powder at the bottom of the vessel and the water, which is called **black ash**. This is the **corruption of the body** which is called by the wise **Saturn**, **Bronze**, the **Lead of the philosophers**, and **Discontinuous Powder**. In such putrefaction and resolution of the body, three signs appear: namely, the **color black**, the **discontinuity of parts**, and a **foul odor** which is likened to the smell of graves.

of which the **Philosophers** <note>The "Philosophers" refers to the long lineage of alchemists whose works formed the basis of this tradition.</note> have spoken so much, which has remained in the lower part of the vessel, and which we must not despise; for in it is the **Diadem of our King** <note>A symbolic reference to the hidden potential for perfection within base matter.</note>, and the **quicksilver** <note>original: "argent vif"; mercury</note>, black and foul, from which one must remove the blackness by continually boiling it down in our water, until it rises up in a white color, which is called the **Goose and the Chicken of Hermogenes** <note>Hermogenes likely refers to Hermes Trismegistus; these birds are symbols for the volatile white spirit rising from the matter.</note>. Therefore, he who removes the blackness from the red earth, and then whitens it, possesses the **mastery** <term>magistery; the completion of a specific stage or the entire alchemical process</term>, just as he who kills the living and resurrects the dead. Whiten the black, then, and redden the white, so that you may complete the **work** <term>the Great Work or "Magnum Opus," the creation of the Philosopher's Stone</term>. And when you see the true whiteness appear, shining like a naked blade, know that the redness is hidden within it; then you must not take this white powder out of the vessel, but you must only continue to cook it, so that with **heat** <term>calidity</term> and **dryness** <term>siccity</term>, there finally occurs the **citrinity** <note>original: "citrinité"; the yellowing stage that bridges the white and red stages</note>, and the most sparkling redness. Seeing this with great **awe** <note>original: "terreur"; in this context, it implies a holy dread or overwhelming wonder at the divine mystery revealed.</note>, you will instantly praise the most good and most great God, who gives wisdom to those He wills, and consequently riches, and according to the iniquity of persons, takes them away and withdraws them perpetually, plunging them into the servitude of their enemies. To whom be praise and glory, **forever and ever** <note>original: "aux siecles des siecles"; translating the Latin "in saecula saeculorum"</note>. Amen.

Therefore, that ash of which the **philosophers** <note>The "philosophers" are the alchemical authorities like Geber or Rhazes often cited in these texts.</note> have spoken so much, which remained in the lower part of the vessel, and which we must not despise; for in it is the **King’s Diadem** <term>Diadema Regis; a symbolic name for the potential of the crown or the highest perfection within base matter</term>, and the **black quicksilver** <term>Argentum vivum; mercury, here described in its impure "black" state</term>, foul, from which the purification of the blackness must be made, by continually boiling it down in our water until it is raised upward into a white color, which is called the **Goose** and the **Chick of Hermogenes** <note>original: "Anser, & Pullus Hermogenis"; Hermogenes is often a pseudonym for Hermes Trismegistus. These birds represent the volatile spirits rising from the matter.</note>.

For he who blackens the red earth and makes it white possesses the **mastery** <term>magisterium; the complete skill and authority over the chemical transformation</term>, just as he who kills the living and brings the dead back to life. Whiten the black, therefore, and redden the white, so that you may complete the work: and when you see the true whiteness appear, which shines like a bared sword, know that the redness is hidden within that whiteness. From then on, it is not necessary to extract that whiteness, but only to cook it, so that with dryness and heat, there may follow the **yellowing** <note>original: "citrinitas"; the transitional stage between the white and red works</note> and the most brilliant **redness** <note>original: "rubedo"; the final stage of the Great Work</note>. When you see this, with the greatest trembling you shall praise God, the Best and Greatest, who gives wisdom to whom He wills—and consequently riches—and according to their iniquities, He snatches it away and removes it forever, thrusting them into the slavery of enemies; to whom be praise and glory forever and ever.
Amen.

To whom be praise and glory, forever and ever <note>original Latin: "aux siecles des siecles."</note>. Amen.

Therefore, that **ash** <note>original: "cinis"; in alchemy, the "ash" or "dead head" (*caput mortuum*) refers to the solid residue left after distillation or sublimation, which surprisingly contains the seed of the final transformation.</note> of which the **philosophers** <note>The "philosophers" refers to the long lineage of alchemical authorities, such as Hermes Trismegistus, whose secret knowledge the author is claiming to share.</note> have spoken so much, which remained in the lower part of the **vessel** <term>vasis; the "Philosophical Egg" or glass flask used in the Great Work</term>, must not be despised by us; for in it is the **King’s Diadem**, and the black, foul **quicksilver** <term>Argentum viuum; mercury, considered the "living silver" and the essential spirit of all metals</term>, from which the purification of the blackness must be performed by boiling it down <note>original: "decoquendo"</note> continually in our water, until it is raised upward into a white color, which is called the **Goose** and the **Chick of Hermogenes** <note>original: "Anser, & Pullus Hermogenis"; these bird metaphors describe the white vapors that rise or "fly" to the top of the flask during the stage of albedo or whitening.</note>. For he who turns the red earth black and restores it to white possesses the **mastery** <term>magisterium; the ultimate skill and secret of the alchemical art</term>, just as he who kills the living and brings the dead back to life.

Whiten the black, therefore, and redden the white, so that you may complete the work; and when you see the true whiteness appear, which shines like a bared sword, know that the redness is hidden within that whiteness. From then on, it is not necessary to extract that whiteness, but only to cook it, so that with dryness and heat, the **yellowing** <term>citrinitas; an intermediate stage between the white and the final red</term> and the most brilliant **redness** <term>rubedo; the final stage of the Great Work, signifying the completion of the Philosopher's Stone</term> may come upon it. When you see this, with the greatest trembling you shall praise God, the Best and Greatest, who gives wisdom to whom He wills, and consequently riches; and according to their iniquities, He snatches them away and forever removes them, thrusting them down into the servitude of enemies. To Him be praise and glory, forever and ever.

Amen.

# OF THE HIEROGLYPHIC

## FIGURES OF NICOLAS
## FLAMEL, SCRIBE, JUST AS THEY

->are in the fourth Arch of the Cemetery<-
->of the Innocents in Paris, entering by the gate on<-

<note>The "Hieroglyphic Figures" refer to a series of symbolic alchemical paintings commissioned by Flamel for the Cemetery of the Innocents, a major Parisian landmark until the late 18th century. "Scribe" or "Scrivener" was Flamel's historical profession.</note>

## TRANSLATED FROM LATIN INTO FRENCH
<note>original: "TRADVIT DE LATIN EN FRANCOIS"</note>

### by P. ARNAULD, *Lord of the Chevalierie,*
### *a Poitevin Gentleman.*
<note>A "Poitevin" is a person from the Poitou region of France.</note>

<sig>F iii</sig>

<summary>This is the title page for Nicolas Flamel's commentary on the alchemical "hieroglyphics" or symbolic figures located in the Cemetery of the Innocents in Paris. It identifies the work as a previously unpublished treatise on metallic transmutation, translated from Latin by P. Arnauld.</summary>

<keywords>Nicolas Flamel, Hieroglyphic Figures, Cemetery of the Innocents, Paris, alchemy, metallic transmutation, P. Arnauld, Poitou, Saint Denis Street</keywords>

--- Page 60 ---
<meta>This page follows the title page of Nicolas Flamel’s treatise. It contains a preface from the translator, Pierre Arnauld, explaining his choice of language and the difficulty of formatting a bilingual text with illustrations.</meta>

# TO THE READER,
## GREETINGS.

I would have (friend Reader) given these commentaries in Latin as well as in French, as I did with **<term>ARTEPHIVS</term>**<note>Artephius was a legendary figure in alchemy, often claimed to be over a thousand years old; Arnauld had previously published a translation of his "Secret Book."</note>, but because of the various figures that must often be represented, I was only able to provide them to you in one language. For it would have been clumsy to put the figures in both the Latin and French texts, or to put them in only one. And by putting them in only one, the figures occupying the space would have prevented the Latin and French from matching up well on the pages; I have therefore been forced to give them to you in this language only. Now, I have chosen the French, so that first of all all good Frenchmen may understand them freely, and thereby withdraw themselves from their errors and expenses <note>Alchemical experiments were notoriously expensive, and many practitioners went bankrupt pursuing false recipes; the author suggests Flamel's true method will save them from such ruin.</note>; and secondly, so that this book does not spread to foreign nations who are very inquisitive about it, compared to the French. But if I see that you take pleasure in it, I will also give them to you in Latin along with the *History of the Garden of the Hesperides* <note>In alchemy, the "Garden of the Hesperides" is a common allegory for the stages of the Great Work, with its golden apples representing the final goal.</note>, composed by **<term>Lorthulain</term>**<note>Usually identified as Ortolanus (the "Gardener"), a 14th-century alchemist famous for his commentary on the Emerald Tablet.</note>, a very serious and very learned Author, which work, along with these, I have recovered at great expense of money from very inquisitive hands, and who have until now preserved them as dearly as the stone itself <note>A reference to the Philosopher's Stone.</note>; also, these Authors here, above all others, are not **<term>envious</term>**<note>In alchemical literature, "envious" (from the Latin *invidus*) refers to authors who deliberately obscured their writings to hide secrets from the "unworthy." Arnauld is claiming these specific texts are unusually clear.</note>. Farewell.

<summary>The translator, P. Arnauld, explains that he published this edition only in French to ensure accessibility for his countrymen and because the inclusion of illustrations made a bilingual layout impractical. He promises a future Latin edition and praises the authors for being "not envious" with their secrets.</summary>

<keywords>preface, Pierre Arnauld, Artephius, Lorthulain, Ortolanus, Garden of the Hesperides, envy in alchemy, Philosopher's Stone, alchemical illustrations</keywords>

--- Page 61 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the translator’s preface by Pierre Arnauld to the primary text attributed to Nicolas Flamel himself. It begins with a devout prayer of thanksgiving before introducing the description of the famous alchemical allegories he supposedly commissioned for the Cemetery of the Innocents in Paris.</meta>

*Praised be eternally the Lord my God, who raises the humble from the low dust, and makes the hearts of those who hope in Him rejoice; Who graciously opens the springs of His bounty to the believers, and places under their feet the worldly circles of all earthly joys. In Him be always our hope; in His fear, our happiness; in His mercy, the glory of the restoration of our nature; and in prayer, our unshakable safety. And You, O Almighty God, as Your bounty has deigned to open before me (Your unworthy servant) all the treasures of the riches of the world upon the earth, may it please Your great mercy, when I am no longer among the living, to open for me also the treasures of Heaven, and let me contemplate Your divine face, whose Majesty is an unspeakable delight, and whose rapture has never entered the heart of a living man. I ask this of You, through the Lord Jesus Christ Your beloved Son, who in the Unity of the Holy Spirit lives with You forever and ever <note>original: "au siecle des siecles," a French rendering of the Latin liturgical conclusion "in saecula saeculorum."</note>. Amen.*

# THE EXPLANATION OF THE FIGURES
## **<term>Hieroglyphics</term>**<note>In this context, "hieroglyphics" does not refer to Egyptian writing, but to symbolic, allegorical images that hide secret alchemical meanings from the uninitiated.</note> placed by me, NICOLAS FLAMEL, Scrivener, in the **<term>Cemetery of the Innocents</term>**<note>A major Parisian cemetery used from the Middle Ages until the late 18th century; it was famous for its "charnel houses" and the "Dance of Death" murals.</note> in the fourth Arch, entering by the large gate on Rue Saint-Denis, and taking the right-hand path.

### PREFACE.

<margin>
1399
+ 1419—see <note>These dates represent the height of Flamel's life and his death; the marginal note likely refers to a chronological cross-reference in a larger history of Paris or alchemy.</note>
</margin>
Although I, NICOLAS FLAMEL, Scrivener and inhabitant of Paris, in this year thirteen hundred and ninety-nine, and living in my house on the **<term>Rue des Écrivains</term>**<note>The "Street of the Scriveners," located near the center of Paris, where professional scribes and book-binders gathered.</note>, near the Chapel of **<term>St. Jacques de la Boucherie</term>**<note>Saint James of the Butchery; today only the gothic "Tour Saint-Jacques" remains of this church.</note>, although, I say, I have learned only a little Latin, due to the meager means of my parents, who were nonetheless esteemed as good people even by my envious rivals: yet it is so [that] through the great grace of God, and the intercession of the blessed Saints (male and female) of Pa-

<summary>Nicolas Flamel begins his treatise with a prayer of gratitude to God for his wealth and then introduces his famous alchemical figures located in the Cemetery of the Innocents. He provides autobiographical details about his residence in Paris and his modest education.</summary>

<keywords>Nicolas Flamel, Alchemy, Hieroglyphics, Cemetery of the Innocents, Paris history, Rue des Écrivains, St. Jacques de la Boucherie, 1399</keywords>

--- Page 62 ---
<meta>This page continues the autobiographical account of Nicolas Flamel. The text begins mid-word, completing the word "paradise," following a passage where Flamel discusses his spiritual journey and his pilgrimage to Spain.</meta>

...dise, especially that of Saint James of Compostela <note>original: "Monsieur saint Iacques de Galice." This refers to the famous pilgrimage to Santiago de Compostela, which Flamel reportedly undertook to find a scholar who could decipher his mysterious book.</note>, I did not fail to study at length the books of the Philosophers, and to learn from them their so-hidden secrets. This is why there will never be a moment in my life—remembering this high blessing—that I do not, on my knees [if the place permits] or else in my heart with all my affection, render thanks to this most kind God. He never leaves the child of the righteous to beg at doors <note>A reference to Psalm 37:25: "I have been young, and now am old; yet have I not seen the righteous forsaken, nor his seed begging bread."</note>, and He does not defraud those who hope entirely in His blessing.

<margin>2</margin> Therefore I, **NICOLAS FLAMEL**, Scrivener <note>A "scrivener" (écrivain) in 14th-century Paris was a professional who prepared legal documents, managed accounts, and copied manuscripts.</note>, just as after the death of my parents I earned my living in our Art of Writing—making inventories, drawing up accounts, and settling the expenses of guardians and minors—there fell into my hands for the sum of two florins <note>A gold coin of the era.</note>, a gilded book, very old and quite large. It was not made of paper or parchment, as others are, but was made only of thin bark [as it seemed to me] of tender saplings. Its cover was of fine copper, all engraved with strange letters or figures; and for my part, I believe they could well have been Greek characters, or some other similar ancient language. In any case, I did not know how to read them, and I know well that they were not notes, nor Latin or French letters, for 

<margin>3</margin> we understand those a little. As for the inside, its leaves of bark were engraved with very great skill, written with an iron point in beautiful and very clear colored Latin letters. It contained three times seven leaves, for they were thus numbered at the top of the page; the seventh of which was always without writing. In place of text, there was painted a Rod and Serpents swallowing each other; on the second seventh, a Cross where a Serpent was crucified; on the last seventh, were painted deserts, in the middle of which flowed several beautiful fountains, from which came several Serpents that ran here and there. On the 

<margin>4</margin> first of the leaves, there was written in large golden capital letters: **ABRAHAM THE JEW, PRINCE, LEVITE PRIEST, ASTROLOGER, AND PHILOSOPHER,**

<summary>Nicolas Flamel describes how he acquired a mysterious, ancient book of bark and copper for two florins, detailing its strange symbolic illustrations and its attribution to Abraham the Jew.</summary>
<keywords>Nicolas Flamel, Abraham the Jew, alchemical book, bark manuscript, symbolic imagery, serpents, alchemy origins, scrivener profession</keywords>

## [ABRA]HAM, TO THE JEWISH PEOPLE BY THE WRATH
## OF GOD, DISPERSED ACROSS GAUL,
## GREETINGS. IN THE NAME OF THE LORD JESUS.
<note>original: "PHE, A LA GENT DES IVIFS PAR LIRE DE DIEV, DISPERSEE AVX GAVLES, SALVT. D. I." The "PHE" is a fragment of the name "ABRAHAM." The phrase "LIRE DE DIEV" uses the archaic "ire" (wrath). "D.I." is an abbreviation for the Latin *Domini Iesu* (the Lord Jesus).</note>

After that, it was filled with great curses and maledictions (with the word **MARANATHA**, which was often repeated there) against any person who might cast their eyes upon it, unless he were a Priest or a Scribe. <note>The term **MARANATHA** is an Aramaic expression meaning "Our Lord has come" or "Come, O Lord." In this context, it is used as part of a ritual curse (Anathema) to protect sacred secrets from the uninitiated.</note>

The one who had sold me this book did not know what it was worth, any more than I did when I bought it. I believe it had been stolen from the wretched Jews, or found hidden somewhere in the old place where they used to live. <margin>5</margin> In this book, on the second leaf, the author comforted his nation, advising them to flee from vices, and above all from idolatry, while awaiting the coming Messiah with gentle patience, who would conquer all the Kings of the earth and reign with his people in eternal glory.

<margin>6</margin> Without doubt, he must have been a very learned man. On the third leaf, and in all the following writings—to help his captive nation pay taxes to the Roman Emperors, and to do other things that I will not say—he taught them <term>metallic transmutation</term> <note>The alchemical art of changing base metals, like lead or mercury, into silver or gold.</note> in common words. He painted the vessels on the side and gave instructions on the colors and everything else, except for the <term>first agent</term> <note>The *prima materia* or "First Matter." This is the secret, fundamental ingredient needed to begin the alchemist's "Great Work." Flamel notes it was missing from the text.</note>, of which he said not a word. However (as he said on the fourth and fifth entire leaves), he painted and depicted it with very great artistry. For although it was very clearly depicted and painted, nevertheless no one could have understood it without being very advanced in their traditional <term>Kabbalah</term> <note>Jewish mystical and esoteric tradition. Flamel suggests the alchemical secrets were encoded using these mystical symbols.</note> and without having studied the books well.

<margin>7</margin> Therefore, the fourth and fifth leaves were without writing, entirely filled with beautiful illuminated figures, or something like that, for this work was very exquisite. First, he painted a young man with wings at his heels, holding a <term>Caduceus</term> <note>A staff entwined by two serpents; the traditional symbol of the messenger god Mercury.</note> rod in his hand, entwined with two serpents, with which he struck a <term>sallet</term> <note>original: "salade." A light, rounded helmet used in the 14th and 15th centuries.</note> that covered his head. He seemed, in my modest opinion, to be the pagan god Mercury. Running and flying toward him with open wings was a great old man, who had a <term>clock</term> <note>original: "horloge." In Flamel's time, this likely represented an hourglass or a mechanical clock, symbolizing Chronos or Father Time (Saturn), who governs the duration of alchemical processes.</note> fastened to his head and in his hands a scythe like Death, with which...

<meta>This page continues Nicolas Flamel's detailed description of the mysterious "Book of Abraham the Jew." On the previous page, Flamel described the first three leaves, which contained illustrations of a rod entwined with serpents, a cross with a serpent, and desert landscapes with fountains. Here, he continues with the fourth and fifth leaves, which contain the core allegories of the alchemical process.</meta>

...terrible and furious, he wanted to cut off the feet of Mercury. <note>In alchemical symbolism, "cutting off the feet of Mercury" refers to "fixing the volatile"—making a substance that naturally evaporates (like quicksilver) stable and solid so it can be worked with in a furnace.</note>

On the other side of the fourth leaf, he had painted a beautiful Flower at the summit of a very high mountain, which the **North Wind** <note>original: "Aquilon"</note> shook very rudely; it had a blue stem, white and red flowers, and leaves as shining as fine gold. Around this mountain, the Dragons and Griffins of the North Wind made their nests and dwellings.

On the fifth leaf, there was a beautiful flowering Rosebush in the middle of a fair garden, climbing against a hollow Oak; at the foot of these bubbled a Fountain of very white water, which went to plunge into the depths, passing first, however, through the hands of countless people who were digging in the earth, searching for it. But because they were blind, no one recognized it, except for one individual who considered its weight.

On the final reverse of the fifth leaf, there was a King with a great cutlass, who, in his presence, had soldiers kill a great multitude of little children. Their mothers wept at the feet of the pitiless men-at-arms. The blood of these little children was then collected by other soldiers and placed in a large vessel, in which the **Sun** and the **Moon** of the Heavens came to bathe. 

Because this history represented the greater part of the story of the **Innocents** <note>Referring to the biblical Massacre of the Innocents ordered by King Herod.</note> slain by Herod, and because I learned the greater part of the Art from this book, it was one of the reasons why I placed these **Hieroglyphic Symbols** of this secret science in their Cemetery. <note>Flamel famously funded and decorated the Cemetery of the Holy Innocents in Paris with mysterious carvings.</note>

That is what was contained in those first five leaves. I will not represent what was written in beautiful and very intelligible Latin on all the other written leaves; for God would punish me, as I would be committing a greater wickedness than he who (as they say) wished that all the men in the world had but one head so that he might cut it off with a single blow. 

Therefore, having this beautiful book at home, I did nothing but study it day and night, understanding very well all the operations it demonstrated. However, I did not know with what matter one had to begin, which caused me great sadness, kept me solitary, and made me sigh at every moment. My wife **Perrenelle**, whom I loved as much [as myself...]

than myself; she <note>Perenelle, Flamel's wife.</note>, whom I had recently married, was quite astonished by this, comforting me and asking with all her heart if she could deliver me from this trouble. I could never hold my tongue, so I told her everything and showed her this beautiful book. At the very instant she saw it, she became as enamored with it as I was, taking extreme pleasure in contemplating its beautiful covers, engravings, images, and portraits—figures which she understood as little as I did. Nevertheless, it was a great consolation to speak of it with her, and to discuss what must be done to obtain their interpretation. 

Finally, I had painted as life-like as I could, in my home, all those figures and portraits from the fourth and fifth leaves, which I showed in Paris to several great scholars <note>original: "Clers," referring to learned men or clerics.</note> who never understood them any more than I did. I even informed them that this had been found in a book that taught the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>The legendary substance sought by alchemists to transmute base metals into gold and achieve immortality.</note>, but most of them mocked me and the blessed stone, except for one named Master Anseaulme. He was a licensed physician and studied this science deeply. 

He had a great desire to see my book, and there was nothing he would not do to see it, but I always assured him that I did not have it, although I gave him a detailed description of its method. He said that the first portrait represented **Time**, which devoured everything, and that it took a space of six years, according to the six written leaves, to perfect the stone; he maintained that then the hourglass must be turned and cooked no longer. 

And when I told him that this was only painted to demonstrate and teach the **first agent** [as was stated in the book], he replied that this six-year <term>coction</term> <note>The process of "cooking" or heating a substance in a sealed vessel over a long period.</note> was like a second agent. He claimed that truly the first agent was painted there, which was the "white and heavy water." This was undoubtedly <term>quicksilver</term> <note>Mercury.</note>, which one could not fix, nor "cut its feet"—that is, remove its volatility—except by this long <term>decoction</term> <note>A process of heating or boiling to extract essence; in alchemy, a stage of maturation.</note> in the very pure blood of young children. [He] was very learned in the sublime sciences, called **Master Canches**. When I showed him the figures of my extract, seized with great astonishment and joy, he immediately asked me if I had news of the book from which they were taken. I answered him in **Latin** <note>original: "Latin"</note>—as he had questioned me in that language—that I had hope of hearing good news of it if someone would decipher these **Enigmas** for me. At once, carried away by great ardor and joy, he began to decipher the beginning for me. Now, to be brief, he was very happy to hear news of where this book was, and I was happy to hear him speak of it (and certainly he had heard it discussed at length, as he said). We resolved on our journey, and from **León** we passed to **Oviedo**, and from there to **Sanson** <note>Likely the port of Santoña in northern Spain.</note>, where we set out to sea to come to **France**. Our journey had been quite successful, and already since we had entered this Kingdom, he had very truly interpreted most of my figures for me, wherein even down to the smallest points <note>original: "points"; referring to the minute details or geometric dots in the alchemical diagrams.</note>, he found great mysteries (which I found quite marvelous). However, arriving at **Orléans**, this learned man fell extremely ill, afflicted with very great vomiting that remained with him from what he had suffered at sea. He feared so much that I would leave him that he could imagine nothing worse. And though I was always at his side, he still called for me incessantly; finally, he died at the end of the seventh day of his illness, by which I was greatly afflicted. To the best of my ability, I had him buried in the **Church of the Holy Cross** in **Orléans**, where he still rests. May God have his soul, for he died a good Christian. And certainly, if I am not prevented by death, I will give some income to this church to have some Masses said for his soul every day. <meta>Following the death of Master Canches in Orleans, as described on the previous page, Nicolas Flamel returns to Paris. Having been taught the keys to the Book of Abraham the Jew, he now recounts his final struggle to master the "first preparation" and his ultimate success in the Great Work.</meta>

<page-num>54</page-num>
<meta>catchword: of</meta>

...whom he had so often invoked. Be that as it may, by the grace of God, and the intercession of the blessed and Holy Virgin, and the blessed Saints James and John, I knew what I desired, that is to say, the first principles—though not yet their first preparation, which is a thing most difficult above all others in the world. But I finally attained it after the long errors of three years or thereabouts, during which time I did nothing but study and labor, just as I can be seen depicted outside of this **Arch** <note>Flamel refers to the "Arch" or monumental decorations he commissioned for the Cemetery of the Innocents in Paris, where he had his alchemical allegories carved.</note>, where I have placed processions against the two pillars of it, under the feet of Saint James and Saint John; always praying to God, rosary in hand, reading most attentively in a book, and weighing the words of the Philosophers, and then trying the diverse operations that I imagined by their words alone. Finally, I found what I desired, which I recognized immediately by the strong scent. Having that, I easily accomplished the <term>magistere</term> <note>The "Magistery": the completed alchemical process or the "Masterwork" of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note>; for knowing the preparation of the first agents, and following my book thereafter to the letter, I could not have failed even had I wished to. 

Therefore, the first time that I made the <term>proiection</term> <note>Projection: the final stage of the alchemical process where a small amount of the Stone is cast upon a base metal to transmute it.</note>, it was upon <term>Mercure</term> <note>Mercury or quicksilver.</note>, of which I converted half a pound or thereabouts into pure <term>argent</term> <note>Silver.</note>, better than that from the mine, as I have tested and had tested many times. This was on the 17th of January, a Monday, around midday, in my house with only **Perrenelle** <note>Perrenelle Flamel (d. 1397), Nicolas's wife, who is historically recorded as a wealthy widow and collaborator in his charitable works and, according to legend, his alchemy.</note> present, in the year of the restoration of the human lineage <note>original: "l'an de la restitution de l'humain lignage" — A pious way of dating the year from the birth of Christ (A.D.).</note> one thousand three hundred eighty-two. 

And then afterward, still following my book word for word, I performed it with the <term>pierre rouge</term> <note>The Red Stone: the final, most potent form of the Philosopher’s Stone, capable of transmuting metals into gold.</note> upon a similar quantity of <term>Mercure</term>, again in the presence of **Perrenelle** alone in the same house, on the twenty-fifth day of the following April of the same year, at about five o'clock in the evening, which I truly transmuted into almost as much pure <term>or</term> <note>Gold.</note>, most certainly better than common gold, being softer and more pliable. I can say this with truth. I have perfected it three times with the help of **Perrenelle**, who understood it as well as I did, for having helped me in the operations; and doubtless, if she had wished to undertake the perfecting of it alone, she would have achieved it. I had quite enough by perfecting it a single time, but I took very great delight...

<summary>Nicolas Flamel describes the culmination of his alchemical quest, successfully transmuting mercury into silver in January 1382, and into gold in April 1382, with the assistance of his wife Perrenelle.</summary>

to see and contemplate in the vessels the admirable works of Nature. To show you how I perfected it <note>Referring to the "Great Work" or the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> three times, you will see in this **Arch**, if you know how to recognize it, three furnaces similar to those used in our operations. I feared for a long time that Perrenelle would not be able to hide the joy of her extreme happiness, which I measured by my own, and that she might let slip some word to her relatives about the great treasures we possessed: For extreme joy takes away one's senses just as much as great sadness; but the goodness of the most high God had not only showered me with this single blessing—of giving me a chaste and wise wife—she was, moreover, not only capable of reason, but also of carrying out what was reasonable, and more discreet and secret than is common among other women. Above all, she was very devout; that is why, seeing herself without hope of children and already well advanced in age, she began, just as I did, to think of God and to attend to works of mercy.

When I was writing this commentary at the end of the year one thousand four hundred and thirteen, after the passing of my faithful companion—whom I shall regret every day of my life—she and I had already founded and endowed fourteen hospitals in this city of Paris, built three entirely new chapels, and decorated with great gifts and good revenues seven churches, with several repairs to their cemeteries, besides what we did at Boulogne, which is hardly less than what we have done here. I will not speak of the good we did together for private poor folk, especially for widows and poor orphans; if I were to tell their names and how I did this, besides the fact that my reward for it would be given to me in this world, I might cause displeasure to these good people <insert>whom God bless</insert>, which I would not wish to do for anything in the world.

Building, therefore, these churches, cemeteries, and hospitals in this city, I resolved to have painted on the fourth **Arch** of the **Cemetery of the Innocents** <note>The Holy Innocents' Cemetery was the largest and oldest cemetery in Paris until it was closed in 1780. Flamel commissioned a famous series of alchemical allegories here.</note> (entering by the great gate on **Saint Denis Street** and turning to the right) the truest and most essential marks of the <term>Art</term> <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy, specifically the Hermetic philosophy of transmuting base metals into gold.</note>, nonetheless under the veils and co-

<summary>Nicolas Flamel describes the discretion of his wife Perrenelle following their alchemical success and lists their extensive charitable contributions to Paris, including hospitals and churches. He explains his decision to encode alchemical secrets in the public decorations of the Cemetery of the Innocents.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the narrative of Nicolas Flamel, following his account of the charitable works he and his wife Perrenelle performed in Paris. The text shifts from their historical deeds to the symbolic purpose behind the "hieroglyphic" images he commissioned for the cemetery of the Holy Innocents.</meta>

<page-num>56</page-num>

...hieroglyphic openings <note>Symbolic or allegorical images intended to reveal hidden truths.</note> in imitation of those in the golden book of Abraham the Jew, which can represent two things according to the capacity and knowledge of those who contemplate them. First, they represent the mysteries of our future and certain resurrection at the day of judgment and the advent of the good JESUS (may it please him to have mercy on us), a subject well-suited to a Cemetery. And then afterward, they can signify to those who are well-versed in natural philosophy <note>An early term for the study of nature and science, specifically alchemy in this context.</note> all the principal and necessary operations of the magistery <note>The "magistery" refers to the entire process of completing the alchemical "Great Work" to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. These hieroglyphic figures will serve as two paths to lead to the celestial life: the first sense is more open, teaching the sacred mysteries of our salvation (as I shall demonstrate hereafter); the other teaches any man, however little he understands the stone, the linear path of the work. Once this work is perfected by someone, it changes him from bad to good and removes the root of all sin (which is avarice), making him liberal, gentle, pious, religious, and God-fearing, however bad he might have been before. For henceforth, he remains always ravished by the great grace and mercy he has obtained from God, and by the depth of His divine and admirable works. 

These are the reasons that moved me to set these forms in this manner, and in this place which is a Cemetery, so that if anyone obtains the inestimable benefit of conquering this rich Fleece <note>The "Golden Fleece" is a classical myth used by alchemists to symbolize the successful creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, he might think, as I did, not to keep the talent of God <note>A reference to the biblical Parable of the Talents, suggesting that spiritual and material gifts should be used productively rather than hoarded.</note> buried in the earth by buying lands and possessions, which are the vanities of this world. Instead, he should work charitably toward his brothers, remembering that he learned this secret among the bones of the dead, with whom he must soon find himself. After this transitory life, he must give an account before a just and fearsome Judge, who will censure even an idle and vain word. Therefore, let him who has well weighed my words and well recognized and understood my figures (knowing, moreover, the first principles and agents—for certainly he will find no trace or teaching of them in these figures and commentaries) perfect the magistery of Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the foundation of alchemy is attributed.</note> to the glory of God, remembering the Holy Catholic, Apostolic, and Roman Church...

He emphasizes that the ultimate goal of alchemy is moral transformation and the performance of charitable works.</summary>
