# ON THE THEOLOGICAL
## *interpretations that can be given to these Hieroglyphics according to my own sense as Author.*

### CHAPTER I.

I have given to this Cemetery a Charnel House <note>A building or vault where human skeletal remains are stored.</note> which is opposite this fourth Arch, with the Cemetery in the middle; and against one of the pillars of this Charnel House, I have had sketched in charcoal and crudely painted an entirely black man who looks directly at these Hieroglyphics <note>Flamel uses this term to mean symbolic or allegorical images that hide deeper truths.</note>, around whom is written in French: *I see a marvel by which I am greatly astonished.* <note>original: "Ie voy merueille dont moult ie mesbahi"</note> This, and also three plates of iron and gilded copper at the East, the West, and the South of the Arch where these Hieroglyphics are—with the Cemetery in the middle—representing the holy Passion and Resurrection of the Son of God, should not be interpreted otherwise than according to the common Theological sense. Except, however, that this black man may just as well cry "marvel" at seeing the admirable works of God in the transmutation of metals <note>The alchemical process of changing base metals into gold or silver.</note>—which are figured in these Hieroglyphics that he watches so attentively—as at seeing so many dead bodies buried who will rise out of their tombs on the fearsome day of judgment. On the other hand, I do not think it necessary to interpret in a Theological sense this earthen vessel in the right hand of these figures, in which there is an ink-horn, or rather a Vessel of Philosophy <term>A specialized container, such as an alembic or crucible, used in the "Great Work" of alchemy.</term> (if you remove the bindings and join the tube to the horn), nor the two other similar ones which are at the sides of the figures of Saint Peter and Saint Paul, in which there is an N. <note>The "N" likely stands for Nicolas, the author's name.</note>

<summary>Nicolas Flamel explains that the symbolic images he commissioned for the Cemetery of the Innocents possess a dual meaning: a traditional Christian message of resurrection and a hidden alchemical code regarding the transmutation of metals.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the specific explanation of the symbols Flamel commissioned at the Cemetery of the Innocents. ...which means N I C O L A S, and an F. which means F L A M E L.
For these vessels signify nothing else but that in similar ones, I have three times perfected the mastery <term>Magisterium: The completion of the alchemical Great Work, or the "philosopher's stone."</term>. Whoever also wishes to believe that I placed these vessels in the shape of niches <note>original: "armoires", likely referring to the arched frames or cases containing the letters.</note> to represent this writing-desk and the capital letters of my name, let them believe it if they wish, because both of these interpretations are true.

One must also not interpret in a theological sense this writing which follows in these terms: N I C O L A S  F L A M E L  A N D  P E R R E N E L L E  H I S  W I F E, insofar as it represents nothing other than that I and my wife have given this Arch.

As for the third, fourth, and fifth Panels following, along the length of which is written: (**How the Innocents were slain by the Command of King Herod.**) The theological sense is also understood well enough by this writing; one must only speak of the rest which is above.

The two dragons joined together, one within the other, of black and blue color, in a field of sable—that is to say black—of which one has golden wings and the other has none, are the sins which are naturally linked together; for one has its birth from the other. Of these, some can be driven away easily, as they come easily, for they fly toward us at every hour. And those who have no wings cannot be driven away, such as is the sin against the Holy Spirit. This gold of the wings signifies that most of these sins come from the accursed hunger for gold <note>original: "sacrée faim de l’or", an allusion to Virgil’s *auri sacra fames*, where "sacred" carries the Latin meaning of "accursed" or "untouchable."</note>, which makes so many people attentive, and which makes them listen so intently to where they might obtain some. And the black and blue color demonstrates that these are desires that come out of the dark pit of hell, which we must entirely flee. These two dragons can further represent, morally, the legions of evil spirits who are always around us, and who will accuse us before the just Judge on the fearsome day of Judgment, who ask for nothing but to sift us <note>A biblical reference to Luke 22:31, where Satan desires to "sift" souls like wheat.</note>.

The man and the woman who come next, colored orange on an azure and blue field, signify that man and woman must not place their hope in this world, for orange marks despair, or the relinquishing of hope as it is here; and the azure and blue color on which they are painted represents that one must think of future celestial things, and say as the man’s scroll <note>original: "rouleau"</note> does, "Man shall come to the judgment of God" <note>original: "Homo veniet ad iudicium Dei."</note>, or as that of the woman, "Truly, that day will be terrible" <note>original: "Vere illa dies terribilis erit:"</note>; so that by guarding ourselves against the dragons, which are sins, God may grant us mercy.

Following that, in a field of Sinople <term>Sinople: A heraldic term for the color green.</term>, which is to say green, are painted two men and a woman rising from the dead, of whom one comes out of a sepulcher and the other two out of the earth, all three of a very white and pure color, lifting their hands before their eyes and toward Heaven above. Over these three bodies are two Angels sounding musical instruments, as if they had called these dead to the Day of Judgment. For above these two Angels is the figure of our Lord Jesus Christ, holding the world in his hand, upon whose head an Angel places a crown, assisted by two others who say in their scrolls, "O Almighty Father, O good Jesus" <note>original: "ô Pater omnipotens, ô IESV boné."</note>. At the right side of this Savior is painted Saint Paul, dressed in citrine-white <term>Citrine: A pale yellow or lemon-like color, often associated in alchemy with the 'citrinitas' stage of the Great Work.</term>, with a sword, at whose feet is a man dressed in an orange robe in which black and white folds appear, who resembles me to the life, who asks pardon for his sins, holding his hands joined, from which come these words written in a scroll, "Blot out the evils that I have done" <note>original: "Dele mala quæ feci."</note>. On the other side, at the left hand, is Saint Peter with his key, dressed in citrine-red, holding his hand over a woman dressed in an orange robe who is at his knees, representing Perrenelle to the life, who holds her hands joined, having a scroll whereon is written, "O Christ, I pray thee, be merciful" <note>original: "CHRISTE precor esto pius"</note>. Behind her is an Angel kneeling with a scroll, who says: "Hail, Lord of Angels" <note>original: "Salue Domine Angelorum."</note>. There is also another Angel kneeling behind my image on Saint Paul’s side, who also holds a scroll, saying: "O eternal King" <note>original: "ô Rex sempiterne."</note>. All of this is very clear, according to the explanation of the resurrection and future...

# THE INTERPRETATIONS
## *Philosophical according to the Magistery of Hermes.* <note>Hermes Trismegistus is the legendary founder of alchemy; Flamel is signaling that he will now explain the chemical secrets hidden in the artwork.</note>
### CHAP. II.

I desire with all my heart that he who seeks this secret of the Sages, having turned over in his mind these Ideas of the life and resurrection to come, should first make his profit from them. In the second place, let him be more discerning than before; let him probe and delve into my figures, colors, and scrolls—especially my scrolls, because in this art one does not speak in a common way <note>Flamel implies that the text in the scrolls contains "blind" or coded alchemical instructions.</note>. Let him then ask himself: why is the figure of Saint Paul on the right hand, in the place where it is customary to paint Saint Peter, and that of Saint Peter in the place of Saint Paul? Why is the figure of Saint Paul dressed in a citrine-white <term>Citrine: A pale, lemon-yellow color, representing a specific chemical state in the alchemical process.</term> color, and that of Saint Peter in citrine-red? <meta>This page continues Flamel's address to the seeker. Having previously warned that his work is not for those ignorant of metallic principles, he now introduces the central apparatus of the Great Work—the furnace and its vessel—explaining that while the "envious" alchemists used many confusing names, the physical reality is a specific, tiered structure designed for precise heat control.</meta>

Moreover, perhaps you are a son of science <note>An initiate or a devoted student of alchemy.</note>; God allows you to learn, and then work for His glory. Listen to me then attentively, but go no further if you are ignorant of the aforementioned principles.

<image-desc>A small, square woodcut illustration of an alchemical furnace or "triple vessel" (athanor). It shows a tall structure with a central compartment containing a snake-like or flame-like shape, and a smaller opening or door at the bottom right.</image-desc>

<margin>x of the</margin>
This earthenware vessel in this form is called by the Philosophers <note>The "Philosophers" is the standard term for alchemical authorities.</note> the **triple-vessel**, for within it there is a stage in the middle, and upon it a bowl full of warm ashes, in which sits the **Philosophical Egg**, which is a glass matrass <term>Matrass: A long-necked glass flask used in distilling, often called a bolt-head.</term> full of the confections of the art (such as the "foam of the Red Sea" and the "grease of the Mercurial wind") which you see depicted in the shape of an inkstand. Now, this earthenware vessel opens from above to place the bowl and the matrass inside, beneath which, through this open door, the **philosophical fire** is placed, as you know. Thus you have three vessels, and the triple vessel; the "envious" <note>The "envious" (invidi) were earlier authors who deliberately used cryptic metaphors to hide secrets from the unworthy.</note> have called it the **Athanor**, Sieve, Dung-heap, Marie's Bath <note>Bain-Marie; a gentle water bath.</note>, Furnace, Sphere, Green Lion, Prison, Sepulcher, Urinal, Phial, and Cucurbit. I myself, in my *Summary of Philosophy* which I composed four years and two months ago, name it near the end: the "house and dwelling of the Chicken," and the ashes of the bowl, the "chicken's straw." Its common name is the furnace, which I would never have found if **Abraham the Jew** had not depicted it with its proportioned fire, in which consists part of the great secret. For it is like the belly and the womb containing the true natural heat to animate our **young King** <note>The "Young King" is a common symbol for the developing Philosopher's Stone.</note>. "If this fire is not measured Clibanically <term>Clibanically: From 'clibanus' (Greek/Latin), an oven. It refers to a soft, uniform, and enclosed heat.</term>," says **Calid** <note>Khalid ibn Yazid, a legendary early alchemist.</note> the Persian, son of Jasiche. "If it is lit with the sword," says **Pythagoras**; "If you fire your vessel," says **Morienus**, "and make it feel the heat of the fire, it will give you a blow and burn its flowers before they have risen from the depths of its marrow, coming out red..."

<meta>This page continues the specific instructions regarding the heat of the furnace. On the previous page, Flamel described the physical structure of the "triple-vessel" or Athanor. Here, he moves from the hardware to the "regimen"—the precise management of the fire over time, using the seasons and the zodiac as a metaphor for temperature control.</meta>

rather than white, and then your operation will be destroyed,
just as if you use too little fire; for then also
you will never see the end of it, because of the **chilling** <note>original: *morfondement*. In alchemy, this refers to the matter "catching a cold" or stagnating because the heat is too low to sustain the reaction.</note>
of the natures, which will not have had movements powerful
enough to digest together.

The heat, therefore, of your fire in this vessel shall be, as
Hermes and Rosinus say, like that of Winter; or rather, as
Diomedes says, according to the heat of the **Bird** <note>The "Bird" is a common alchemical metaphor for volatile substances rising as vapor and falling as liquid within the vessel.</note> that begins to fly
so gently from the sign of Aries, until that of
Cancer. For, know that the infant at the beginning is full
of cold phlegm and of milk, and that heat which is too vehement
is an enemy to the coldness and moisture of our **Embryo** <note>The initial state of the matter in the vessel.</note>,
and that the two enemies—that is to say, our Elements of cold
and hot—will never embrace each other perfectly except
little by little, having first made a long stay together
in the midst of the temperate heat of their bath, and having
changed themselves by long **decoction** <term>Decoction: The process of heating a substance in a liquid to extract its essence or mature it.</term> into **incombustible sulfur** <note>A stable, "fixed" substance that can no longer be destroyed or altered by fire.</note>.

Govern, then, gently, with equality and proportion, your
**haughty natures** <note>The volatile and reactive chemicals, personified here as proud or temperamental beings.</note>, for fear that if you favor some more
than others, they—who are naturally enemies—might
turn against you out of jealousy and dry anger, and make
you sigh for a long time. Beyond this, you must maintain
them perpetually in this temperate heat—that is to say,
night and day—until the "winter" (that is, the time
of the moisture of the materials) has passed; because they make
their peace and shake hands by warming themselves together, and
if they found themselves for even half an hour without fire,
these natures would be forever irreconcilable. This is why
it is said in the *Book of the Seventy Precepts*: "Make their fire
last indefatigably without ceasing, and let none of their days
be forgotten." And Rasis says: "Haste, which brings with
it too much fire, is always followed by the devil and error."

When the **Golden Bird**, says Diomedes, has arrived in
Cancer, and from there runs toward the Scales <note>Libra.</note>, then you
must increase the fire a little. And likewise, again
when this beautiful Bird flies from Libra toward
Capricorn, which is the desired Autumn, the time of harvest
and of fruits already ripe.

# THE TWO DRAGONS OF
## yellowish, blue, and black color like the Background.
### CHAPTER III.

<image-desc>A rectangular woodcut illustration showing two dragons or serpents. The dragon on top has wings and is biting the neck of the wingless dragon beneath it. They are positioned on a plot of land with small plants.</image-desc>

Contemplate well these two Dragons, for these are the true principles of philosophy which the sages have not dared to show even to their own children. The one underneath, without wings, is the **fixed** <note>In alchemy, "fixed" refers to matter that is stable and does not evaporate when heated.</note> [principle], or the male; the one above is the **volatile** <note>The "volatile" principle refers to substances that easily vaporize or change state.</note>, or rather the black and dark female, who will take dominance for several months. The first is called **Sulfur**, or indeed heat and dryness, and the latter is **Quicksilver** <term>Quicksilver: Another name for mercury, representing the fluid, feminine, and transformative principle in the Great Work.</term>, or coldness and moisture. These are the **Sun** and the **Moon** of Mercurial source and Sulphurous origin, which by continual fire adorn themselves with Royal garments, so that once united and then changed into the **quintessence** <term>Quintessence: The "fifth essence" or perfected state of matter, believed to be the most potent medicinal and transformative substance.</term>, they may overcome every metallic thing, whether solid, hard, or strong. These are those Serpents and Dragons that the ancient Egyptians painted in a circle, the head biting the tail <note>This symbol is known as the Ouroboros, representing the eternal cycle of nature and the unity of the beginning and the end.</note>, to signify that they had come from one and the same thing, and that it alone was self-sufficient, and that in its circuit and circulation, it brought itself to perfection. These are the dragons that the ancient poets set to guard, without sleeping, the golden apples in the gardens of the **Hesperian virgins** <note>The Hesperides: Nymphs in Greek mythology who guarded a garden of golden apples, which alchemists interpreted as the secret of the Philosophers' Stone.</note>. These are the ones upon whom **Jason**, in the adventure of the **Golden Fleece**, poured the juice prepared by the beautiful **Medea**; of whose discourse the books of the Philosophers are so full, that there has never been a philosopher who has not written about them since

<summary>Flamel explains the symbolism of the two dragons, identifying them as the "fixed" (Sulfur) and "volatile" (Quicksilver) principles that must be unified to achieve the quintessence. He links this imagery to ancient Egyptian symbols like the Ouroboros and Greek myths like Jason and the Golden Fleece.</summary>
<keywords>Dragons, Sulfur, Quicksilver, Sun and Moon, Quintessence, Ouroboros, Jason and the Golden Fleece, Medea, Hesperides, Alchemical Principles</keywords>

--- Page 81 ---
<meta>This page continues the exposition of the third chapter regarding the allegory of the two dragons. Flamel is connecting his visual symbols to the long lineage of alchemical authorities, explaining the process of "putrefaction"—the symbolic death and decay of matter required before it can be reborn in a perfected state.</meta>

...since the truthful Hermes Trismegistus, Orpheus, Pythagoras, Artephius, Morienus, and the others following, until my time. These are the two Serpents sent and given by Juno <note>In this context, Juno represents "metallic nature" or the cosmic force that governs the elements.</note>, who is the metallic nature, which the strong Hercules—that is to say, the sage—must strangle in his cradle; that is to say, conquer and kill, to make them rot, corrupt, and engender at the beginning of his work. These are the two Serpents attached around the Caduceus <note>The staff carried by Hermes/Mercury, traditionally entwined by two serpents representing balanced dualities.</note> and Wand of Mercury, with which he exercises his great power and transforms himself as he wishes.

He who kills one, says Haly <note>Likely referring to the 11th-century Arab astrologer and alchemist Haly Abenragel.</note>, will also kill the other, because one cannot die except with its brother. These two (which Avicenna <note>A Persian polymath and one of the most influential thinkers of the Islamic Golden Age; his works were fundamental to medieval alchemy.</note> calls the Bitch of Corassene <note>Khorasan; a traditional alchemical metaphor for the volatile substance.</note> and the Dog of Armenia), these two being then put together in the Vessel of the Sepulcher <note>The "Sepulcher" is the sealed glass flask or crucible where the chemical "death" or putrefaction occurs.</note>, they bite each other cruelly, and through their great poison and furious rage, they never let go of each other from the moment they have seized one another (if the cold does not prevent them) until both, with their frothing venom and mortal wounds, are bloodied in all parts of their bodies, and finally, killing each other, are suffocated in their own venom, which changes them after their death into living and permanent water. Before this, they lose, through corruption and putrefaction, their first natural forms, to take up afterward a single new one, more noble and better.

These are those two Seeds <note>original: "Spermes"</note>, masculine and feminine, described at the beginning of my *Philosophical Summary*, which are engendered (say Rhazes, Avicenna, and Abraham the Jew) in the kidneys, entrails, and from the operations of the four Elements. They are the radical moisture <note>The essential, life-sustaining fluid believed to exist within all matter.</note> of metals, **Sulfur** and **Quicksilver** <term>Quicksilver: Another name for mercury, representing the fluid, feminine, and transformative principle.</term>—not the common kind sold by merchants and apothecaries, but those given to us by these two beautiful and dear bodies that we love so much. These two Seeds, said Democritus, are not found upon the earth of the living. Avicenna says the same, but he adds, they are gathered from the dung, filth, and rot of the **Sun** and the **Moon**. Oh, how blessed are those who know how to gather them! For from these, they afterward make a **Theriac** <term>Theriac: A legendary medicinal concoction, originally an antidote for poison, here representing the universal medicine or Philosopher's Stone.</term> which has power over all pain, sadness...

<summary>Flamel explains that the two dragons represent the masculine and feminine principles (Sulfur and Quicksilver) that must "kill" each other through putrefaction in the alchemist's vessel to be reborn as a perfected substance. He cites numerous historical authorities to validate that these "seeds" of metals are found not in common materials, but through the corruption of the Sun and Moon (Gold and Silver).</summary>

<keywords>Hercules, Juno, Caduceus, Haly, Avicenna, Putrefaction, Vessel of the Sepulcher, Radical Moisture, Sulfur, Quicksilver, Theriac, Rhazes, Abraham the Jew</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Flamel's explanation of the "Two Dragons"—the symbolic representation of the volatile and fixed principles in the alchemical Great Work. He is specifically detailing the stage of "putrefaction," where the matter turns black and emits a foul odor, signaling the "death" of the old form before its spiritual rebirth.</meta>

66

disease, infirmity, and weakness, which fights powerfully against death, lengthening life by God’s permission until the determined time, triumphing over the miseries of this world and filling man with its riches. Of these two Dragons or metallic principles, I said in the summary cited above that the enemy would inflame the fire of his enemy by his heat, and then, if one were to pay attention, one would see a poisonous and foul-smelling smoke in the air, far worse in flame and poison than the venomous head of a Serpent or a Babylonian dragon. The reason I have depicted these two Seeds <term>Spermes: in alchemy, the essential "seeds" or vapors of metals that allow them to grow or transmute.</term> in the form of Dragons is because their stench is very great, similar to theirs, and the vapors that rise in the matras <term>matras: a long-necked glass flask used for heating substances.</term> are dark, black, blue, and yellowish <note>original: "flavaſtres," a term for a muddy yellow or blond color.</note>, just like these two painted Dragons; the strength of which, and of the dissolved bodies, is so poisonous that truly there is no greater venom in the world. For it is capable, by its strength and stench, of mortifying <note>To "mortify" in alchemy is to "kill" the metallic nature, stripping it of its previous form so it can be transformed.</note> and killing every living thing. The Philosopher <note>The "Philosopher" refers to the practicing alchemist.</note> never smells 
<margin>Breakage</margin> this stench unless he breaks his vessels, but only judges it to be so by the sight and the change of colors resulting from the rotting of his mixtures.

These colors, therefore, signify the putrefaction and generation <note>The alchemical belief that "the corruption of one thing is the generation of another"—life comes from death.</note> given to us by the bite and dissolution of our perfect bodies. This dissolution proceeds from the external helping heat and from the Pontic fieriness <note>original: "igneité Pontique." A technical term for the "biting" or "acidic" quality of the alchemical solvent.</note> and the admirable sharp virtue of the poison of our Mercury, which reduces and resolves into pure dust—indeed, into an impalpable powder—whatever it finds resisting it. Thus, the heat acting upon and against the metallic, viscous, or oily radical moisture, engenders blackness upon the subject. For at that same time, the matter dissolves, corrupts, turns black, and conceives in order to engender; because all corruption is generation, and this blackness must always be desired. It is also that black veil with which the ship of Theseus <note>In Greek myth, Theseus forgot to change his black sails to white upon returning from Crete; seeing the black sails, his father, Aegeus, believed him dead and threw himself into the sea.</note> returned victorious from Crete, which was the cause of his father's death; so too must the father die, so that from the ashes of this Phoenix another may be reborn, and the son may be King. Certainly, he who does not see...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "blackness" (nigredo) stage. ->67<-
->I ij<-
<meta>Checkmarks in the right margin next to lines 15 and 27.</meta>

...this blackness, at the beginning of its operations, during the days of the Stone; if he sees any other color, he fails entirely in the **magistery** <term>Magistery: the successful completion of the alchemical process or the "Great Work."</term>, and can no longer complete it with this chaos. For he is not working correctly, as there is no putrefaction; and if one does not putrefy, one does not corrupt or engender, and consequently the Stone cannot take on **vegetative life** to grow and multiply. And truly I tell you again, that even if you were to work on the true materials, if at the beginning—after having placed the mixtures in the **Philosophical Egg** <term>Philosophical Egg: a hermetically sealed glass vessel, often egg-shaped, used to contain the matter during the process.</term>, that is to say, some time after the fire has provoked them—you do not see this **Raven's Head** <note>original: "teſte du Corbeau." This is the traditional alchemical term for the deep black color (nigredo) that signals successful putrefaction.</note> of a black most black, you must start over. For this mistake is irreparable and incorrigible. In particular, one must fear an orange or semi-red color, because if at this beginning you see it in your egg, without doubt you are burning—and have burned—the greenness and liveliness of the stone.

This color which you must have should be entirely perfect in blackness, similar to that of these **Dragons**, within the space of 40 days. Therefore, let those who do not have these essential marks withdraw early from the operations, so that they may save themselves from certain loss. Know also and note well that it is nothing in this art to have blackness; there is nothing easier to obtain. For from almost all things in the world mixed with moisture, you will get blackness through fire. You must have a blackness that comes from the perfect metallic bodies, which lasts a long space of time and is only lost in five months, after which the desired whiteness succeeds. If you have that, you have much, but not all.

As for the bluish and yellowish <note>original: "bluaſtre & flauaſtre."</note> color, it signifies that the solution and putrefaction are not yet finished, and that the colors of our **Mercury** are not yet well mixed and rotted with the rest. Therefore, this blackness and these colors clearly teach that at this beginning, the matter and compound are starting to rot and dissolve into a powder finer than the **Atoms of the Sun** <note>original: "Atomes du Soleil." This refers to the tiny dust motes visible in a shaft of sunlight, used here to describe the extreme fineness of the dissolved matter.</note>, which later change into **permanent water**. And this dissolution is called by the **envious Philosophers** <note>Alchemists often called those who hid their secrets in difficult riddles "envious" (les envieux).</note>, Death, Destruction, and...

<summary>Flamel explains that a deep black color, the "Raven's Head," is essential for the stage of putrefaction; without it, the matter cannot be "reborn" or multiplied. He warns that an orange color indicates the fire is too high, "burning" the stone's vitality before it can transform.</summary>

<meta>catchword: Ser- (Serpent)</meta>

**Perdition**, because the natures change form; from this have come so many allegories about the dead, tombs, and sepulchers. Others have named it **Calcination** <term>Calcination: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile substances or to oxidize it.</term>, **Denudation**, **Separation**, **Trituration** <term>Trituration: grinding or crushing into a fine powder.</term>, and **Assation** <term>Assation: a process of heating or roasting in a dry heat.</term>, because the mixtures are changed and reduced into very small pieces and parts. Others [call it] **Reduction to first matter**, **Mollification**, **Extraction**, **Commixtion**, **Liquefaction**, **Conversion of Elements**, **Subtiliation**, **Division**, **Humation**, **Impastation**, and **Distillation**, because the mixtures are liquefied, reduced to seed, softened, and circulate within the **matrass** <term>Matrass: a long-necked glass flask used for boiling or distilling.</term>. Others [call it] **Xir** <note>original: "xir." This likely refers to a dry alchemical powder (from the Greek *xērion*).</note>, **Putrefaction**, **Corruption**, **Cimmerian Shadows** <note>A classical reference to a mythical land of perpetual darkness; in alchemy, it refers to the deep blackness of the *nigredo* stage.</note>, **Abyss**, **Hell**, **Dragons**, **Generation**, **Ingression**, **Submersion**, **Complexion**, **Conjunction**, and **Impregnation**, because the matter is black and watery, and the natures mingle perfectly and retain one another. For when the heat of the Sun acts upon them, they first change into a powder, or a greasy and glutinous water which, feeling the heat, flees upward into the "head of the chicken" <note>An alchemical term for the top part or "cap" of the distilling vessel.</note> with the smoke—that is to say, with the wind and the air. From there, this water, drawn and melted from the mixtures, goes back down; in descending, it reduces and dissolves as much as it can the rest of the aromatic mixtures, continuing this way until everything is like a black, slightly greasy broth.

This is why this is called **Sublimation** and **Volatilization**, because it flies upward; and **Ascension** and **Descension**, because it rises and falls within the **cucurbit** <term>Cucurbit: the lower, gourd-shaped vessel of a still, which contains the substance to be distilled.</term>. Some time later, the water begins to thicken and coagulate further, becoming like very black pitch, and finally becomes body and earth, which the **envious** <note>A traditional alchemical term for uninitiated or rival authors who supposedly hid secrets out of spite.</note> have called **Stinking** and **Fetid Earth**. For then, because of the perfect putrefaction—which is as natural as any other—this Earth is stinking, and gives off an odor similar to the stench of sepulchers filled with rot and bones still laden with natural moisture. This Earth was called by **Hermes** <note>Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy.</note> "The Earth of Leaves"; nevertheless, its most proper and true name is the **Leton** <note>original: "Leton." A technical term for the base matter of the Stone when it has reached the black stage but before it has been purified to white.</note>, which must afterward be whitened. The ancient wise **Cabalists** have described it in the *Metamorphoses* under the story of the Ser-

# OF THE MAN AND WOMAN
->clothed in orange robes, upon a field of azure and blue, and of their scrolls.<-

### CHAPTER IV.

<image-desc>A woodcut emblem showing two bust portraits facing each other within a rectangular frame. On the left is a bearded man in a cap, and on the right is a woman wearing a hood or veil. A scroll winds around them with Latin text: "Man shall come to the judgment of God" <note>original: "HOMO VENIET AD IVDICIVM DEI"</note> on the left, "Truly that day" <note>original: "VERE ILLA DIES"</note> in the center, and "Shall be terrible" <note>original: "TERRIBILIS ERIT"</note> on the right.</image-desc>

The Man depicted here resembles me exactly from life, just as the woman represents Perrenelle <note>Perrenelle Flamel (d. 1397), the wife of Nicolas Flamel, who was said to be his collaborator in his alchemical endeavors.</note> very naturally. The reason why we are painted from life is not for a specific personal purpose. For it was only necessary to represent the male and the female, for which our specific likeness was not necessarily required. But it pleased the sculptor to put us there, just as he also did in this same Arch <term>Arch: Referring to the Cemetery of the Holy Innocents in Paris, where Flamel commissioned a series of symbolic carvings.</term> higher up at the feet of the figures of Saint Paul and Saint Peter, as we were in our youth, and elsewhere in several places such as on the door of the chapel of Saint-Jacques-de-la-Boucherie <note>The church where Flamel was a parishioner; its famous tower still stands in Paris today.</note>, near my house (though in this latter one there is a specific cause), as well as on the door of Sainte-Geneviève-des-Ardents where you can see me. Therefore, I depict for you here two bodies, one of a male and the other of a female, to teach you that in this second operation you truly have—though not yet perfectly—two

--- Page 86 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of the symbolic figures on Flamel's Arch, moving from the identity of the figures (Flamel and his wife Perrenelle) to the alchemical theory of the "conjoined natures" and the transition from the black stage (nigredo) toward the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</meta>

...conjoined and married natures, the masculine and feminine, or rather the four Elements; and that the natural enemies—heat and cold, dry and moist—begin to approach one another in a friendly manner, and by means of the mediators of peace, gradually lay down the ancient enmity of the old chaos. You know well enough who these mediators are: between heat and cold, it is moisture, for it is kin and allied to both—to heat through its warmth <note>original: "calidité"</note>, and to cold through its humidity. This is why, to begin making this peace, you have already, in the preceding operation, converted all the mixtures into water through dissolution <term>dissolution: the alchemical process of liquefying a solid substance, often seen as the first stage of the "Great Work."</term>

And then afterward, you caused the necessary water to coagulate, which has been converted into this black earth of the blackest black <note>The "nigredo" stage, symbolizing the death and decomposition of the matter before its purification.</note>, to achieve complete peace. For the earth, which is dry and moist <note>Flamel here describes earth as having a dual nature to explain how it bridges the gap between other conflicting elements.</note>, being also kin and allied with the dry and the moist which are enemies, will appease them and reconcile them entirely. 

Do you not perceive a most perfect mixture of all these four Elements, having first converted them into water, and now into earth? I will teach you later of the other conversions into air, when all shall be white <note>The "albedo" or white stage, representing purification.</note>, and into fire, when all shall be a perfect purple <note>The "rubedo" or red stage, the final stage of perfection and the creation of the Stone.</note>. Thus you have here two married natures, of which one has conceived from the other, and by this conception, has been converted into the body of the male, and the male into that of the female; that is to say, they have become a single body, which is the Androgyne <term>Androgyne: a being combining male and female qualities, symbolizing the perfect union of opposites.</term> of the ancients, which is also otherwise called the Raven's Head <term>Raven's Head: "Caput Corvi" in Latin; a traditional term for the black stage of the alchemical work.</term>, and converted Elements. 

In this way, I depict for you here that you have two reconciled natures which (if they are guided and governed wisely) can form an Embryo in the matrix of the vessel, and then give birth to a most powerful King, invincible and incorruptible, because he shall be an admirable quintessence <term>quintessence: the "fifth essence," a substance of celestial purity that transcends the four physical elements.</term>. That is the principal end of this representation, and the most necessary. The second, which is also very notable, is that I had to depict two bodies, because in this operation you must divide what has been coagulated, in order to provide nourishment afterward—a milk of life—to the newborn infant, who is endowed (by the living God) with a vegetative soul <note>The "vegetative soul" refers to the power of growth and "multiplication" that the Alchemical Stone possesses once it has been brought to life.</note>.

<summary>Flamel explains the alchemical union of opposites—the masculine and feminine principles and the four elements—through the mediating role of moisture and earth. This process leads to the "Androgyne" or "Raven's Head" stage, eventually promising the birth of the "King" or Quintessence.</summary>
<keywords>Four Elements, Androgyne, Raven's Head, Nigredo, Quintessence, Dissolution, Alchemical Marriage, Vegetative Soul</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the alchemical explanation of the symbolic figures on Flamel's Arch. Having established the "nigredo" or black stage on the previous page, Flamel now describes the transition toward the "albedo" (white stage) using mythological metaphors of Apollo, Python, and Theseus.</meta>

This is a most admirable and very hidden secret which has driven mad, for lack of understanding it, all those who have sought it without finding it, and which has made wise every person who contemplates it with the eyes of the body or the mind.

You must therefore make two parts and portions of this coagulated body, one of which will serve as the *Azoth* <term>Azoth: The essential secret agent or universal solvent in alchemy, often associated with mercury or the "First Matter."</term> to wash and cleanse the other, which is called *Leton* <term>Leton: Also known as Laton, it represents the impure "black" matter or base metal that must be purified and "whitened" into silver.</term> which must be whitened. He who is washed is the Serpent Python, who, having taken his being from the corruption of the mud of the earth gathered by the waters of the flood (when all the mixtures were water), must be slain and conquered by the arrows of the God Apollo—by the golden Sun—that is to say, by our fire equal to that of the Sun.

<margin>Jason <note>Jason, leader of the Argonauts; Flamel uses the myth of sowing the dragon's teeth to describe the chemical reactions within the vessel.</note></margin> He who washes, or rather these washings, which must be continued with the other half, these are the teeth of this Serpent that the wise operator—the valiant Theseus—will sow in the same earth, from which will be born soldiers <note>original: "gendarmes"</note> who will eventually defeat themselves, allowing themselves by "apposition" <note>The addition of matter to matter to build up the substance.</note> to be resolved into the same nature as the earth, leaving behind the hard-earned conquests. It is about this that the Philosophers have written so often, and so many times repeated: "It dissolves itself, congeals itself, blackens itself, whitens itself, kills itself, and gives itself life."

I had their field painted azure and blue to show that I am only beginning to emerge from the blackest blackness. For azure and blue is one of the first colors revealed to us by the "dark woman"—that is to say, the moisture yielding slightly to heat and dryness. The man and the woman are mostly orange. This signifies that our bodies (or our body, which the wise call here the <term>Rebis</term> <note>Rebis: From the Latin "res bina," meaning "double thing." It represents the divine hermaphrodite, the union of spirit and matter.</note>) do not yet have enough digestion, and that the moisture from which the black, blue, and azure come is only half-conquered by the dryness.

For when dryness dominates everything, it will be white; and while it is fighting or being equal to the moisture, everything is partially according to these present colors. The envious <note>Alchemists often called themselves "philosophers" and those who hid the truth "the envious."</note> have also called these mixtures in this operation: *Money* <note>original: "Numus"</note>, *Ethelia* <note>An alchemical term for the volatile spirit or vapor.</note>, *sand* <note>original: "arena"</note>, *Boritis* <note>A purifying agent</note>, *Corsufle*, *Cambar*, *Whiteness of copper* <note>original: "Albar æris"</note>, *Duc-* <meta>catchword: Duc-</meta>

<summary>Flamel explains the purification process (the washing of the Leton) using the myths of Python and Theseus, marking the transition from the black stage toward the white through the balance of moisture and dryness.</summary>
<keywords>Azoth, Leton, Serpent Python, Apollo, Theseus, Rebis, Albedo, purification, alchemical colors</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Flamel’s explanation of the symbolic figures on his arch. Following the previous page's discussion of the purification of "Leton" (the black matter) through the metaphors of Python and Theseus, he now moves into the specific signs of the "Albedo" or whitening stage. The catchword "LA" on the previous page leads into the list of alchemical authorities.</meta>

nech, Randeric, Kukul, Thabitris, Ebifemeth, Ixir, etc., whom they have commanded to whiten.

The female has a white circle in the shape of a roll around her body, to show you that the <term>Rebis</term> <note>From the Latin "res bina," meaning "double thing." In alchemy, this is the hermaphroditic figure representing the union of spirit and matter, or male and female principles, reaching a state of stability.</note> will begin to whiten in this same way, whitening first at the extremities all around this white circle. **The Scale of the Philosophers** says: The sign of the first perfect whiteness is the manifestation of a certain small capillary circle—that is to say, passing over the head—which will appear around the matter on the sides of the Vessel in a sub-citrine <note>A pale, lemon-yellow color that appears just as the "blackness" begins to fade into "whiteness."</note> color.

There is on their scrolls:

->**Man shall come to the judgment of God.**<-
<note>original: "Homo veniet ad iudicium Dei"</note>

->**Truly, (says the woman) that day shall be terrible.**<-
<note>original: "Verè illa dies terribilis erit"</note>

These are not passages from Holy Scripture, but only sayings speaking in the theological sense of the future resurrection. I have placed them thus; for they serve me toward those who contemplate only the gross and more natural artifice, taking the interpretation of the resurrection. And likewise, they serve those who, wishing to gather the parables of science, use the eyes of Lynceus <note>In Greek mythology, Lynceus was an Argonaut with sight so keen he could see through the earth; Flamel uses this to describe the "philosophical vision" needed to see the hidden changes within the alchemical vessel.</note> to penetrate beyond visible objects. There is, therefore, "man shall come to the judgment of God; truly this day shall be terrible." It is as if I were saying: this matter must come to the coloring of perfection, to be judged and cleaned of its blackness and filth, and to be spiritualized and whitened. 

Certainly this day will be terrible; yes, truly, as you will also find in the allegory of Aristeus: "Horror held us in prison for eighty days in the darkness of the Waves, in the extreme heat of Summer, and the troubles of the Sea." All these things must first pass before our King can be whitened, coming from death to life, to later defeat all his enemies. To teach you even better this <term>albification</term> <note>The "Albedo" or whitening stage. This is the second major goal of the Great Work, resulting in the "White Stone" which can transmute metals into silver.</note>, which is more difficult than all the rest (up until which time you can err at every step, and afterward not, unless you were to break your vessels), I have also made for you this following table.

<summary>Flamel describes the transition from the black stage to the white stage (Albedo), identifying the appearance of a "capillary circle" in the vessel as a primary sign of success. He uses the theological metaphor of the Resurrection and Judgment Day to describe the "rebirth" of the matter after it has been purified of its initial corruption.</summary>

Following the description of the scrolls and the first signs of the whitening (Albedo) stage, he now focuses on the visual representation of a figure resembling St. Paul. This figure represents the transition into the yellowing (citrinitas) and eventually the final reddening of the alchemical matter.</meta>

# THE FIGURE OF A MAN

resembling that of St. Paul, dressed in a white lemon-yellow <note>original: "citrine"</note> robe, bordered with gold, holding an unsheathed sword, having at his feet a kneeling man, dressed in a robe of orange, white, and black, holding a scroll.

## CHAPTER V.

Library
of the Czech Theosophical Society
in Prague.

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration contained within a triangular frame. A standing bearded man, representing St. Paul, holds a large sheathed sword upright. A smaller figure of a man kneels before him in supplication. To the left, an angel holds a banner that reads "O Eternal King" <note>original: "O REX SEMPITERNE"</note>. A small scroll at the feet of the figures reads "Blot out the evils I have done" <note>original: "DELE MALA QVA FECI"</note>. In the background, a small architectural structure resembling a church or shrine is visible.</image-desc>

Look closely at this man in the form of a St. Paul, dressed in a robe entirely of white lemon-yellow <note>Original: "citrine." This color signifies the Citrinitas stage, a transition between the "white" work and the "red" work.</note>. If you consider him well, he turns his body in a posture that shows he wants to take up the unsheathed sword, either to cut off the head <note>In alchemy, decapitation often symbolizes the separation of the soul from the body or the removal of "superfluities" from the base matter.</note>, or to do some other thing to this man who is at his feet on his knees, dressed in an orange robe.

<sig>K</sig>

<summary>This page describes a symbolic figure resembling St. Paul, whose lemon-yellow robe signifies the "Citrinitas" stage of the Great Work. The act of the sword-wielding figure over the kneeling man represents the refinement and purification of the matter.</summary>
<keywords>St. Paul, Citrine, lemon-yellow, Albedo, Citrinitas, unsheathed sword, orange robe, purification, decapitation symbol</keywords>

--- Page 90 ---
<meta>This page continues the detailed iconographic analysis of the figures on Flamel's arch. The previous page ended while describing the robe of the kneeling man; this page completes that description and explains the transition from the black stage of the work (Nigredo) to the white stage (Albedo).</meta>

...orange, white, and black, who says on his scroll: 
> "Blot out the evils I have done" <note>original: "Dele mala quæ feci"</note>, 

as if saying: "Take away my blackness,"* <gloss>a term of the art</gloss> <note>Alchemical "art" refers to the process of transforming matter. "Blackness" refers to the initial stage of decay and purification.</note>. For "evil" <note>Latin: "malum"</note> signifies blackness by allegory; thus in the *Turba* <note>The *Turba Philosophorum*, an ancient alchemical text depicting a gathering of philosophers.</note> one often finds: 
> "Cook until the blackness," 

which one will deem to be "evil." But do you want to know what this man taking up the sword teaches? He signifies that one must "cut off the head of the Raven" <note>A famous alchemical metaphor for removing the dark, impure elements of the material to reach the next stage.</note>, that is to say, from this man dressed in various colors who is kneeling. I took this feature and figure from Hermes Trismegistus <note>The legendary, mythical founder of the Hermetic tradition and alchemy.</note> in his book on the secret art, where he says: 
> "Take the head from this black man, cut off the head of the Raven," that is to say, whiten our sand. 

Lambspring <note>A noble German alchemist known for his book of symbolic emblems.</note>, a noble German, had also already employed this in the commentary of his hieroglyphs, saying: 
> "In this wood there is a beast which is all covered in blackness; if someone cuts off its head, then it will lose its blackness and put on the most white color." 

Do you want to understand what this is? The blackness is called the "Raven's Head," which, once removed, the white color immediately arrives; then—that is to say, when the cloud no longer appears—this body is called "headless." These are his own words. In the same sense, the Sages have also said elsewhere: 
> "Take the viper called *Rexa* <note>A term often used in medieval alchemy to refer to a specific mineral or the "regulus" of a metal.</note>, cut off its head, etc.," 

that is to say, take away its blackness. They have also used this periphrasis <note>A roundabout way of describing something.</note> when, to signify the multiplication of the Stone <note>The process of increasing the power and quantity of the alchemical Elixir.</note>, they invented a Hydra serpent, from which, if one head were cut off, ten more would grow back. For the Stone increases tenfold each time one cuts off this Raven's head—that is, when one blackens it and whitens it, which means dissolving it anew and then re-coagulating it.

Notice that the naked sword is wrapped with a black belt, and that the ends of it do not surround it at all. This naked, shining sword is the "Stone at the White" <note>The *Albedo* or white stage, which follows the blackness and indicates a high degree of purity.</note>, so often described by the philosophers under this form. Therefore, to reach this perfect, sparkling whiteness, you must understand the windings of this black belt and follow what they teach, which is the quantity of the imbibitions <note>The OCR reads "inhibitions," but in this context, it refers to *imbibitions*: the repeated process of moistening the dry matter with "mercurial water" to nourish it.</note>. The two ends which do not wrap around it at all represent the beginning and the end. For the beginning, it teaches that one must imbibe during this first stage...

<summary>Flamel explains the symbolism of "cutting off the Raven's head" as the transition from the black stage (Nigredo) to the white stage (Albedo). He references various authorities like Hermes Trismegistus and Lambspring to illustrate how dissolving and re-coagulating the matter increases its power.</summary>

gently and sparingly, giving then to the stone a little
milk, as if to a small newborn child, so that the Elixir <note>original: "l'Iſir"</note>
(say the Authors) is not submerged. The same must be done
at the end, when we see that our King is satiated and

in such a way that the Virginal milk <term>Virginal milk: a symbolic term for a mercurial liquid or solvent used to "wash" the matter</term> surrounds all the matter.

I have had these twists of the belt painted black,
because they are imbibitions <term>imbibition: the process of repeatedly adding liquid to a solid substance so it absorbs it</term>, and consequently
blacknesses. For fire with moisture (as has been so many times
said) causes blackness. And as these five full twists 
demonstrate that this must be done five times entirely,
likewise they make it known that this must be done over
five full months, one month for each imbibition: This is
why Hali Abenragel <note>A famous 11th-century Arab astrologer and alchemist</note> said, *The cooking of things is perfected in three times fifty days.* It is true that if you wish
to count these small imbibitions of the beginning and end,
there are seven. Regarding which one of the most envious <note>In alchemical literature, "envious" authors were those who deliberately obscured their secrets to keep them from the unworthy.</note> has said, *Our Raven’s head is leprous:* that is why, whoever wishes
to clean it, must make it descend seven times to the river of regeneration, to the Jordan, just as the Prophet commanded 
the leper Naaman the Syrian. Including in this the 
beginning, which is only a few days, the middle, and the
end, which is also very short. I have therefore given you this picture
to tell you that you must whiten my body which is 
kneeling, which asks for nothing else. For nature tends
always toward perfection. This you will accomplish by the addition of the Virginal milk, and by the decoction <note>The process of boiling or heating the substance in liquid.</note> that you will make of the 
matters with this milk, which, drying upon this body, will tint it
in the same citrine white <note>A yellowish-white color signaling the transition from the White stage (Albedo) toward the Yellow/Red stage.</note> as the one who takes up the sword is clothed,
in which color you must make your Corsufle <term>Corsufle: a technical term derived from Arabic, referring to the "body" or metallic substance of the stone</term> arrive. The garments of the figure of St. Paul are broadly embroidered with
a golden and citrine red color. O my son, praise God, if you see
this one day. For already from Heaven you have obtained mercy Im- <meta>catchword: Im-bibitions or Impossible?</meta>

drink then and tinge, until the Little child <note>A symbolic name for the Alchemical Stone in its early developing state</note> be strong
and robust to fight against water and fire. Accomplish-

in reverse of the beginning, by coagulating what 
you previously dissolved, and making earth of what you previously made water.
When you have whitened, you have conquered the enchanted Bulls,
who breathed fire and smoke from their nostrils. Hercules has 
cleaned the stable full of filth, rot, and blackness.
Jason has poured the juice over the Dragons of Colchos, and you have 
in your power the Horn of Amalthea <note>The Cornucopia, a symbol of infinite abundance</note>, which (even though it be
white) can fill the rest of your life with glory,
honor, and wealth. To obtain it, you had to fight 
valiantly, and in the manner of a Hercules: For this Achelous <note>A river god who fought Hercules; here, representing the fluid, changing nature of the primary matter</note>,
this humid river which is the blackness, is endowed with a 
very powerful force, besides which it often transforms itself from one form
to another: Thus you have completed it, since the rest is 
without difficulty. These transformations are described 
particularly in the book of the *Seven Egyptian Seals*, where it is said,
(as also by all the Authors) that before entirely leaving
the blackness, and whitening in the manner of a 
shining marble, and a naked, flaming sword, the Stone
will clothe itself in all the colors you can imagine;
often it will liquefy itself, and often coagulate
again; and amid these diverse and contrary operations
(which the <term>Vegetative Soul: the internal life force or "spirit" within the matter that drives its growth and change</term> which is in it causes it to perfect 
at the same time) it will turn citron, green, red—though not 
a true red—yellow, and become blue and orange, 
until, being entirely conquered by dryness and heat, all...

...all these infinite colors end in this admirable lemon-white <note>original: "blancheur citrine"</note> 
of Saint Paul’s garment, which in a short

and then the perfect Lake-red <note>a deep, crimson pigment</note>, where it will rest 
henceforth. I do not want to forget, in passing, to warn you 
that the Milk of the Moon <note>the alchemical solvent used for the white stage</note> is not like the Virginal Milk of the Sun <note>the solvent used for the red stage</note>; 
consider, therefore, that the <term>imbibitions: the process of "feeding" or soaking the alchemical matter with liquid</term> of the whiteness 
require a whiter milk than those of the redness and <term>aureité: the state of being golden or the quality of gold</term>.

Abraham the Jew <note>the legendary author of the book Flamel claimed to have discovered</note>; for this reason, I had the figure painted for you 
who holds the naked sword, in the color that is necessary for you, 
as it is this figure who performs the whitening.

### CHAP. VI.

<image-desc>A woodcut alchemical emblem in the shape of a cross. At the top is a triangular pediment containing a half-length figure of an angel. Below this, in the horizontal crossbar area, two angels emerge from clouds on either side, holding scrolls. The left scroll reads "O ALMIGHTY FATHER" <note>original Latin: "O PATER OMNIPOTENS"</note> and the right scroll reads "O GOOD JESUS" <note>original Latin: "O IESV BONE"</note>. I have had a green field painted for you in this way because in this
decoction <note>the process of heating the alchemical substance in a vessel</note> the confections <note>the medicinal or alchemical mixtures</note> become green, and they keep
this color longer than any other after the black <note>the "nigredo" or stage of decay and blackness that begins the work</note>.

<keywords>Green Field, Resurrection, Savior, lemon-white, decoction, confections, alchemical colors, black stage</keywords>

--- Page 95 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of the alchemical imagery, specifically focusing on the transition from the black stage of "death" to the white stage of "resurrection," represented by three white figures rising from a green field.</meta>

This greenness demonstrates particularly that our Stone has a vegetative soul, and that it has been converted by the industry of art into a true and pure germ <gloss>seed</gloss>, to sprout abun-<margin>branches</margin>dantly, and produce afterwards infinite branches. "O blessed greenness," says the Rosary <note>The *Rosarium Philosophorum*, or "Rosary of the Philosophers," a seminal 16th-century alchemical treatise</note>, "who produces all things; without you nothing can grow, vegetate, nor multiply." The three resurrected figures clothed in sparkling white represent the body, the soul, and the spirit of our white Stone. The Philosophers commonly use these terms of the art to hide the secret from the wicked. They call "body" the black, obscure, and dark earth which we whiten. They call "soul" the other divided half of the body, which by the will of God and the power of nature gives to the body—through its imbibitions <note>the process of moistening dry matter with liquid</note> and fermentations—a vegetative soul; that is to say, the power and virtue to sprout, grow, multiply, and become white like a naked, shining sword. They call "spirit" the tincture and dryness, which, like a spirit, has the power to penetrate all metallic things. It would take too long to show you here by how many reasons they have said everywhere: "Our Stone has, like man, a body, soul, and spirit." I only want you to note well that just as man, endowed with body, soul, and spirit, is nevertheless but one, so also you now have only a single white confection <note>a completed alchemical preparation</note>, in which nevertheless are the body, soul, and spirit, which are inseparably united. 

I could well give you very clear comparisons and explanations of this body, soul, and spirit, but to explain them I would have to say things that God reserves to reveal to those who fear him and who love him, which consequently must not be written down. I have therefore had painted for you here a body, a soul, and a spirit, all in white, as if they were being resurrected, to show you that the Sun, the Moon, and Mercury <note>the three primary alchemical principles often associated with Gold, Silver, and Quicksilver</note> are resurrected in this operation; that is to say, they are made Elements of the air, and whitened. For we have already called the blackness "death"; continuing the metaphor, we can therefore call the whiteness a "life" that only returns with and through the resurrection. To show you the Body more clearly, I have had it painted lifting the stone from its tomb in which it was locked away. The soul

ther substance, animate or inanimate; for it is a quin-
tessence <term>the "fifth essence" or purest distillation of a substance</term>, a most pure silver, passed through the cupel <term>a small, porous bowl used in assaying to separate precious metals from impurities</term> and refined 
seven times, as the Royal Prophet David says.

There is no need to interpret what the two
Angels playing instruments above the heads of the resurrected signify; 
these are divine spirits, singing the wonders of 
God in this miraculous operation, rather than Angels cal-
ling us to judgment. Purposely, to make a distinction, 
I have given a lute to one and a horn <term>buccine: a curved Roman trumpet or horn</term> to the other, not 
trumpets, which are always given to them to call to 
judgment. The same must be said of the three Angels who are above 
the head of our Savior, of whom one crowns him, and the 
other two say on their scrolls while assisting him, *O Almighty Father, O 
good Jesus* <note>original: "ô Pater omnipotens, ô Iesu bone"</note>, rendering him eternal thanks.

# UPON A VIOLET AND BLUE FIELD, two orange-colored Angels, and their scrolls.

## CHAP. VII.

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration depicting two winged angels standing in a landscape. Each angel holds a long, unfurled scroll. The scroll on the left contains the translated text "Rise, you dead" <note>original: "SVRGITE MORTVI"</note>, and the scroll on the right contains "Come to judgment" <note>original: "VENITE AD IVDICIVM"</note>.</image-desc>

THIS violet and blue field shows that, in wanting to pass from the **White Stone** to the **Red**, you have soaked it with a little **Solar Virginal Milk** <note>A poetic term for a refined alchemical solvent, often associated with "mercury of the sun," used to nourish the Stone during its growth.</note>, and that these colors have come out of the **Mercurial moisture** that you have dried upon the **Stone**. In this operation of **reddening** <term>rubification: the final stage of the alchemical process, where the substance turns red, signifying its ultimate power and perfection</term>, even though you soak it, you will have hardly any blackness, but rather <margin>x Peacock</margin> violet, blue, and the color of the **peacock's tail** <note>The "Peacock's Tail" (cauda pavonis) is a famous stage in the Great Work where the matter in the flask displays a shifting iridescent rainbow of colors.</note>: For our stone is so triumphant in **dryness** <note>original: "siccité"</note>, that as soon as your **Mercury** touches it, **nature rejoicing in nature** <note>A fundamental alchemical axiom, suggesting that substances of a similar spiritual essence naturally attract and perfect one another.</note>, joins itself to it and drinks it greedily; and therefore the blackness that comes from moisture can only show itself a little beneath these violet and blue colors, since dryness (as has been said) now governs absolutely. I have had these two **Angels** painted with wings to represent to you that the two substances of your concoctions—the **Mercurial** and **Sulfurous**, the fixed as well as the **volatile**—being perfectly fixed together, also fly together in your **Vessel**. For in this operation, the fixed body will gently rise to Heaven, entirely spiritual, and from there it will descend to the Earth, and wherever you wish, following everywhere the spirit which moves always upon the fire. Since they are made one

<summary>This page explains the transition from the White Work to the Red Work, focusing on the shifting colors (the "Peacock's Tail") and the unification of fixed and volatile substances. It uses the imagery of angels to represent the spiritualized matter rising and descending within the alchemist's vessel.</summary>

<keywords>Red Work, Rubedo, Peacock's Tail, Solar Virginal Milk, fixed and volatile, Mercurial moisture, alchemical marriage</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the description of the transformation from the White Work to the Red Work. The previous page concluded by describing the iridescent "Peacock’s Tail" colors appearing as the Stone reaches a state of triumphant dryness, noting that "nature rejoices in nature."</meta>

of the same nature, and the compound is entirely spiritual, and the spiritual
is entirely bodily, so much has it been refined upon our <term>marble</term> <note>original: "marbre" — a flat stone used for grinding materials or a specific vessel where the substances were blended</note> by the
preceding operations. The natures are thus trans-

subtle; so too are they now true <term>tinctures</term> <note>substances capable of instantly transforming and coloring other metals</note>. 
Now, remember to begin the <term>rubification</term> <note>the final "reddening" stage of the alchemical process</note> by adding 
material, and only once or twice, according to what you see. For
this operation must be perfected by dry fire, <term>sublimation</term> <note>the process of vaporizing a solid and re-condensing it</note> and 
dry <term>calcination</term> <note>reducing a substance to powder through high heat</note>: And truly I tell you a secret here that 
you will very rarely find written, for I am not at all

## CHAP. VIII.

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration within a quadrant-shaped frame. It depicts a bearded man as Saint Peter standing on the left, holding a large key in his right hand and resting his left hand on the head of a kneeling woman. The woman, representing Perrenelle <note>Perrenelle Flamel (1320–1397) was the wife and collaborator of Nicolas Flamel; her presence here emphasizes the importance of the partnership in the alchemical work.</note>, holds a scroll inscribed with "Christ, I pray, be merciful" <note>original Latin: "CHRE PRECOR ESTO PIUS"</note>. In the background between them is a small architectural structure featuring a shield with the letter "B".</image-desc>

Look at this woman dressed in an orange robe who looks so natural, like Perrenelle as she was in her youth; she is painted in the manner of a suppliant, on her knees, with hands joined, at the feet of a man who has a key in his right hand, who listens to her graciously and then extends his left hand over her. Do you wish to kno- <note>The text cuts off mid-word ("sçavoir" / "know") at the end of the page.</note>

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the allegorical figures of Saint Peter and a kneeling woman (Perrenelle). Flamel explains that this stage represents "Multiplication," the process of increasing the potency of the Philosopher's Stone through repeated cycles of dissolution and coagulation.</meta>

<page-num>85</page-num>
<sig>L iij</sig>

...w wish to know <note>This completes the sentence from the previous page: "Do you wish to know..."</note> what that represents? It is the Stone which asks, in this operation, for two things from the Solar Mercury of the Philosophers (depicted in the form of the man): namely, multiplication and a richer adornment. This she must obtain at this time. Thus, the man, placing his hand on her shoulder in this way, says, "I grant it to her." But why did you have a woman painted? I could just as easily have had a man painted as a woman, or an Angel (for the natures are now all spiritual and corporeal, masculine and feminine), but I preferred to have a woman painted so that you might judge that she asks for this rather than anything else, because these are the most natural and proper desires of a woman. 

To show you even further that she asks for multiplication <term>multiplication: The alchemical process of increasing the power, quantity, and transmuting virtue of the Philosopher's Stone.</term>, I had the man to whom she prays painted in the form of a Saint Peter, holding a key, having the power to open and close, to bind and unbind. For the envious Philosophers <note>Alchemists often referred to themselves or their predecessors as "envious" because they deliberately obscured their secrets from the unworthy.</note> have never spoken of multiplication except under these common terms of the art: *Open, close, bind, unbind.* They have called "opening" and "unbinding" the process of making the body (which is always hard and fixed) soft, fluid, and flowing like water; and "closing" or "binding," the process of coagulating it afterward by a stronger decoction <term>decoction: A process of continued heating to concentrate or change the state of a substance.</term>, returning it once more into the form of a body.

I therefore had to represent a man with a key to teach you that you must now open and close—that is to say, multiply—the germinating and growing natures. For as many times as you dissolve and fix, so many times will these natures multiply in quantity, quality, and virtue according to a multiplication of ten; from this number coming to a hundred, from a hundred to a thousand, from a thousand to ten thousand, from ten thousand to a hundred thousand, from a hundred thousand to a million, and from there by the same operation even unto infinity, just as I have done three times, praised be God. And when your Elixir <term>Elixir: The final, perfected substance of the Great Work, also called the Tincture or the Stone.</term> is thus conducted to infinity, a single grain of it falling upon a quantity of molten metal as deep and vast as the ocean would tinge and convert it into a most perfect metal—that is, into silver or gold, according to how it had been steeped and fermented—chasing away and leaving far behind...

<vocab>stone, Solar Mercury, Philosophers, multiplication, Elixir, silver, gold, transmutation</vocab>

<summary>Flamel explains that the figures of Saint Peter and the woman represent the process of Multiplication, where the Stone is repeatedly dissolved and solidified to increase its power exponentially. He claims this process can be repeated until the Elixir is so potent that a single grain could transmute an entire ocean of molten metal into gold or silver.</summary>

# THE FIGURE OF A MAN
resembling Saint Peter, dressed in a red citrine <note>citrine: a yellow-orange color in alchemy representing the *citrinitas* stage, which falls between the white and red stages of the Great Work.</note> robe, holding a key in his right hand, and placing the left upon a woman dressed in an orange robe, who is at his feet, on her knees, holding a scroll.

## CHAPTER VIII.

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration depicting three figures. On the left stands a man identified as Saint Peter, bearded and wearing a long robe, holding a large key in his right hand. He places his left hand on the shoulder of a woman kneeling before him. The woman, identified in the text as Perrenelle, has her hands joined in prayer and holds a scroll (phylactery) that reads "Christ, I pray, be merciful" <note>original Latin: "CHRE PRECOR ESTO PIVS"</note>. To the right, a winged angel kneels, holding a long, curved banner that reads "Hail, Lord of the Angels" <note>original Latin: "AVE DOMINE ANGELORVM"</note>. The entire scene is enclosed in a simple rectangular border.</image-desc>

Look at this woman dressed in an orange robe who so naturally resembles Perrenelle <note>Perrenelle Flamel (1320–1397) was the wife of Nicolas Flamel and his collaborator in alchemical research.</note>, as she was in her youth; she is painted in the manner of a supplicant, on her knees, with hands joined, at the feet of a man who has a key in his right hand, who listens to her graciously, and then extends his left hand over her. <meta>This page continues the explanation of the eighth figure from page 84, where Nicolas Flamel describes the symbolic meaning of the man (Saint Peter) and the woman (Perrenelle) in the context of the alchemical process of Multiplication.</meta>

<page-num>85</page-num>

...to see what this represents? It is the Stone which, in this operation, asks two things of the Philosophers’ Solar Mercury <note>Solar Mercury: a refined form of mercury that has been "animated" or joined with the solar (gold) principle, essential for the final stages of the Work.</note> (depicted in the form of the man): namely, multiplication and a more splendid raiment. This she is to obtain at this time. Thus, the man, placing his hand upon her shoulder in this way, grants it to her. But why did you have a woman painted? I could just as well have had a man painted as a woman, or an Angel (for the natures are now entirely spiritual and corporeal, masculine and feminine). But I preferred to have a woman painted so that you might judge that she asks for this more than anything else; because these are the most natural and proper desires of a woman. To show you even more clearly that she asks for multiplication, I had the man to whom she addresses her prayer painted in the form of a Saint Peter, holding a key, having the power to open and close, to bind and to loose. Since the envious Philosophers <note>envious Philosophers: a traditional term for alchemical authors who "envied" the reader the truth and therefore hid their secrets behind metaphors and riddles.</note> have never spoken of multiplication except under these common terms of the art: *Open, close, bind, loose.* They have called "opening" and "loosing" the act of making the body (which is always hard and fixed) soft, fluid, and flowing like water; and "closing" or "binding" the act of coagulating it afterwards by a stronger decoction <note>decoction: the process of heating a substance in a liquid to extract its essence; here, it refers to the prolonged application of heat to the alchemical vessel.</note>, restoring it once more to the form of a body.

I therefore had to represent a man with a key to teach you that you must now open and close—that is to say, multiply—the germinating and growing natures. For as many times as you dissolve and fix, so many times will these natures multiply in quantity, quality, and virtue according to a multiplication by ten; reaching from that number to a hundred, from a hundred to a thousand, from a thousand to ten thousand, from ten thousand to a hundred thousand, from a hundred thousand to a million, and from there by the same operation until infinity, just as I have done three times, Praise be to God. And when your Elixir <note>Elixir: the final, most potent form of the Philosopher's Stone, capable of transmuting base metals and prolonging life.</note> is thus led to infinity, a single grain of it falling upon a quantity of molten metal as deep and vast as the Ocean will tinge and convert it into a most perfect metal—that is to say, into silver or gold, according as it has been imbued and Fermented—casting out and leaving far behind...

<summary>Flamel explains that the figure of Saint Peter represents the alchemical power to "open and close" (dissolve and coagulate), which allows the Stone to be multiplied in power and quantity toward infinity. He describes how the Elixir, once multiplied, can transmute vast amounts of base metal into gold or silver.</summary>

--- Page 108 ---
<meta>This page continues Nicolas Flamel’s explanation of the symbolic figures. Having established that the woman represents the "Stone" and the man represents "Saint Peter" (the key to multiplication), he now describes the purification process and the specific colors of their garments which signal the progress of the Work.</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>

...from itself all the impure and foreign matter that had joined it during its first coagulation. For the same reason that I had a key painted for the man who is in the form of a Saint Peter—to signify that the Stone required being opened and closed in order to multiply—for that same reason also, I did this to show you with which Mercury you must perform this, and why I gave the man a citrine-red garment and the woman an orange one. Let this suffice so as not to break the silence of Pythagoras <note>silence of Pythagoras: a reference to the Pythagorean tradition of keeping sacred or philosophical knowledge secret from the uninitiated.</note>, and to teach you that the woman—that is, our Stone—asks to have the rich finery and color of Saint Peter. 

She has written on her scroll: *O Christ, I pray, be merciful* <note>original Latin: "Christe precor esto pius."</note>. "Jesus Christ, be gentle with me," as if she were saying: "Lord, be gentle with me, and do not permit the one who has reached this point to spoil everything with too much fire." It is quite true that from now on I shall no longer fear enemies, and that every fire will be the same to me; nevertheless, the vessel <term>vessel: often called the "Philosopher’s Egg," this is the glass container in which the alchemical matter is heated.</term> that contains me is still fragile. For if one raises the fire too much, it will burst, and by shattering, it will carry me away and scatter me unhappily among the ashes. 

Take heed, then, of your fire at this step, ruling this admirable quintessence <term>quintessence: the "fifth essence," the purest and most concentrated spiritual force extracted from the elements.</term> gently and with patience; for its fire must be increased, but not too much. And pray to Sovereign Goodness that He might not permit the evil spirits who guard the mines and treasures to destroy your operation, or fascinate your sight when you consider these incomprehensible movements of this quintessence within your Vessel.

<summary>Flamel explains the final stages of the alchemical process, focusing on the careful regulation of heat (fire) to prevent the glass vessel from bursting and the importance of spiritual focus to avoid "fascinations" or failures.</summary>
<keywords>multiplication, Saint Peter, Mercury, citrine, orange, Pythagoras, fire management, quintessence, philosophical vessel, spiritual protection</keywords>
