## ON A DARK VIOLET FIELD,
## obscure, a purplish-red man, holding the
## foot of a Lake-red Lion, which has wings,
## and seems to seize and carry the man away.

->CHAPTER IX.<-

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration framed in a rectangle. It depicts a winged lion standing over a man who appears to be reclining or falling. The lion is grabbing the man's foot or lower leg with its claws. Above the man's head is a small tablet or banner containing the word "MEI" <note>original Latin: "MEI" meaning "of me" or "mine," often referring to the soul in this context.</note>.</image-desc>

This dark and obscure violet field represents that the Stone has obtained, through complete decoction <term>decoction: the process of heating a substance in a liquid to extract its essence, a vital stage in alchemical maturation</term>, the beautiful garments—entirely yellow and red—that it requested from Saint Peter, who was clothed in them. It also shows that its complete and perfect digestion (signified by the total citrinity <term>citrinity: the yellowing stage of the alchemical process, occurring between the white and red stages</term>) has caused it to cast off its old orange robe. The Lake-red <note>Lake-red: a deep, transparent red pigment made from organic dyes, highly prized by artists and alchemists for its brilliance.</note> color of this flying Lion, similar to the pure and clear scarlet of the seed of the true red Pomegranate, demonstrates that the Work is now accomplished in all righteousness and balance. It is like a Lion, devouring every pure metallic nature and changing it into its own true substance—into true and pure gold, finer than that from the best mines. Thus, it now carries the man out of this valley of miseries—that is to say, away from the discomforts of poverty and infirmity—and with its wings, it raises him gloriously out of the stagnant waters of Egypt <note>waters of Egypt: a common alchemical metaphor for the material world or the "mercurial" state of bondage before the soul or matter is purified.</note> (which are the ordinary thoughts of mortals). By making him despise his present life and riches, it causes him to meditate night and day on God and His Saints, to dwell in the Empyrean Heaven <term>Empyrean Heaven: the highest part of the heavens in ancient cosmology, thought to be the realm of pure fire and the dwelling place of the Divine.</term>, and to drink from the sweet springs of the fountains of eternal hope.

<vocab>Stone, Red Lion, decoction, citrinity, Scarlet, gold, Egypt, Empyrean Heaven</vocab>

<page-num>88</page-num>

Praised be God eternally, who has granted us the grace to see this beautiful and entirely perfect purplish color <note>original: "couleur purpurine"</note>, this beautiful color of the Wild Poppy of the Rock <note>The "Wild Poppy" refers to the deep, vibrant red achieved at the very end of the alchemical process, often compared to the flower's petals.</note>, this sparkling and flaming Tyrian color <term>Tyrian: a legendary purple-red dye of antiquity; in alchemy, it represents the highest possible grade of the Philosopher's Stone</term>, which is incapable of change or alteration, and over which Heaven itself and its Zodiac can no longer have dominion or power. Its radiant and dazzling brilliance seems almost to communicate something super-celestial to man, causing him (when he contemplates and knows it) to wonder, tremble, and shudder all at once. O Lord, grant us the grace that we may use it well, for the increase of Faith, for the profit of our soul, and for the growth of the glory of this noble Kingdom. Amen <note>original: "Amen"</note>.

<meta>The text continues from the preceding page of a treatise attributed to Synesius, an author often identified in alchemical traditions as a learned Greek abbot or bishop.</meta>

# THE TRUE BOOK OF THE LEARNED GREEK
## ABBOT SYNESIUS, TAKEN FROM THE LIB-
### rary of the Emperor.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'C' featuring floral scrollwork and foliage.</image-desc>
Although the ancient philosophers have written in various ways about this science, hiding the true principles of the art under an infinity of names, they did not do so without the weightiest considerations, which we will set forth hereafter. And though they spoke quite differently, they were not at all in disagreement; rather, they tended toward the same end. Speaking of the same thing, they saw fit to name the actual agent, above all, by strange names, sometimes contrary to its nature and qualities. Now, un-

<margin>3</margin>
-derstand then, my son, <note>a traditional alchemical form of address, where the teacher adopts the role of a spiritual father to the "Son of the Art"</note> that the great God created two Stones with this universe: the white and the red. Both are found within the same subject, and later they grow in such abundance that anyone can take as much as they wish. Their matter is of such a sort that it holds the middle ground between metal and mercury, and it is partly fixed and partly non-fixed; otherwise, it would not hold the middle position between metals and mercury. This matter is the instrument that will fulfill our desire, provided we prepare it. Because of this, those who work in this art without this <term>medium</term> <note>the mediating substance or "middle" agent between spirit and body</note> lose all their labor. But if they know this medium, all things will be possible and favorable to them. Know that this medium is found existing as an aerial thing with the celestial bodies; and in it alone are the masculine and feminine genders, properly speaking. It possesses a firm, strong, fixed, and permanent virtue. Of its essence (as I was saying), the philosophers have spoken only through metaphors and figures. This was done so that the science might never be grasped by the ignorant—for should that happen, all would perish—but only by patient souls and refined spirits, set apart from the mire of the world and cleansed of the filth of the terrestrial mud that is avarice. By this avarice, the ignorant are held with their noses to the earth in this world (without this admirable <term>quintessence</term> <note>literally the "fifth essence," the purest part of a substance</note>), which is the home of all pov-

<margin>4</margin>
-erty. Be assured that these divine souls, after having plunged into the <note>original: "puis de Democrite"</note> well of Democritus—that is, into the truth of Nature—will undoubtedly recognize the chaos that would befall all social orders and trades if everyone could produce gold in whatever quantity they desired. And

<summary>This page introduces an alchemical treatise attributed to Synesius, explaining that philosophers used cryptic language to hide the "medium" or "Stone" from the unworthy. It describes the substance as a balance between fixed and volatile states and warns that the secret must be kept to prevent social and economic collapse.</summary>

--- Page 113 ---
<page-num>91</page-num>
<sig>M ij</sig>

...therefore they chose to speak through figures, types, and analogies, so as to be understood only by wise and holy souls, illuminated by Wisdom <note>The author refers to *Sapience*, or Divine Wisdom, often personified in alchemical literature</note>. It is true, however, that in their composed works, they have provided a certain path, way, and rule by which the wise man can understand all that they have written secretly, and at last arrive there <margin>5</margin> after some error—just as I have done, praise be to God. And although the ignorant commoners ought to understand these reasons, and thereby venerate that which cannot enter into their brains, on the contrary, they have accused the philosophers of falsehood and wickedness, so much so that the art is despised almost everywhere because there are so few wise men. Now I tell you that they have always spoken according to the true truth, but very covertly, and sometimes through fables, which I clearly explain in this little book, and in such a way that everyone who desires knowledge will understand what has been hidden by the philosophers. <margin>6</margin> However, if he thinks to understand me without knowing the nature of the Elements and created things, and our rich metal, he would labor in vain. But if he knows the fleeing and following natures <note>Refers to volatile substances that evaporate ("flee") and fixed substances that remain in the heat ("follow")</note>, by the grace of God he will be able to reach the goal. Therefore I pray to God that he who understands this present secret may work for the glory and praise of His holy Divinity. Know then, my dear son, that the ignorant man cannot understand the secret of the art, because it depends on the knowledge of the true body which is hidden from him. Know then, my son, the Natures: the pure and the impure, the clean and the unclean; for nothing can give what it does not have. And because things are not, and cannot be made <gloss>except</gloss> <note>original: "quod"</note> according to their nature, use the most perfect and closest member that you can find, and it will suffice for you. Leave the mixture aside, and take its simple form. For it <margin>7</margin> is of the quintessence. And note that we have two bodies of very great perfection, filled with quicksilver <note>original: "vif argent"; mercury</note>; therefore, from them draw your quicksilver, and you shall make from it the medicine, called by some "quintessence," which is a power—imperishable, permanent, and always victorious; indeed, it is a clear light which illuminates with true goodness every soul that has once tasted it. It is the knot and the bond of all the Elements which it contains within itself, and the spirit that nourishes all things, by means of which nature works in the universe. It is the strength, the beginning, and the end of the entire work; and so that I may manifest the whole to you in a single word, know that the quintessence and the hidden thing of our stone is nothing other than our viscous, celestial, and glorious soul, drawn by our mastery <note>The *magistery* refers to the technical process or "mastery" of the alchemical operation</note> from its mineral, which alone engenders it. It is <margin>8</margin> not possible for us to make this water by art, but nature alone is what engenders it; and this water is the very sharp Vinegar <note>An alchemical solvent, likely a specialized acid or "mercurial water" used to dissolve gold</note> which causes gold to become pure spirit; indeed, it is that blessed Nature which engenders all things, which is most unified with its own putrefaction, and with...

<summary>The author explains that alchemical secrets are hidden in allegory to protect them from the "ignorant vulgar" and identifies the "quintessence" or "viscous soul" as the vital agent of the Great Work. He emphasizes that the alchemist must work with nature's own laws and "sharp vinegar" to spiritualize gold.</summary>

--- Page 114 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructional discourse from page 91, shifting from the philosophical necessity of secrecy to practical, yet metaphorical, laboratory instructions regarding the "water" and the transformation of the elements.</meta>

<page-num>92</page-num>
<sig>M ij</sig>

its <term>Viridity</term> <note>original: "Viridité"; in alchemy, this refers to the "greenness" or life-force within matter, often associated with the "Green Lion" or the beginning of the Great Work</note> causes several colors to appear. And I tell you, my son, that you should disregard other things as vain, but only [focus on] this water, which burns, whitens, dissolves, and congeals; it is this which putrefies and causes germination. And therefore I advise you that your entire focus should be on the <term>decoction</term> <note>A process of heating or boiling a substance to extract its essence</note> of your water, and do not be troubled by the length of time, otherwise you will have no fruit. Cook it gently, little by little, until it changes from a false color to a perfect one; and take care that at the beginning you do not burn its flowers and its vivacity, and do not hurry to reach the end quickly. Seal your vessel well, so that what is inside cannot escape, and thus you will be able to achieve the effect.

<margin>9</margin> And note, that dissolving, calcining, dyeing, whitening, cooling, bathing, washing, coagulating, imbibing, cooking, fixing, grinding, drying, and distilling are one and the same thing, and mean nothing more than cooking nature until it is perfect. Note again, that extracting the soul, or the spirit, or the body, is nothing other than the aforementioned calcinations, because they signify the operation of <term>Venus</term> <note>In alchemical symbolism, Venus usually represents copper or a specific stage of harmony and attraction between substances</note>. It is therefore with the fire of the extraction of the soul that the spirit comes forth sweet; understand me. This can also be said of the extraction of the soul from the body, and its subsequent reduction back into that compound, until the whole is drawn into the commixture of all four elements. And thus, **what is below is like what is above** <note>A famous maxim from the *Emerald Tablet* of Hermes Trismegistus, expressing the correspondence between the earthly and the divine</note>; and thus two luminaries are made, one fixed and the other not, of which the fixed remains below and the volatile above, moving perpetually until the one below, which is the male, rises over the female and everything becomes fixed; and then a peerless luminaire is born. And as in the beginning there was only one, similarly in this matter everything will come from one and return to one. This is called "converting the Elements"; and converting the Elements means making the moist dry and the fugitive fixed <note>original: "fugitif"; refers to volatile substances that evaporate easily</note>, so that the thickened thing diminishes and weakens the thing that fixes the others, remaining as the fixative of the thing. Thus is made the death and the life of the Elements, which, being composed, germinate and produce; thus one thing perfects the other, and helps it to combat the fire.

->Alchemy, Viridity, Elements, Venus, decoction, calcine, distill, fixative, volatile, commixture<-

<summary>The author explains that all alchemical operations—such as distilling or washing—are essentially a single process of "cooking" nature. He emphasizes the Hermetic principle of correspondence ("as above, so below") to describe the unification of fixed and volatile elements into a single, perfect substance.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the instructional discourse from page 92, moving from general laboratory advice about gentle heating to the specific nature of the primary substance used in the Work: the Philosophers' Mercury.</meta>

<page-num>93</page-num>
# PRACTICE.
<sig>M iij</sig>

My son, it is necessary that you work with the <term>Philosophers' Mercury</term> <note>Also known as "Mercury of the Wise," this is not the common quicksilver found in a thermometer, but a specialized substance prepared by the alchemist</note>, which is not the common kind, nor of the common kind in its entirety, but according to the sages is the <term>First Matter</term> <note>The primordial substance from which all things are made</note>, the soul of the world, the cold Element, the blessed water, the water of the wise, the poisonous water, the very strong vinegar, the mineral water, the water of celestial grace, the <term>virginal milk</term> <note>A term for a white, liquid stage of the matter</note>, our mineral and corporeal Mercury. For this alone perfects both the white and red <term>Stones</term> <note>The "White Stone" converts base metals to silver; the "Red Stone" or "Philosophers' Stone" converts them to gold</note>. Observe what <term>Geber</term> <note>A Latinized name for Jabir ibn Hayyan, a foundational figure in alchemy and chemistry</note> says: That our art does not consist in a multitude of diverse things, because Mercury is a single thing—that is to say, a single Stone in which the entire <term>magistery</term> <note>The complete mastery or successful completion of the alchemical process</note> consists; to which you shall add no foreign thing, except that in its preparation you shall remove from it all superfluous matters, insofar as all things necessary to this art are contained within this matter. And for this reason he specifically says, "We shall add nothing foreign except the <term>Sun and the Moon</term> <note>Symbolic names for Gold and Silver</note> for the white and red <term>tincture</term> <note>The essence that "colors" or transforms the base metal</note>," which are not foreign, but constitute its <term>Ferment</term> <note>A substance that acts like yeast, triggering a transformation in the rest of the matter</note> by which the work is accomplished. Finally, note, my son, that these Suns and Moons are not similar to the common suns and moons, because our Suns and Moons are better in their nature than the common suns and moons. Inasmuch as our Sun and our Moon in one and the same subject are living, and those of the common people are dead, in comparison to ours which exist and remain permanent in our Stone. Following this, you will remark that the Mercury drawn from our bodies is similar to aqueous and common Mercury; and for this reason the thing rejoices in its like, and takes pleasure with it, and accompanies it better and more willingly, just as the simple and the compound do, which has been hidden by the philosophers in their books. Therefore the entire benefit that is in this art lies in the Mercury, the Sun, and the Moon, and all the rest is in vain. Thus <term>Diomedes</term> <note>An authority often cited in alchemical compendiums</note> says: "Use the matter to which you must introduce no foreign thing, powder, nor water," because diverse things do not improve our stone, and by this he demonstrates to whoever understands him well, that the tincture of our Stone is drawn only from the Mercury of the philosophers, which is their principle, their root, and their great tree from which so many branches later emerge.

<summary>This page identifies the "Philosophers' Mercury" as the singular, essential substance of the Great Work, possessing many allegorical names, and explains that it is combined with alchemical "Sun" and "Moon" to act as a ferment.</summary>
<keywords>Philosophers' Mercury, First Matter, Geber, Sun and Moon, Ferment, Magistery, Tincture, Diomedes, White Stone, Red Stone</keywords>

--- Page 116 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general description of the Philosophers' Mercury to the first practical step of the Great Work: Sublimation. It continues the instructional tone, citing the authority of Geber to emphasize that this single process encompasses all other alchemical operations.</meta>

<page-num>94</page-num>

# FIRST OPERATION,

## SUBLIMATION.

<margin>by elevation</margin>
It is not at all common, but rather philosophical, by which we remove the surplus of that stone; in effect, it is nothing other than the elevation of the non-fixed part through smoke and vapor, for the fixed part must remain at the bottom. Furthermore, we do not want the one to be separated from the other, but rather that they remain and become fixed together. And know that he who shall properly sublime our <term>Philosophical Mercury</term> <note>The essential starting substance of the alchemists, purified of dross</note>, in which lies all the virtue of the stone, shall complete the <term>magistery</term> <note>The "masterwork" or the successful creation of the Philosophers' Stone</note>. And for this reason <term>Geber</term> <note>Jabir ibn Hayyan, a foundational 8th-century alchemist</note> says: "All perfection consists in sublimation," and within this sublimation are all the other operations, namely: distillation, <term>assation</term> <note>The process of heating a substance in its own moisture, or dry roasting</note>, destruction, coagulation, putrefaction, calcination, fixation, and the reduction of the white and red <term>tinctures</term> <note>The transformative liquids or powders capable of changing the properties of metals</note> produced and engendered in one furnace and one vessel. This is the straight path until the final consummation, about which the philosophers have written various chapters to halt the ignorant.

Take then, in the name of the great GOD, the venerable matter of the philosophers, named the first <term>Hylec</term> <note>A term derived from the Greek word for "matter," referring here to the primordial substance of the Sages</note> of the Sages, which contains the aforementioned Philosophical Mercury, called the first matter of the perfect body. Place it into its vessel as is required—clear, lucid, and round, well-plugged and closed by the <term>seal of seals</term> <note>Also known as the Hermetic seal; an airtight closure of a glass vessel</note>. Set it to be heated in its well-prepared place with tempered heat for one continual <term>philosophical month</term> <note>A symbolic period of time, often forty days, required for a specific stage of the work</note>, keeping it in the "sweat" of sublimation until it begins to purify itself, to heat up, to take on color, and to congeal with its metallic humidity. Let it become so fixed that nothing more can rise as a smoky, airy substance, but instead remains fixed at the bottom, altered and deprived of all viscous humidity, purified and black, which is called the "black robe," "darkness," or the <term>Crow’s Head</term> <note>The 'Caput Corvi,' representing the 'Nigredo' or black stage of alchemy, signifying the death and putrefaction of the matter</note>. Thus, when our stone is in the vessel and rises upward in smoke, this manner is called sublimation; and when it falls from top to bottom, it is called distillation and descension. When it begins to hold the smoky substance and putrefy, and through the frequent rising and falling begins to coagulate, then <term>putrefaction</term> <note>The breaking down of the substance through rot or fermentation</note> is formed, and the "devourer" suffers. Finally, by the lack or deprivation of the moisture of the <term>radical water</term> <note>The fundamental, life-giving moisture inherent in the matter</note>, calcination and fixation are achieved at the same time by a single <term>decoction</term> <note>The process of boiling or heating a substance in a liquid</note> in a single vessel, as I have said already. Furthermore, in this sublimation is made the true separation of the Elements, because in our sublimation the <term>elixir</term> of water is changed into the Element...

<summary>This page describes the first operation of the Great Work, Sublimation, explaining it as a cyclical process of rising and falling that leads to the "Crow's Head" or black stage. The author emphasizes that this single process in a sealed vessel contains all other alchemical transformations like distillation and calcination.</summary>

<keywords>Sublimation, Philosophical Mercury, Geber, Hylec, Crow's Head, Nigredo, Hermetic seal, Putrefaction, Radical moisture, Alchemical furnace</keywords>

--- Page 117 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the first stage of the alchemical process, specifically explaining the relationship between the four elements during the "blackening" or nigredo phase. It connects the "first Hylec" mentioned at the end of the previous page to its elemental qualities of earth and fire.</meta>

<page-num>95</page-num>

...dry and hot earth, by which thing it is manifest that the separation of the 4 Elements in our **Stone** is not common but philosophical. And because of this, there are in our **Stone** only two formed Elements, namely: Earth and Water; but the earth holds in its thickness the virtue and dryness of Fire. And the water contains within itself Air with its moisture. Thus in our **Stone** we have only two Elements in view, even though in effect we have four. And by this you can judge that the separation of the 4 Elements is entirely physical, not common and real, as the ignorant do daily. Therefore continue the **decoction** <note>The process of heating a substance in a liquid to extract its essence</note> at a slow fire, until all the black matter appearing on the surface is entirely removed by the <term>magistery</term> <note>The master-process or technical execution of the alchemical work</note>, which blackness is named by the philosophers the **Tenebrous Robe of the Stone**, which afterwards remains clear, and is named Modified Water of the earth, or indeed of the elixir. And note, that the blackness which appears is a sign of <term>putrefaction</term> <note>The breakdown of the substance, a necessary stage where the matter "dies" before being reborn</note>. And the commencement of the dissolution is a sign of the conjunction of two Natures, and this blackness appears sometimes in 40 days, more or less, according to the quantity of the matter and the good industry of the worker, who aids greatly in the separation of the said blackness. Now my son, by the grace of God you have henceforth one Element of our **Stone** which is the black earth, the **Raven's Head**, by others called the dark shadow, upon which earth—as upon a trunk—all the rest is founded. And this earthly and dry Element is named <term>Laton</term> <note>An alchemical term for the "copper" or base metal during its transition to white</note>, Bull, Black Dregs, our Metal, our Mercury. And thus by the deprivation of the <term>adustive humidity</term> <note>A "burnt" or corrosive moisture that must be removed for the matter to become pure</note> which is removed by Philosophical sublimation, the volatile is made fixed, and the soft is made dry and earth; indeed, according to **Geber**, a change of temperament occurs, such as from a cold and moist Nature into a <term>dry choler</term> <note>Referring to the four humors; choler is naturally hot and dry</note>, and from the liquid into the thick, according to **Alphidius** <note>An early Arabic alchemical author frequently cited by Western Europeans</note>. And thus the intention of the philosophers is apparent when they say that the operation of our **Stone** is nothing but a change of Natures and a revolution of Elements. You see then how by this incorporation, the moist becomes dry, and the volatile fixed, the spiritual corporeal, and the liquid thick, the water fire, and the air earth; and thus they certainly change their true nature, and all 4 Elements circulate one into another.

<summary>This page describes the 'Nigredo' or blackening phase of the Great Work, explaining how the four elements are condensed into Earth and Water to form the 'Raven’s Head.' It emphasizes that alchemical transformation is essentially a 'revolution' or cycling of elements where their physical properties are fundamentally reversed.</summary>

<keywords>Nigredo, Raven's Head, Four Elements, Geber, Alphidius, Laton, Putrefaction, Philosophical Sublimation, Decoction</keywords>

--- Page 118 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions following the separation of the elements, moving from the "blackness" or nigredo stage mentioned at the end of page 95 into the second major operation: the whitening or albedo.</meta>

<page-num>96</page-num>
<header>OF THE SECOND OPERATION.</header>

## DEALBATION.
<note>From the Latin *dealbatio*, meaning "whitening" or "bleaching," this is the stage where the matter is purified of its impurities.</note>

It converts our Mercury into the White Stone, and this by **decoction** <note>The process of heating or "cooking" a substance in a liquid</note> alone. After the earth has been separated from its water, then the vessel must be placed upon the ashes, as is used in the distillation furnace, and the water distilled at a slow fire at the beginning, in such a manner that the water comes so gently that you can distinctly count up to forty names, or else say fifty-six words <note>A traditional method of timing the rhythm of the drops before the invention of precise stopwatches</note>; and let this order be observed throughout the distillation of all the black earth. That which is found at the bottom of the vessel, which is the <term>feces</term> <note>The solid dregs or sediment remaining after distillation</note> left with the new water, will then dissolve; this water will contain three or four parts more than those same feces, so that everything dissolves and converts into Mercury and **quicksilver**. I tell you that you shall do this so many times, that nothing remains but the <term>marc</term> <note>The exhausted solid remains</note>.

In this distillation there is no determined time, but it is done according to the large or small quantity of the water, always observing the intensity of the fire. Afterwards you shall take the earth which you will have reserved in its glass vessel with its distilled water, and thus with a slow and gentle fire—like that of the distillation or purification, or perhaps a little stronger—you shall continue until the earth is dry and white, and has drunk all its water while drying. 

This being done, you shall put on it the aforementioned water, and thus as at the beginning, you shall always continue your decoction, until the said earth is entirely white, cleansed, and clear, and has drunk all its water. And note that the said earth will be thus washed of its blackness by its decoction, as I have told you, because it easily purifies itself with its water and cleanses itself, which is the end of the <term>magistery</term> <note>The master-work or finished process of this stage</note>; and then you shall keep this white earth diligently, for it is White Mercury, white **magnesia** <note>In this context, not the modern chemical, but a name for the crude mineral matter of the Stone</note>, and **foliated earth** <note>original: "terre feuillée"; a term describing the flaky, pure white state of the matter</note>.

Afterwards, you shall take this white earth, rectified as above, and place it in its vessel upon the ashes at the fire of **sublimation**, to which you shall give a strong fire, until all the coagulated water which shall be inside comes into the <term>alembic</term> <note>The upper part of the still, where vapors condense</note>, and the earth remains at the bottom well **calcined**. Then you will have the earth, the water, and the air; and although the earth contains within itself the nature of fire, nevertheless it is not yet apparent in effect, as you will see when, by a greater decoction, you make it become red—so that then you will see the fire manifestly in appearance—and thus one must proceed to the **Fermentation** of the earth

<meta>catchword: white,</meta>

<summary>This page details the process of "Dealbation" or whitening, where the black matter (the "feces") is repeatedly distilled and "cooked" until it becomes a pure, white "foliated earth." This stage marks the successful purification of the substance and prepares it for the final transformation into the red state through fermentation.</summary>

<keywords>Dealbation, Albedo, distillation, White Stone, foliated earth, magnesia, calcination, sublimation, purification, Mercury</keywords>

--- Page 119 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the "Second Operation" (the whitening) before transitioning into the final "Third Operation" (the reddening). The previous page ended with instructions on heating the earth and water.</meta>

<page-num>97</page-num>
<sig>N</sig>

white, so that the dead body is animated and brought to life, and so that its virtue multiplies to infinity. But note that the Ferment cannot enter into the dead body, except by means of the water that has formed the marriage and conjunction between the Ferment and the white earth. And know that in every Ferment one must observe the weight, so that the quantity of the volatile does not overcome the fixed, and so the marriage does not vanish in smoke. For, says Senior <note>Referring to Senior Zadith, the Latinized name of the 10th-century Arab alchemist Ibn Umayl</note>: "If you do not convert the earth into water, and the water into fire, the spirit and the body will not join together." And to do this, take a glowing metal plate, and place upon it a drop of our medicine; it will penetrate and color itself with a perfect color, which shall be a sign of perfection. And if it happens that it does not dye, reiterate the dissolution and coagulation, until it becomes tinting and penetrating. And note, that seven imbibitions <note>The process of repeatedly soaking a solid substance in liquid until it is saturated</note> are sufficient at most, and five at least, so that the matter liquefies and is without smoke, and then the matter is perfected at the white stage. For the matter is fixed sometimes in a longer time, and sometimes in less, according to the quantity of the Medicine. And note that our Medicine, from the creation of our Mercury, requires a term of seven months to reach whiteness, and five to reach red, which makes twelve in total.

# OF THE THIRD OPERATION.
->RUBIFICATION<-
<note>From the Latin *rubedo*, this is the final stage of the alchemical "Great Work," where the matter turns red, signifying the creation of the Red Stone or Elixir capable of transmuting metals into gold.</note>

Take as much of the white Medicine as you wish, and place it with its glass vessel upon the warm ashes, until it is as dry as they are. Afterward, give it the water of the Sun <note>Also called "Solar Water," typically a reference to a gold-based solution or the philosophical sulfur needed for the red stage</note>, which you will have kept aside for the said task, and continue the fire of the second degree, until it becomes dry; then give it the aforementioned water again, and thus successively soak and dry it, until the matter turns red and liquefies like wax, and flows upon the red plate, as was said; and then the matter shall be perfected at the red. But note, that at each time you must not put more of the Solar water than what is needed to cover the body, and no more, and this is done so that the Elixir is not submerged or drowned; and thus the fire must be continued until desiccation, and then the second soaking must be done, and so proceed in order until the perfection of the Medicine—namely, until the power of the fire's digestion converts it into a very red powder, which is the true Oil of the Philosophers, the Bloodstone, the Purple Red Coral, the Precious Ruby, the Red Mercury, and the Red Tincture.

<summary>This page concludes the whitening process (Albedo) and details the final stage of the Great Work, the Rubification (Rubedo). It describes the precise timing, the use of "Solar water," and the various poetic names for the final Red Stone.</summary>
<keywords>Ferment, Senior Zadith, imbibition, Rubification, Solar water, Oil of the Philosophers, Red Tincture, Alchemical timing</keywords>

--- Page 120 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "Third Operation" regarding the reddening (rubification) of the Stone, which began on page 97. It moves into the final stage of "Projection" and concludes with a philosophical summary based on the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus.</meta>

<page-num>98</page-num>
<header>P R O J E C T I O N.</header>

The more you dissolve and congeal, the more its power will multiply unto infinity. But note, that the Medicine multiplies more slowly through solution <note>The process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state</note> than through Fermentation. Therefore, the dissolved thing does not work well unless it first fixes itself in your Ferment. Nevertheless, the multiplication of the dissolved Medicine is more abundant than the Fermented, because there is a greater refinement. Furthermore, I advise you that in the multiplication you place one part of the work upon four of the other, and in a short time it will become powder, according to the Ferment.

### EPILOGUE ACCORDING TO HERMES.

Thus you shall separate the earth from the fire, the gross from the subtle, gently and with great spirit—that is to say, you shall separate the parts joined in the furnace by the dissolution and the separation of the parts, such as the earth from the fire, the subtle from the thick, etc. Namely, the purest substance of the Stone, until it remains clean for you, without any blemish or impurity. And when he says,
> It rises from the earth to Heaven, and then returns once more to the earth,
this must be understood as the sublimation <note>The process where a substance is heated into vapor and then condensed back into a solid</note> of the bodies. Furthermore, to properly explain distillation, he says,
> The wind carries it in its belly,
Namely, when the water distills through the Alembic <term>Alembic: A specialized apparatus used in distilling, consisting of two vessels connected by a tube</term>, where it first rises by the smoky and vaporous wind, and afterwards returns to the bottom of the vessel as water again. Wishing also to show the congealing of the matter, he says,
> Its power is complete if it returns to the earth,
that is to say, if it is converted by decoction <term>Decoction: A method of extraction by boiling herbal or chemical materials to dissolve their chemicals</term>. And to generally demonstrate all the things mentioned above, he says,
> And it shall receive the power of the inferior and superior,
that is to say, of the Elements; for if the Medicine receives the power of the light parts—namely, of the air and fire—it will also receive the heavier and weightier parts, changing into water and earth, and this is so that the matters thus perpetually joined together may have permanence, endurance, firmness, and stability. Praise be to GOD.

->T H E   E N D.<-

# TREATISE ON MERCURY
## AND
## THE PHILOSOPHERS' STONE
## BY GEORGE RIPLEY.
<note>George Ripley (c. 1415–1490) was an influential English canon and alchemist. This treatise focuses on the "First Matter" required for the alchemical transformation.</note>

<margin>1.</margin>
MY very dear son, I shall instruct you in this blessed science which has been hidden by the ancient Philosophers, upon whom God has deigned to grant so great a favor in consideration of their good works, and by virtue of their prayers; and in whose name, after having addressed our own to Him many times, we shall begin to reveal to you such an important secret. 

<margin>2.</margin>
Understand then that our matter is the first Being <note>The "First Being" or *Prima Materia* is the primitive, formless base of all matter in alchemical theory</note> of all things that are on earth, and that it is esteemed at a lowly price and of very little consequence, as you shall know more clearly in what follows; for if the water is incorporated with the earth, it will be the least esteemed of all the other things we see, but if it is fixed with fire, it will rise to the supreme degree of Nature; and by this means you may know the manner by which water becomes the most significant and the most lowly of all created Beings; especially since it is a very constant maxim, as we have said above, that it is of very lowly price, because our earth contains within it the said water, and that in this dirty and stinking earth, you will find a pure and clear water, which is our Seed <note>original: "Sperme"; here referring to the creative principle or "mercury" of the metals</note> and our Quintessence <term>Quintessence: Literally the "fifth essence," the purest and most concentrated medicinal or spiritual core of a substance</term>, which dirty and stinking earth at that time can be of no use and is worth nothing at all. 

<margin>3.</margin>
As for what I have said, that water was the first of Beings, we can prove it in various ways. Understand, my son, that without water we can make no bread nor any other thing whatsoever that God has created in Nature. From this you will easily understand that water is the primary matter of all things that are born or engendered in the world. And you will know for certain that nothing grows or increases without the help of the four Elements. And consequently all that is elemental must have been made by the virtue of the four Elements, in the same manner that the origin of all things born or growing is done by means of water. However, do not believe that this refers to common water, but to that water which is the matter of all natural things, and from which each of them is produced according to its kind, and consequently understand that air is first engendered from water, fire from air, and earth from fire. 

<margin>4.</margin>
Now to speak to you more familiarly and as a friend, and going even further, I shall declare to you little by little this magistery <term>Magistery: The master-operation or secret process of the alchemists to produce the Stone</term> of the Sages, lest by our haste there should happen to us...

<summary>This page introduces George Ripley's treatise, identifying "Philosophical Water" found within a lowly, "stinking" earth as the essential First Matter of the alchemical work. It explains the interconnectedness of the four elements, positing water as the foundational substance for all natural generation.</summary>

<keywords>George Ripley, First Matter, Philosophical Water, Quintessence, Four Elements, Magistery, Alchemy, Prima Materia</keywords>

--- Page 122 ---
<meta>This page concludes a general discourse on alchemical processes, specifically the stage of "Projection"—the final transformation of metals. It provides an "Epilogue" that interprets the famous "Emerald Tablet" of Hermes Trismegistus, explaining its cryptic metaphors through practical laboratory operations like distillation and sublimation. This serves as the conclusion to the current volume or section before the treatise by George Ripley begins on the following page.</meta>

<page-num>98:</page-num>

# P R O J E C T I O N .
<note>Projection is the final step in the alchemical "Great Work," where the finished Philosopher's Stone (the Medicine) is "cast" onto base metals to transform them into gold or silver.</note>

The more you dissolve and coagulate, the more its power will multiply unto infinity. But note, that the Medicine multiplies more slowly through solution <note>dissolving a solid into a liquid state</note> than through Fermentation. Therefore, the dissolved thing does not work well if it does not first fix itself in your Ferment <note>A "ferment" is a substance, usually pure gold or silver, added to the alchemical medicine to give it the "nature" of that metal</note>. Nevertheless, the multiplication of the dissolved Medicine is more abundant than the Fermented one, because there is a greater degree of subtilization <note>refining a substance into its most pure, vaporous, or "subtle" form</note>. Furthermore, I advise you that in the multiplication, you should put one part of the work upon four of the other, and in a short time, it will become a powder, according to the Ferment.

## EPILOGUE ACCORDING TO HERMES.
<note>Hermes Trismegistus is the legendary founder of alchemy. These lines are an interpretation of his "Emerald Tablet," the most famous foundational text of the hermetic tradition.</note>

Thus you shall separate the earth from the fire, the gross from the subtle, gently and with great spirit; that is to say, you shall separate the parts joined in the furnace by the dissolution and the separation of the parts, such as the earth from the fire, the subtle from the thick, and so on. Namely, the purest substance of the Stone, until it remains clean for you, without any blemish or filth. And when it says: *It rises from the earth to the Heaven, and then returns once more to the earth* <note>original: "Elle monte de la terre au Ciel, & puis une autre fois retourne en terre"</note>, one must understand this as the sublimation of the bodies. Further, to properly explain distillation, it says: *The wind carries it in its belly* <note>original: "Que le vent le porte dans son ventre"</note>, namely when the water distills through the Alembic <note>A chemical apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube, used for distilling volatile substances</note>, where it <note>strikethrough: rises</note> rises first by the smoky and vaporous wind, and afterward returns to the bottom of the vessel again as water. Wishing further to show the congelation <note>solidification</note> of the matter, it says: *Its strength is entire if it returns to earth*, that is to say, if it is converted by decoction <note>prolonged heating or boiling</note>. And to generally demonstrate all the things mentioned above, it says: *And it shall receive the inferior and superior strength*, that is to say, of the Elements; for if the Medicine receives the strength of the light parts—namely of the air and fire—it shall also receive the heavier and weightier parts, which change into water and earth. This is so that the matters thus perpetually conjoined may have permanence, endurance, firmness, and stability.

Praise be to G O D . .

# TREATISE ON MERCURY
### A N D
## ON THE PHILOSOPHER’S STONE
### BY GEORGE RIPLEY.
<note>George Ripley (c. 1415–1490) was a famous English Augustinian canon and alchemist whose works were widely translated into French and Latin during the Renaissance.</note>

<margin>1.</margin>My very dear son, I shall instruct you in this blessed science which has been hidden by the ancient Philosophers, to whom God has deigned to grant such a great favor in consideration of their good works, and by virtue of their prayers; and in whose name, after having addressed ours to Him several times, we are going to begin to reveal such an important secret to you. <margin>2.</margin>Understand then that our matter is the first Being of all things that are on earth, and that it is estimated to be of base price and of very little consequence, as you will know more clearly in what follows; for if water incorporates with earth, it will be the least esteemed of all other things we see, but if it is fixed with fire, it will rise to the supreme degree of Nature; and by this means you may know the manner by which water becomes the most considerable and the basest of all created Beings; insofar as it is a very constant maxim, as we have said above, that it is of very base price, because our earth contains within it the said water, and that in this <note>The "dirty and stinking earth" refers to the *Prima Materia*, the raw, unrefined substance that alchemists believed was ignored by the common people despite containing the "seed" of the Stone.</note>dirty and stinking earth, you will find a pure and clear water, which is our <term>Sperm</term><note>In alchemy, "sperm" refers to the generative "seed" or life-force within matter, rather than the biological fluid.</note> and our <term>Quintessence</term><note>The "fifth essence," the purest and most potent spiritual core of a substance.</note>, which dirty and stinking earth at that time can be of no use and is worth nothing at all. <margin>3.</margin>As for what I said, that water was the first of Beings, we can prove it in various ways. Understand, my son, that without water we can make no bread nor any other thing whatsoever that God has created in Nature. From this you will easily understand that water is the first matter of all things that are born or gendered in the world. And you will certainly know that nothing grows or increases without the help of the four Elements. And consequently everything that is elemental must have been made by the virtue of the four Elements, in the same manner as the origin of all things born or growing is made by means of water. However, do not believe that this refers to common water, but to that water which is the matter of all natural things, and from which each of them is produced in its kind, and consequently understand that air is first gendered from water, fire from air, and earth from fire. <margin>4.</margin>Now to speak to you more familiarly and as a friend, and going even further, I shall declare to you little by little this <term>magistery</term><note>The "Magistery" is the completed alchemical work or the process required to achieve it.</note> of the Sages, for fear that by our haste it might happen to us

<summary>This page introduces George Ripley’s treatise, identifying "Philosophical Water" as the fundamental "First Being" of all creation. It emphasizes that the secret matter of the Stone is a lowly, "stinking" substance that contains a hidden, pure quintessence.</summary>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page continues George Ripley’s treatise on the composition of the Philosopher’s Stone. The previous page established the concept of "Philosophical Water" and its role as the first principle of the work. This page defines the "Mercury of the Philosophers" as a union of opposites and begins the practical instructions for the laboratory work.</meta>

<page-num>102</page-num>

...will remain a virgin, and shall be called virgin’s milk, holy water, water of life <note>original: *eau de vie*. In alchemy, this refers to a purified, "living" solvent rather than common brandy.</note>, and by many other names besides.

<margin>10.</margin>
Now, my son, to tell you something of the Mercury of the Philosophers, learn that when you have combined your water of life with the red man (who is our Magnesia <term>Magnesia: not the modern mineral, but a symbolic term for the raw, metallic "mother" of the stone</term>) and with the white woman, whom we call Albific <term>Albifique: the "whitening" principle or agent</term>, and when they are all joined together so that they form but one and the same body, it is surely then that you possess the Mercury of the Philosophers. For after it is joined in this manner with the male and the female, this water is named not only the Mercury of the Philosophers, but their water of life, the blood of the red man, his flesh, his body, and his bones.

<margin>11.</margin>
Understand, therefore, that there are several kinds of milk: namely, virgin’s milk, woman’s milk, and also man’s milk; as soon as these are allied together and the woman has become pregnant through conception, it is then that this child must be nourished with milk. Thus it is easy to understand that this milk is not a virgin’s milk, but rather the milk of the man and the woman, with which the child must always be nourished until he has become more robust, at which time he must be given a stronger and more ample nourishment. This nourishment I mean is the fermentation that gives him his form, by virtue of which he may hope for a manly work <note>The "manly work" refers to the *Opus Virile*, the stage where the Stone becomes powerful enough to transmute metals.</note>. For until the child—that is to say, our Stone—has completely received its form, and has been fermented with its own kind—which is understood as the white blood of the Green Dragon and the red blood of the Red Dragon—whether the Stone be white or whether it be red, it can never perform a perfect work. Understand then, my son, that the first water is this water and this milk that God formed from Nature, and that it is truly the cause of generation, as we noted above. Then, after the conjunction that occurs from this marriage, they engender the water of life and the milk of the Philosophers, with which you shall increase and nourish your Stone without ceasing.

<margin>12.</margin>
I could tell you much more about this first matter, but what I have told you is sufficient to avoid all sorts of obscurity in my words; let us come at last, with God’s help, to the practice of this Philosopher’s Stone. Take note then, my son, to place precisely these three matters (which are nonetheless but one and the same thing) into a glass vessel, and let them gently putrefy there. Finally, place the alembic <term>alembic: the upper part of a still, used to collect vapors</term> upon your vessel, and draw from it all the water you can distill. This distillation you shall perform in a Bain-Marie <term>Bain-Marie: a water bath used to provide gentle, consistent heat</term>, then placing your vessel upon a fire of ashes <note>A "fire of ashes" or ash-bath provides a more intense but still regulated heat than water.</note>, maintaining a slow fire for twelve hours; then remove your matter from the vessel and grind it alone, without mixing in the water you have drawn from it by distillation. Then, once it is well ground, put it back into the vessel, pouring the distilled water over it, and after having sealed it well, place it in the bath for three days, then distill it in the same [manner]...

water bath, as you have done previously, then it shall become much blacker than before. This you shall repeat three times, and not grinding it further afterward, you shall always pour over it the water that you distill from it; and at each distillation, you shall apply a suitable fire for six hours or more, until it becomes moderately dry. Then put your water in once again, and dissolve it once more in the water bath with the blind head <term>chappe aveugle: a still-head without a spout, used to force vapors to condense and fall back into the vessel, a process called reflux</term>. And in all the distillations that you perform, you must separate the phlegm <term>flegme: the watery, inactive part of a substance</term> from it—that is to say, rejecting at the beginning the first six or seven drops of water from each distillation. Observing this order, you shall manage it so that it drinks seven times as much of its own water as it weighed at the beginning. Then it shall be of a white color, and all the whiter the more it has drunk of its own water. And this is what is called the White Elixir.

<margin>13</margin>
Besides the fact that our water is called "Homogeneous," and by several other names, note also that this water and this first matter engender both the Red Stone and the White. Learn also that when this first matter is pushed to a perfect whiteness, then the end of the one is the beginning of the other—that is to say, of the Red Stone, which is our Red Magnesia <term>Magnesia: a term for the "maternal" raw material of the Stone, not the modern element magnesium</term> and the virgin copper, as we noted at the start. See to it, my son, that you well understand the meaning of these words. Our virgin copper is our gold; I do not say, however, that all copper is gold. Likewise, our copper is our sulfur-vive <note>original: *soufre-vif*. "Living" or "native" sulfur, believed to be the active male principle in metals.</note>; but not all sulfur-vive is ours. Similarly, quicksilver is our Mercury; yet I do not say that common quicksilver is our Mercury. But as I said above, this water of life—which is our Sperm and our first matter—is our Mercury and our spirit of life, which is drawn from this blessed land of Ethiopia <note>original: *benite terre d’Ethiopie*. A traditional alchemical metaphor for the black state (nigredo) of the matter, from which the "light" of the Stone emerges.</note>, which is called Magnesia, and to which many other names are also given.

<margin>14</margin>
Furthermore, note my son, that there is no perfect generation without corruption <note>In alchemy, "corruption" refers to the breaking down or "death" of a substance so it can be reborn in a purified state.</note>, for corruption causes purity, and purity causes generation. Consider then that our tinging venom <term>venin teingeant: the potent, transformative essence that "colors" or transmutes metals</term> gives tincture and likewise receives it without ceasing; and this is what we call our body, our soul, and our spirit; and when they are joined and united together, they become but one and the same thing, outside of which there is and never shall be anything. This is why we hold that he should not pass entirely for a wise man who believes that another Medicine can be found that is transmutable into the Sun or the Moon <note>original: *Sol ou Lune*. These are the standard alchemical symbols for Gold and Silver.</note>. This Medicine, in truth, will not be of great use to you if it is not mixed with the body, for then it will perfect its work according to the form to which it was born; for it never received being except to become corporeal. Moreover, know that there is as much difference between the first matter (which is called the Sperm of metals) and the Medicine, as there is between the said Medicine and gold. For the Sperm does not
<margin>15</margin>

<summary>This page details the repetitive distillation process (reflux) required to create the White Elixir and introduces the concept that the "corruption" of the matter is essential for its "generation" into the Red Stone. It emphasizes that alchemical copper, sulfur, and mercury are philosophical principles rather than common materials.</summary>

<keywords>White Elixir, Red Stone, Magnesia, distillation, virgin copper, sulfur-vive, quicksilver, Ethiopia, corruption and generation, Sun and Moon, Sperm of metals</keywords>

--- Page 126 ---
<meta>This page continues the philosophical explanation of the alchemical "Work," moving from the creation of the White Elixir (on the previous page) to the relationship between the Medicine, the Elixir, and the "Stone." It introduces the foundational alchemical concept that corruption must precede generation, using the Fall of Man as a spiritual parallel.</meta>

<page-num>104</page-num>

[the alchemist] will never make the Medicine <term>Medecine: in alchemy, this is not a drug for illness, but the perfected substance used to "heal" or transmute base metals into gold</term> without a body, nor will the Medicine ever become a metal without taking on a body. There is still a great deal of difference between the Elixir and the Medicine, just as there is between the Seed <note>original: "Sperme"</note> of a man and a woman and the child who is conceived by them in the womb. You see now that the Seed is one thing and the child another, even though they both come from the same root and the same genus, and they are a single thing, a single operation, and finally a single vessel, even though different names are given to it. It is true that the child is born of the man and the woman, even though the man is one thing and the woman another, despite them both being of the same species. This is also what you must understand regarding the composition of our Stone.

<margin>16</margin>
As for what I said above—that corruption is the cause of generation—it is a truth; for you must know that every thing in its "first matter" <term>premiere matiere: the raw, chaotic starting material of the universe or an alchemical experiment</term> is corrupt and bitter. This bitterness and corruption is called a tinging venom <term>venin teingeant: a potent, poisonous "dye" or essence that has the power to radically change the nature of other substances</term>, which nonetheless gives life to all living things. You will know this clearly if you examine the natures of things with right reason. And reflect seriously, my son: when Lucifer, that angel of pride, first revolted against God and transgressed the commandment of the Most High, that blow became wretched, very hard, and bitter for him. It was no different than the fall and infidelity of our first parents, Adam and Eve, which was soon punished by the death that followed, causing them an unbearable corruption and bitterness, as well as for us, to whom this same corruption has extended. I could provide you with many other similar examples if needed. But leaving them in silence to speak of what is more to our purpose: notice carefully that of all the precious fruits born of the earth, their first matter is bitter and harsh, such that it still retains some mark of that first corruption and preceding rot; nevertheless, through the continuous action of a gentle and natural heat, this bitterness is converted into an extreme sweetness. Now, my son, if you have understanding, what I have just told you in few words should suffice for you to penetrate deeper into the hidden mysteries of Nature and conceive my thought. Remember well, then, that according to the ancient proverb: he who has not tasted bitter things has not earned the right to taste those that are sweet and pleasant.

<margin>17</margin>
To tell you now something more concerning our Brass <note>original: "Airain," referring to a bronze or copper-based alloy</note>, know that the word "Airain" signifies a stable thing, or "permanent water." And what must further be considered in the nature of our Brass are the four mysterious letters of its name, which is called B.R.A.S. This word is properly English, and signifies in our language "Airain" <note>The author is pointing out that the English word "Brass" serves as a four-letter acronym for his secret process</note>. In the first place, the letter **B** signifies the first body of our work, which is our sweet and bitter olive, and our brass...

<summary>This page explains that alchemical generation requires "corruption" or "putrefaction," drawing a parallel between the bitterness of the Fall of Man and the harsh "first matter" that eventually produces the "sweetness" of the Stone. It also begins a linguistic breakdown of the word "Brass" (B.R.A.S.) as a coded technical term.</summary>
<keywords>Medicine, Elixir, Stone, corruption, generation, tinging venom, Lucifer, Adam and Eve, first matter, Brass, Airain, B.R.A.S.</keywords>

--- Page 127 ---
<meta>This page continues the symbolic interpretation of the alchemical "Work." Having previously discussed the "first matter" and the role of corruption, the author now uses a four-letter name (likely "ORAS" or "ARAS") to explain the fundamental components of the Stone: the root, the masculine and feminine principles (Adam and Eve), and the life-giving spirit (Quintessence).</meta>

<page-num>105</page-num>

...permanent in its form. Secondly, the **R** marks the **root** of our work and the source of the **radical moisture** <term>humeur radicale: in alchemy and early medicine, the essential life-preserving fluid found in all things</term> which is permanent; this is our **tincture** <term>teinture: the potent essence capable of transforming the color and nature of a metal</term> and our **red rose**, which putrefies and purifies all things to give them being according to their kind. Next, the **A** gives you to know our father **Adam**, the first of men, from whom was born **Eve**, the first of women. From this, you may learn that in our **magistery** <term>magistere: the mastery or the completed alchemical process</term>, there is likewise a male and a female. Note then that our **brass** <note>original: "airain"</note> is the beginning of our work, our gold and our olive, insofar as it is the first matter of metals, just as man is the origin of man and woman. Finally, the **S** signifies the **soul** of our life—I mean that spirit of life that God breathed into Adam and into all other creatures, which is called the **Quintessence** <term>Quintessence: the "fifth element," a pure, celestial essence that transcends the four earthly elements</term>.

<margin>18</margin> Furthermore, I tell you, my son, that by these four letters we understand the **four Elements**, without which nothing is engendered in Nature. These letters also signify the **Sun** and the **Moon**, which are the cause of all living things, of their seed and their growth. And therefore, in this name composed of four letters consists our entire operation, because in our brass is found the male and the female, from whom is born he who is called the **Begotten**. Note well then, my son, what is signified by our "sweet brass," which is called our **Sandiver** <note>original: "Sandiver" or "Suint de verre," a byproduct of glassmaking used here as a code name</note>, or our **Salt-nitre**, which is also named **Dragon's blood**, **Sol** and **Luna**; finally our **Mercury** and our **water of life**, and various other names of which the Philosophers have spoken obscurely and in riddles. <margin>19</margin> You must therefore know that our **first matter** is neither common gold nor silver, nor is it of a corrosive nature, nor any of the other foreign things used today by those who are led astray, and who seem to walk only by groping in the darkness. Finally, take care not to use anything of a different kind, because you must be certain that one can only reap what one has sown. Moreover, understand that when our **Stone** is perfect and completed in its own kind, it will then be a hard stone that does not dissolve easily. However, if you join its female to it, it will turn into oil, which is called the **oil of the Philosophers**, the **incombustible oil**, and many other names.

<margin>20</margin> Know also that there are various kinds of **fermentations**, some corporeal, others spiritual. The corporeal concern quantity, and the spiritual concern quality. Corporeal fermentation increases the weight and the quantity of the **Medicine**; however, it does not have as much strength as the Medicine itself, or as spiritual fermentation, for it increases the Medicine only in quantity and not in virtue <note>virtue: meaning its inherent power or efficacy</note>; but spiritual fermentation increases it in both ways, so that where the corporeal has power over a hundred, the spiritual has power over a thousand. Besides, every time it is fermented by [qualities]...

<meta>catchword: qua- (referring to "qualitez" or "qualities")</meta>

<summary>This page decodes the symbolic letters of the alchemical "Work," identifying them with Adam and Eve (male and female principles) and the four elements. It also warns against using common materials, distinguishing between "corporeal" and "spiritual" fermentations that increase the potency of the Medicine.</summary>
<keywords>Adam and Eve, Radical Moisture, Quintessence, First Matter, Sandiver, Salt-nitre, Dragon's Blood, Incombustible Oil, Corporeal Fermentation, Spiritual Fermentation</keywords>

--- Page 128 ---
<meta>This page concludes the discourse on the alchemical "Work," transitioning from the spiritual qualities of the initial "Medicine" to the tangible "Elixir." The author provides final instructions on the feeding of the Stone and the cyclical nature of the elements.</meta>

<page-num>106</page-num>

...spiritual qualities, it always retains the same name of **Medicine** <term>Medecine: in alchemy, a substance that "heals" base metals by transforming them into gold</term>; but when it is fermented with a bodily substance, it is called **Elixir**. There are thus various ways of fermenting, just as there is a difference between the Medicine and the Elixir; for the one is spiritual, and the other is corporeal. Learn also that while the ferment remains spiritual, it will always be in the form of gum and liquid oil, which cannot easily be transported from one place to another: but when your **Stone** is in powder, it will be a Stone that you can carry everywhere in your purse. Consequently, you now see the difference that exists between the Medicine and the Elixir. There is no less difference between the Elixir, gold, and silver; especially since gold and silver are difficult to melt <note>literally "of difficult fusion"</note>, unlike the Elixir, which melts at the same time and easily by the flame of a candle. From this you will know only too well how much difference there is between our composition and its temperament.

<margin>21</margin>
Finally, to say something of the drinking and eating that serves as their nourishment, understand that their meat <note>original: "viande," used here in the archaic sense of solid food or sustenance</note> is taken from the aerial Stones, and that their beverage is drawn from two perfect bodies, which are the **Sun** and the **Moon** <note>In alchemical symbolism, the Sun represents Gold and the Moon represents Silver</note>. The drink drawn from the Sun is called **Potable Gold**; that which is drawn from the Moon is called the **Virgin’s Milk**.

Now, my son, we have spoken clearly enough to you, provided divine grace does not fail you; for the drink drawn from the Sun is red, and that drawn from the Moon is white, and therefore one is called Potable Gold and the other Virgin’s Milk; the one is also male and the other is female, although both take their origin from the same source and the same genus. 

<margin>22</margin>
Think then on the words I have just told you, otherwise if you wander into the darkness, harm will inevitably come to you for lack of understanding. See to it that you are diligent in the circulation of the **Philosophical Wheel** <term>roue Philosophique: the repeated cycle of evaporation and condensation intended to purify the substance</term>—that is to say, so that you know how to draw water from the earth, air from water, fire from air, and earth from fire—and that all these things finally be extracted from the same stem and root, which is to say, from their own kind; and that you nourish them with their own meat and natural food, by which their life may be sustained without end. Whoever has understanding, therefore, let him understand what I have said, as I am not permitted to say more; and you, my son, if you have well heard and understood what I have presented above, I do not doubt that you will carefully hide secrets so great and so significant.

->F I N I S.<-

<summary>This concluding page distinguishes between the spiritual 'Medicine' and the physical 'Elixir,' detailing the preparation and 'feeding' of the Philosopher's Stone using solar and lunar essences. It ends with a warning to keep these powerful secrets hidden and a final exhortation to master the elemental cycles of the Philosophical Wheel.</summary>

<keywords>Medicine, Elixir, Potable Gold, Virgin’s Milk, Philosophical Wheel, Sun and Moon, Alchemical Fermentation, Elements</keywords>
